# Spec — Dutch Oven Daddy iOS App v1

**Status:** Draft, Phase 1 — Specify
**Governed by:** [`../constitution.md`](../constitution.md)

## Vision

A native iPhone and iPad reader for the Dutch Oven Daddy cooking blog. Readers can discover recipes from the home feed, browse by category, search, and save recipes so they're available while cooking — even with no internet in the kitchen.

The app is **mostly read-only** — every recipe is a tap away without sign-in — with two write surfaces in v1.0: posting a rating (US-13) and posting a comment (US-14) on a recipe, both via the dutchovendaddy.com WordPress REST API under a Keychain-stored guest identity (US-15). No accounts. Recipes are the hero; UI chrome stays out of the way.

## Personas

- **Weekend Cook (primary)** — opens the app on a Saturday morning looking for "what should I make today." Browses, picks one, cooks from it.
- **Returning Reader** — has a few favorite recipes saved, wants to re-cook them, sometimes offline.
- **Recipe Hunter** — has a specific ingredient or dish in mind and searches.

## v1 scope (in)

1. Browse latest recipes (home feed)
2. Browse by category
3. Search recipes
4. View recipe detail (hero, ingredients with check, steps, video, related recipes)
5. Save recipes for offline reading
6. Share a recipe via native share sheet

## v1 scope (out — deferred to v2)

- User accounts and sign-in *(remains out; the round-6 OAuth framing is superseded by US-41 / CL-86 — there is **no** account in our app, the user's iCloud account is the sync identity per CloudKit private DB; see CL-87 for the OAuth-to-CloudKit replacement trail)*
- ~~Cross-device sync (iCloud) of saved recipes~~ *(Graduated 2026-05-28 as US-41 / CL-86..CL-99 / T-700..T-708 — CloudKit private-DB sync replaces Spencer's round-6 OAuth framing per CL-86 + CL-87. Opt-in via Settings → iCloud Sync per AC-41.3; app works without iCloud sign-in per AC-41.1.)*
- Push notifications for new recipes
- Meal planning / weekly calendar
- ~~Shopping list~~ *(Graduated 2026-05-27 as US-39 — see "Recently graduated" in backlog.md.)*
- ~~Cooking mode (screen-awake, in-app timers)~~ *(Graduated 2026-05-23 as US-7 / CL-16 — see backlog.md.)*
- watchOS, macOS, visionOS targets

---

## User stories

### US-1 — Browse the home feed
**As a** Weekend Cook,
**I want** to open the app and immediately see the most recent recipes from the blog,
**so that** I can quickly find inspiration for what to cook.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-1.1** Given a first-time launch with internet, when the app opens, then the home feed shows the 20 most recent published recipes from dutchovendaddy.com, ordered newest-first.
- **AC-1.2** Given the home feed, when the user scrolls to the bottom, then the next 20 recipes load (infinite scroll), with a visible loading indicator while fetching.
- **AC-1.3** Given the home feed, each row shows: hero image, recipe title, short description (excerpt), and total time. No author byline.
- **AC-1.4** Given the home feed, when the user pulls to refresh, then the feed refetches and shows newly published recipes at the top.
- **AC-1.5** Given the home feed with no internet on first launch, when the app opens, then an empty state explains "You need internet to load recipes the first time" with a Retry button.
- **AC-1.6** Given the home feed has been loaded before, when the app opens offline, then the last cached feed is shown with a non-blocking banner: "Offline — showing recent recipes."
- **AC-1.7** ~~Given any list (home, category, search, related), posts whose recipe-detail fetch + JSON-LD parse has previously failed are filtered out and not rendered. The filter resets on pull-to-refresh so newly-fixed posts can return. (See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-9.)~~ *Amended by CL-63 / T-640 (2026-05-27, US-37): posts whose recipe-detail fetch + JSON-LD parse fails are no longer filtered out — they are routed to article rendering instead per US-37 / AC-37.2. The blocklist plumbing (`CachedRecipe.jsonLDFailedAt`, `RecipeStore.markJSONLDFailed(_:)`, `RecipeStore.clearBlocklist()`, list-query `jsonLDFailedAt == nil` predicates) stays in place as the article-classification signal: a non-nil `jsonLDFailedAt` row now means "this post is an article, render with HTML body," not "this post is hidden." Lists render both recipes and articles in the same `RecipeListItem` row format (hero + title + excerpt + total time per AC-1.3) — the kind difference shows only on the detail screen, not in the list cell. The pull-to-refresh reset behavior is preserved (newly-fixed posts can return to recipe rendering on the next detail open).* Original wording: posts with parse failures were filtered out and not rendered; see CL-9 + CL-63 for the lineage.

### US-2 — Browse by category
**As a** Recipe Hunter,
**I want** to see recipes grouped by category (e.g. Beef, Chicken, Desserts),
**so that** I can narrow down by the kind of meal I'm planning.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-2.1** Given the app, when the user opens the Categories tab, then a list of all WordPress categories from the blog is shown, alphabetically.
- **AC-2.2** Given the categories list, each category shows its name and the count of recipes in it.
- **AC-2.3** Given the user taps a category, when the category screen loads, then it shows only recipes in that category, in the same row format as the home feed, with infinite scroll.
- **AC-2.4** Categories with zero recipes are hidden.
- **AC-2.5** Empty categories or fetch failure: a clear empty state with a Retry button, no spinner stuck on screen.

### US-3 — Search recipes
**As a** Recipe Hunter,
**I want** to search recipes by keyword,
**so that** I can find a specific dish or use up an ingredient.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-3.1** Given the Search tab, when the user types ≥ 2 characters, then results update with a 300ms debounce.
- **AC-3.2** Search matches against recipe title ~~and excerpt~~. Ingredient-body matching is **not required in v1** (call out as known gap in the empty state if results are sparse). *(Amended in T-642 / CL-120: the "and excerpt" half is **removed** — body-only matches (whether WP returned them for an excerpt hit, a tag hit, or its own fuzzy ranker) are filtered out client-side because they surfaced false positives like "Cast Iron Bacon Wrapped Pickles" for `?search=nachos`. Title-match is the user's contract; the matcher is tolerant — plural/singular swap and Levenshtein-1 typos still find the recipe. See CL-120 / REG-29 / AC-12.1 amendment.)* *(Amended in T-643 / CL-121: title-match is **joined by category-name match** — when the typed query also names a known WP category (e.g. "Dessert Recipes"), the pipeline additionally surfaces every post in that category via a parallel `?categories=` fetch. The title-precision contract is preserved for queries that don't name a category. See CL-121 / REG-30 / AC-12.1 amendment for the merge rule.)*
- **AC-3.3** Results show in the same row format as the home feed. Tapping a result opens recipe detail.
- **AC-3.4** Given a query with zero results, then an empty state shows: "No recipes match '<query>'. Try a different word."
- **AC-3.5** The search field has a Clear button when non-empty. *(Amended in T-648 / CL-126: the search field is the shared `DODSearchField` component (`DODDesignSystem.Components.DODSearchField`) — `DODColor.surfaceElevated` (brand brown) background filled into a `Capsule(style: .continuous)` shape, magnifyingglass leading glyph, `TextField` body, and the conditional `xmark.circle.fill` clear button when text is non-empty. The Search-tab call site supplies `onClear: { viewModel.clear() }` so the clear button preserves the full VM-side cleanup (state / items / lastQuery / debounce-cancel) instead of just clearing the query string. See CL-126 / REG-32 for the cross-tab visual contract.)*
- **AC-3.6** Search queries are tracked via TelemetryDeck **with the query string hashed**, never raw (per constitution §9).
- **AC-3.7** Offline: search shows "Search needs internet" with a Retry on connectivity restore. Searching saved recipes locally is **out of scope for v1**.

### US-4 — View recipe detail
**As a** Weekend Cook,
**I want** to see everything I need to make a recipe on one screen,
**so that** I can cook without hunting around.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-4.1** Recipe detail shows, from top: hero image, title, short description, meta row (prep time, cook time, total time, ~~servings~~). *Amended by CL-129 / T-732 (2026-05-31): the meta-row `servings` mini-chip is removed — the `RecipeServingsScaler` row immediately below the meta pills (AC-31.1's "Serves N" stepper) is the single Serves indicator on the recipe detail screen. The duplicative mini-chip was visual noise above the canonical stepper. `prep time` / `cook time` / `total time` are unchanged in the meta-row contract (the current production surface only renders the `totalTime` clock chip — `prep` / `cook` per-step rendering is a future surface; this amendment is bounded to the `servings` removal).* *Amended by CL-185 (T-789, 2026-06-20, DUT-96): a published-date caption now renders below the meta row — the shared `PublishedDateCaption` ("Published June 1, 2026", `DateFormatter.dateStyle = .long`, locale-aware, sourced from `listItem.publishedAt`), the same component + format the article header uses. Recipe detail previously showed no published date.* *Amended by CL-197 (T-803, 2026-06-20, DUT-179): the published-date caption moves from below the meta row to directly below the hero — the exact inset + position `ArticleDetailView` uses (the `DODSpacing.lg`-spaced slot right under the hero, `.padding(.horizontal, DODSpacing.md)`), for parity + easy scanning across recipes and articles. CL-185 first placed it below the meta pills in a slot with no horizontal inset, which left it flush to the leading screen edge; the shared `PublishedDateCaption` component + "Published <Month D, YYYY>" format are unchanged. Render order is now hero → published date (inset) → meta row → servings stepper.* *Amended by CL-198 (T-804, 2026-06-20, DUT-180): on iPad the recipe detail now adapts to the wider canvas — the full-bleed hero stays edge-to-edge, but everything below is capped to a centered reading column (`View.readableContentColumn`, `DODContentWidth.reading` = 700pt) so prose + buttons stop stretching full-width, and in landscape (canvas width ≥ 1000pt, read via `GeometryReader` since iPad is `.regular` in both orientations) Ingredients + Instructions lay side by side in a wider band (`DODContentWidth.wide` = 1040pt) instead of leaving the horizontal space empty. iPhone (compact) is byte-identical — the column helper returns `self` unchanged, mirroring the `adaptiveListRows` (CL-178) convention. Section builders moved to `RecipeDetailView+Sections.swift` / `+Loading.swift` for the type-body cap.*
- **AC-4.12** (Added by CL-129 / T-732 (2026-05-31); amended by CL-130 / T-733 (2026-05-31); amended by CL-131 / T-734 (2026-05-31); amended by CL-132 / T-735 (2026-05-31); amended by CL-133 / T-736 (2026-05-31)) The recipe detail's "short description" surface from AC-4.1 — pre-T-732 rendered as a single `Text(viewModel.listItem.excerpt)` that surfaced the WordPress `the_excerpt()` server-side `[…]` / `[...]` truncation tail verbatim — now collapses-by-default to ~~the excerpt text with the trailing `[…]` / `[...]` truncation suffix stripped at display time~~ *(amended by CL-131 / T-734 — the collapsed surface now renders the first paragraph from `viewModel.blurbBlocks` via the same shared `ArticleBlocksView` the expanded surface uses, so paragraph 1's bold / italic / link / `.label`-color styling matches the expanded view byte-identically; the WP-excerpt-as-`Text` path remains only as the fallback when `blurbBlocks` is empty per `hasExpandableBlurb == false`, in which case the WP excerpt renders with `.dodFont(DODType.body)` + `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.label)` — not `.labelSecondary` — to match the article-rendering visual register everywhere on the page)*, a literal `"..."` tail appended at display time as a visual continuation cue *(added by CL-130 / T-733 — the "..." is view-only, NEVER comes from the WP source, NEVER stored, and NEVER rendered in the expanded state; amended by CL-131 / T-734 — the "..." is attached inline to the first paragraph block's `AttributedString` via a pure `appendingEllipsisTail(to:)` helper that preserves the paragraph's inline bold / italic / link attribute runs, then renders the mutated block through `ArticleBlocksView` so the tail sits at the end of the prose flow in the same typographic register)*, and an inline **"More"** affordance (`DODType.caption`, `DODColor.accent`) appended; tapping "More" reveals the recipe's ~~full~~ *(amended by CL-130 / T-733 — first 1-2 paragraphs of, not full, see below)* narrative blurb rendered as native rich blocks (paragraphs, headings, photos, lists) via the shared `ArticleBlocksView` helper (same per-block styling `ArticleDetailView` uses for articles), followed by a **"Less"** affordance with identical caption + accent styling at the bottom of the expanded blocks; tapping "Less" collapses back to the first-paragraph-via-`ArticleBlocksView` + `"..."` + "More". The rich blocks are parsed from the recipe page's HTML body via `ArticleBodyExtractor.extractRecipeBlurb(html:`*`paragraphLimit: 2`*`)` *(amended by CL-130 / T-733 — the helper now accepts a `paragraphLimit: Int = 2` parameter and caps the returned HTML at the Nth `<p>` boundary so the expanded blurb is bounded to 1-2 additional paragraphs of intro, NOT every block before the recipe card; pre-CL-130, long-form blog-style recipes dumped 30-40 paragraphs of intro prose into the expansion. Live-API sampling on 2026-05-31 confirmed 40 and 30 non-empty `<p>` blocks on recipes 21238 and 23034 — the cap shrinks both to 2 paragraphs + any heading/image between them. Headings / images / lists that sit BEFORE the Nth `<p>` boundary are preserved; content after is dropped)* — it crops at the WPRM `<div class="wprm-recipe-container">` boundary so the ingredient + instructions structured content stays in its canonical AC-4.2 / AC-4.3 sections and is not duplicated in the blurb — and then `ArticleHTMLParser.parse(html:)` to produce the `[ArticleBlock]` array. The More/Less affordance only renders when `viewModel.blurbBlocks` contains at least one `.paragraph` block *(amended by CL-130 / T-733 — pre-CL-130 gate was `!blurbBlocks.isEmpty` which could surface the More button on a recipe whose pre-WPRM content was only a heading or image with nothing meaningful to expand into; the broadened-but-narrower gate is a `hasExpandableBlurb` computed property that pattern-matches for a `.paragraph` case)*. The shared `ArticleBlocksView` is the single source of truth for native block rendering — `ArticleDetailView`'s rich-render branch (AC-37.3) consumes the same helper, so the paragraph / heading / image / list styling agrees byte-identically between the two surfaces. `ArticleDetailView` itself is preserved visually (`L4 ArticleDetailViewSnapshotTests` baselines unchanged) and is unaffected by the CL-130 / T-733 paragraph cap (articles render the FULL body — `extractArticleBody` / `extractContentHTML` do not take the `paragraphLimit` parameter). The expansion state is view-local (`@State`); collapsing the blurb does not persist across screen re-entries (matches the AC-4.2 ingredient check lifetime contract — view-lifetime state, not cross-launch persistence). *(Amended by CL-132 / T-735 — the collapsed and expanded blurb surfaces filter `.image` blocks out of `viewModel.blurbBlocks` before rendering through `ArticleBlocksView`, via a new `textOnlyBlurbBlocks` view-side computed property + a `static func textOnlyBlurbBlocks(from:)` testable helper. The More-button visibility gate operates on the filtered set so a blurb whose only blocks are images correctly hides the affordance — there's nothing text-y to expand into. Live-API audit on 2026-05-31 of 11 production recipes confirmed 3 recipes (`?p=274, 5016, 524`) carry image-first block sequences where the pre-T-735 collapsed surface rendered a Pinterest-preview / lazy-load-placeholder image instead of the first text paragraph. The blurb-rendering path is now text-only — a narrative intro peek, not a media gallery — per the user's stated brief; `ArticleDetailView` is NOT touched and continues to render images as part of its rich body per AC-37.3. The extractor's WPRM-boundary detection (`sliceBeforeWPRMCard`) also broadens defensively to additional class tokens — `tasty-recipes`, `mv-create`, `wp-block-tasty-recipes-recipe-card` — so future recipes (or guest posts) using Tasty Recipes / Meal Vista plugins instead of WPRM still get a clean pre-card crop. The 2026-05-31 audit found zero recipes in the sample use these alternate plugins; the broadening is anticipatory.)* *(Amended by CL-133 / T-736 — `RecipeDetailViewModel.onAppear()`'s cache-hit branch for `cached.kind == .recipe` was not populating `viewModel.blurbBlocks` (the property is view-local and only set by the fresh-fetch `fetchAndParse()` path), so every re-open of a previously-cached recipe rendered the empty-`blurbBlocks` fallback (`Text(strippedExcerpt)` with `DODColor.label` + `DODType.body`) instead of the rich `ArticleBlocksView` path — visibly missing the "..." continuation cue, the More button, and the inline bold / italic / link attribute runs from the source HTML. T-736 audit (50 newest + 50 oldest + 100 mid-catalog recipes on `dutchovendaddy.com`, 2026-05-31) confirmed extractor coverage is at 100% (50/50, 50/50, 99/100 with one transient HTTP failure) so the gap is exclusively in the view-model cache-hit path, not the extractor. The fix adds a new `refreshBlurbBlocks(forCanonicalURL:)` private helper on `RecipeDetailViewModel+Fetch` that the cache-hit branch fires-and-forgets after `loadState = .ready`: it gates on `dependencies.isOnline()` (offline cache-hits skip the network call — the fallback path is acceptable when there's no fresh data), `try?`s `dependencies.fetchHTML(for: canonicalURL)` (fail-silent on transient errors — no snackbar disturbs the already-rendered cached view), runs the same `ArticleBodyExtractor.extractRecipeBlurb` + `ArticleHTMLParser.parse` pipeline, and assigns `blurbBlocks` only when the result is non-empty (guard against transient-parse-failure downgrade). Pattern matches `loadRatingsAndComments` (same cached-first-then-refresh shape, REG-14 / AC-14.6 fail-silent convention). The view's `@Observable` binding picks up the assignment and re-renders the blurb from the fallback `Text` path to the rich `ArticleBlocksView` path with the "..." + More affordance, closing the visible-content gap. The fallback path itself (lines 124-134 of `RecipeDetailView+Blurb.swift`) is unchanged — pre-T-736 already uses `DODColor.label` (T-734 / CL-131 — not `.labelSecondary`); the dark-mode visual register was correct before T-736. The article cache-hit path (`cached.kind == .article` line 132-138) is unchanged — articles already persist `articleBodyHTML` in `Recipe` and render rich on re-open without needing the refresh. `Recipe` data model is unchanged (no new `blurbHTML` field; the refresh re-fetches HTML rather than persisting blocks — `AttributedString` is non-trivially `Codable` and the offline cache-hit fallback path is already a valid render).)* See CL-129 + CL-130 + CL-131 + CL-132 + CL-133 + REG-33 for the full contract.
- **AC-4.2** Ingredients section shows each ingredient as a tappable row with a checkbox. Tapping toggles a strikethrough state. Check state persists for the lifetime of the screen but is **not** saved across app launches in v1.
- **AC-4.3** Instructions section shows numbered steps, in order, with adequate line spacing for readability while cooking. Dynamic Type respected up to AX5.
- **AC-4.4** If the recipe has an embedded video (WPRM video field), then a video player is shown inline above the instructions. Tapping play starts playback; the player respects iOS PiP. *Amended by CL-199 (T-805, 2026-06-20, DUT-181): the inline player is now an `AVPlayerViewController` (`RecipeVideoPlayer`, `UIViewControllerRepresentable`) instead of SwiftUI's `VideoPlayer`, giving system playback controls + a fullscreen button + PiP, and its container is sized to the clip's real display aspect ratio — read from the asset (`AVURLAsset` natural size × preferred transform), capped at 440pt tall and centered — so a portrait clip renders as a tall centered player and a landscape clip fills the column width, with no black side-bars (the fixed 200pt full-width box that letterboxed portrait clips is gone). The macOS test host falls back to `VideoPlayer`. Applies on iPhone + iPad.*
- **AC-4.5** If the recipe has no video, no empty video block is shown.
- **AC-4.6** A "Related Recipes" section shows 3–4 recipes from the same primary category at the bottom. Tapping one opens its detail.
- **AC-4.7** A Save button (bookmark icon) is in the navigation bar. Tapping toggles saved state with haptic feedback. *Amended by CL-38 (T-380, 2026-05-24); supersedes the earlier "heart icon" wording, which was retained in v1 by the carve-out in AC-16.3. Original: ~~"A Save button (heart icon) is in the navigation bar."~~* *Further amended by CL-42 (T-410, 2026-05-25): the sticky bottom-trailing `RecipeDetailFloatingActions` duplicate added by T-302 is removed — the nav-bar Save is now the single in-recipe Save affordance. See US-26 / AC-26.1 / AC-26.2.*
- **AC-4.8** A Share button (native share sheet) is in the navigation bar; sharing exposes the recipe's web URL.
- **AC-4.9** Recipe detail is fully usable offline if the recipe is saved (per US-5).
- **AC-4.10** VoiceOver: hero image has a recipe-title alt, ingredient checkboxes announce checked/unchecked state, steps are reachable in order.
- **AC-4.11** Recipe detail data (ingredients, instructions, prep/cook/total times, servings, nutrition, video URL) is sourced from the JSON-LD `@type: Recipe` block on the post's rendered HTML page (per CL-1). Recipe list data (title, excerpt, hero image, link, categories) is sourced from the WP REST API. The two sources are stitched into one local `Recipe` model. ~~If JSON-LD parse fails on first detail open, the user sees no error screen — instead the post is hidden from subsequent lists per AC-1.7 and the navigation pops back with a brief snackbar "Recipe unavailable."~~ *Amended by CL-63 / T-640 (2026-05-27, US-37): a JSON-LD-less post is now classified as a `PostKind.article` and rendered via `ArticleDetailView` (HTML body sanitized + rendered, no ingredients section, no Cook Mode CTA, no servings stepper) — see US-37 / AC-37.3. The auto-pop-on-`.unavailable` path still exists as the final fallback (article-body extraction returns empty / both the JSON-LD AND the article-body extraction failed), but for the dominant case (post exists, JSON-LD missing, HTML body present) the user lands on the article detail screen instead. The recipe-vs-article kind discriminator lives on `Recipe.kind: PostKind` (or an equivalent domain projection — implementation choice per CL-63's mapping discussion).*

### US-5 — Save recipes for offline
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** to save recipes I love so I can cook them without internet,
**so that** spotty kitchen wifi doesn't break my cook.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-5.1** Tapping the Save button (bookmark) on a recipe detail marks it saved. Tapping again unsaves with a confirmation undo (snackbar). *Amended by CL-38 (T-380, 2026-05-24); supersedes the earlier "(heart)" wording. Original: ~~"Tapping the Save button (heart) on a recipe detail marks it saved."~~* *Amended by CL-158 (T-761, 2026-06-12): the success snackbar reads "Saved." (was ~~"Saved for offline."~~) — Save is a lightweight favorite (a bookmark for findability) and does **not** trigger an offline download; the offline-pin guarantee is owned by the explicit Download action (US-35 / AC-35, which also marks the recipe saved). See CL-158 for the Save/Download decoupling.*
- **AC-5.2** When a recipe is saved, the full recipe payload (text, ingredients, steps, metadata) **and** the hero image are downloaded for offline use within 5 seconds of saving on a normal connection. *Amended by CL-158 (T-761, 2026-06-12): this save-driven pre-download is superseded — tapping **Save** on recipe detail is a lightweight favorite that does **not** force an offline download. (The recipe text is already cached from the detail open; the recipe-detail Save path never actually pre-pinned the hero image — `RecipeStore.toggleSaved` only sets `isSaved` + mirrors the synced row, and `LiveSavedDependencies.preDownloadImages` is uncalled.) The full-payload + hero-image offline pin is owned by the explicit **Download** action (US-35 / AC-35.2), which also marks the recipe saved. Saving alone adds the recipe to the Saved tab for findability.*
- **AC-5.3** The Saved tab shows all saved recipes, newest-saved-first, in the same row format as the home feed. *Amended by CL-104 / T-635 (2026-05-29): unsaving a recipe via the long-press "Unsave" context menu (US-34 / AC-34.6) removes the card from the Saved-tab grid **instantly** via optimistic local removal in `SavedViewModel.optimisticallyRemove(id:)` — the user does not have to switch tabs to see the row disappear. The store toggle bubbles asynchronously through `TabStack.saveFromCard(item:store:)` in the same call; the next `.task { await viewModel.refresh() }` on tab re-appear reconciles. Unsaving the last saved recipe transitions `loadState` to `.empty` immediately, surfacing AC-5.8's empty-state view in the same frame.* *Amended by CL-171 (T-774 / DUT-80, 2026-06-14): the Saved-tab cards now show a "Downloaded" badge — a burnt-orange capsule with an `arrow.down.circle.fill` glyph, bottom-leading on the hero (the time chip stays top-trailing) — on recipes that are saved AND explicitly downloaded for offline use (`CachedRecipe.downloadedAt != nil`). `SavedViewModel` hydrates a `downloadedIDs: Set<Int>` from the new `RecipeStore.downloadedRecipeIDs()` (via `SavedDependencies.downloadedRecipeIDs()`, default `[]`) alongside the recipes on each `refresh()`, and `SavedView` passes `isDownloaded: downloadedIDs.contains(recipe.id)` to a new defaulted `RecipeCard(isDownloaded:)` param — so Feed / Categories / Search cards (which don't pass it) are unchanged. Saved-only recipes get no badge; the badge distinguishes saved-only from saved + downloaded at a glance.* *Amended by CL-172 (T-775 / DUT-81, 2026-06-14): the badge now **clears** when the user removes a download — via the recipe-detail toggle (AC-35.1; reflected on the next Saved-tab `refresh()`) or the Saved-tab "Remove Download" long-press item (AC-34.6), which clears `SavedViewModel.downloadedIDs` optimistically so the capsule disappears instantly. The card stays in the grid — un-download keeps the recipe saved.* *Amended by CL-173 (T-776 / DUT-82, 2026-06-14): the Saved tab's large "Saved" title is now rendered as an **in-content header** (a bold large `Text` at the top of the view), not via `.navigationTitle` — a workaround for an iOS 26 NavigationStack bug that drops the OS large title after a card context-menu action (Unsave / Remove Download) re-renders the view. The cart toolbar, cards, and list behavior are unchanged; the header scrolls with the content rather than collapsing into the bar.*
- **AC-5.4** Given the device is offline, when the user taps a saved recipe, then the full recipe detail opens with no network calls and all text + hero image present. *Amended by CL-158 (T-761, 2026-06-12): the guaranteed-offline contract (text **and** hero image, no network) now applies to **downloaded** recipes (US-35 / AC-35.2) and to any recipe whose hero image was already cached from a prior online view. A recipe that was only **saved** (never downloaded, never viewed online with images loaded) opens offline with its text but may show the "image unavailable offline" placeholder — Save is a lightweight favorite, Download is the offline guarantee.*
- **AC-5.5** Inline videos in saved recipes do **not** need to play offline in v1. Show a "Video unavailable offline" placeholder if offline.
- **AC-5.6** Related recipes section is hidden when offline.
- **AC-5.7** Saved recipes persist across app launches, reinstalls **not** required (SwiftData store survives normal launch; ~~reinstall wipes saves — known limitation, document in onboarding~~). *Amended by CL-86 / US-41 (2026-05-28): with CloudKit sync enabled (AC-41.3 / AC-41.4), reinstalling on the same iCloud account restores the user's saved recipes from the user's iCloud private DB after the AC-41.2 opt-in flow re-completes. The "reinstall wipes saves" limitation only applies when sync is disabled or the user is not signed into iCloud — see CL-97 for the full backward-compat + restore story.*
- **AC-5.8** The Saved tab shows an empty state when no recipes are saved: "Tap the bookmark on any recipe to save it for offline." *Amended by CL-38 (T-380, 2026-05-24). Original: ~~"Tap the heart on any recipe to save it for offline."~~* *Amended by CL-161 (T-764 / DUT-70, 2026-06-13): the message is reworded to "Tap the bookmark on any recipe to find it again later." — T-761 / CL-158 decoupled Save from the offline download (Save is a lightweight favorite now; Download is the offline action), so the "for offline" framing is dropped. The title ("No saved recipes yet", asserted by the L3 smoke + L5 journey) is unchanged.*

### US-6 — Share a recipe
**As any** reader,
**I want** to share a recipe with a friend,
**so that** I can recommend it.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-6.1** Tapping Share on recipe detail opens the native iOS share sheet.
- **AC-6.2** The shared payload is the recipe's canonical web URL on dutchovendaddy.com (not a deep link to the app in v1).
- **AC-6.3** Share action is tracked via TelemetryDeck (event: `recipe_shared`).

### US-7 — Cook Mode
**As a** Weekend Cook,
**I want** a hands-free cooking surface,
**so that** I can follow a recipe at the stove without the screen sleeping.

Added by consultant-pass amendment (CL-16, 2026-05-23). Constitution §2 was amended in the same pass to bring Cook Mode in scope for v1.0; constitution §9 documents the idle-timer toggle as a UIKit device-state change (not a new data category) and adds `cookModeStarted` to the analytics allowlist.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-7.1** Recipe detail has a prominent "Cook Now" button (visually distinct primary CTA, reachable without scrolling on iPhone 13 baseline).
- **AC-7.2** Tapping "Cook Now" opens a full-screen takeover showing the recipe title, a step counter formatted "Step N of M", the current step's instruction text in large type (Dynamic Type up to AX5 per CC-1), and a persistent ingredients drawer that the user can pull up without leaving Cook Mode.
- **AC-7.3** While Cook Mode is the foreground surface, the screen does not auto-lock. Implementation: set `UIApplication.shared.isIdleTimerDisabled = true` on entry and restore the prior value on exit. This is the only place in the app that touches `isIdleTimerDisabled`.
- **AC-7.4** Swipe-left (or tap "Next") advances one step; swipe-right (or tap "Back") reverses one step. After the last step, a "Done" state is shown with an explicit "Finish" or "Done" affordance — no auto-loop back to step 1.
- **AC-7.5** The ingredient check state from regular recipe detail (AC-4.2) carries into Cook Mode for the current screen lifetime — checking off an ingredient in Cook Mode's drawer and then exiting back to detail shows the same checks, and vice versa. State is **not** persisted across app launches (matches AC-4.2 lifetime contract).
- **AC-7.6** The user exits Cook Mode via an explicit "Done" button in the navigation bar or via the system back gesture. Either restores normal navigation (recipe detail underneath) and re-enables auto-lock per AC-7.3.
- **AC-7.7** Telemetry: a new event `cookModeStarted(recipeID:)` is sent the first time Cook Mode is entered for a given recipe during a session. It carries only the integer WP recipe ID — no free-text payload. The event is added to the constitution §9 allowlist by the consultant-pass amendment; the `AnalyticsEvent` enum gains the corresponding case in the follow-up implementation task (see plan.md Phase 6 cluster, task T-305).

### US-12 — Ingredient search, filters, recents
**As a** cook,
**I want** to search recipes by ingredient and filter results by cook time, category, or what I've cooked before,
**so that** I can find what to make with what I have in 20 minutes.

Added by Tier-4 consultant-pass amendment (CL-19, 2026-05-23). Reframes AC-3.2's "ingredient-body matching is **not required in v1**" caveat. Instead of changing WP's REST surface (it can't be done without server-side work — see CL-1), the app now maintains a local SwiftData ingredient index sourced from the JSON-LD details that the detail view already parses.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-12.1** Ingredient-aware ranking. Each time a recipe's detail JSON-LD parses successfully, the ingredient lines are written into a local SwiftData index (`CachedIngredient`). Subsequent searches run a REST `search=` pass **and** a local ingredient pass and merge with this ranking: (1) REST title contains the query, (2) ~~any other REST hit~~, (3) local-ingredient hit that REST missed. Duplicate recipes dedupe at the highest tier they appear in. *(Amended in T-642 / CL-120: the "any other REST hit" tier is **removed** — body-only matches are no longer admitted, ever. The merger now keeps only REST candidates whose **title** matches the query via `TitleSearchMatcher.match(query:title:)` — `.exact` > `.substring` > `.fuzzy` (plural/singular swap or Levenshtein-1 typo tolerance), stable within tier on WP source order. The merged set's ranking becomes: (1) title `.exact`, (2) title `.substring`, (3) title `.fuzzy`. The local-ingredient pass is **v1-deferred** for the same fix — the user's primary contract is title precision; ingredient search returns as a labeled tier in dad's broader "Make search way better" backlog entry. Paired with `WPRestClient.searchPageSize = 100` (up from 20) so the candidate pool is wide enough to catch every WP title-bearing match before the filter trims body-only noise. Locked by REG-29. See CL-120 for the live-API truth: a 2026-05-30 curl returned exactly 4 posts with "nacho" in their titles; the pre-T-642 pipeline showed 3 + 17 body-only false positives, the post-T-642 pipeline shows the 4 + 0.)* *(Amended in T-643 / CL-121: the merger now sources from **two parallel paths** unioned-deduped — Path A is the T-642 title-match path above (unchanged); Path B is a new category-name match path that fires `?categories=<id>&per_page=100` whenever the query matches a known WP category name via `CategoryNameMatcher.match(query:in:)` (topic-stripping rule: trailing `" recipes"` removed → `"Dessert Recipes"` → topic `"dessert"`; substring-of-topic gated on ≥ 4 characters; junk-query guards reject single-token `"recipe"`/`"recipes"`; matched categories capped at top-2 by post count). Both paths fire via `async let` so Path B doesn't block Path A. Order in the merged set: Path A's tier-ranked title matches first (T-642 ordering intact), then Path B-only contributions in WP's natural date-desc order. Restores the "Dessert Recipes" category-navigation flow T-642 inadvertently broke without weakening the title-precision contract for non-category queries. Locked by REG-30. See CL-121 for the rule and the live-API top-8 category fixture.)* *(Amended in T-649 / CL-127: when the merged result set settles with **fewer than 3 items** AND the user did issue a search (`lastQuery` non-empty), the viewmodel computes a "did you mean?" suggestion via the new pure helper `SearchSuggestionEngine.suggest(query:cachedTitles:minDistance:maxDistance:)` in `DODSupport`. The helper tokenizes the cached recipe titles (lowercase, punctuation-stripped, length ≥ 3 to skip noise tokens like "a"/"of"/"and"), finds the closest cache token to any query token with Levenshtein distance in `(1, 4]` — exclusive of 1 because `TitleSearchMatcher`'s fuzzy tier already absorbs distance-1, inclusive of 4 because past 4 edits the suggestion stops resembling what the user typed — and substitutes only the changed token back into the query (preserving multi-word phrases verbatim — `"cast iron naxhos"` → `"cast iron nachos"`). Tie-break across equal-distance candidates prefers the cache token with higher frequency. Self-suggestions (substituted query normalizes to the same string the user typed) return `nil`. The result populates `SearchViewModel.didYouMean: String?`; the view renders a tappable banner (`"Did you mean: <suggestion>"` in `DODColor.accent`, underlined) above the result list when non-nil. Tap → `viewModel.applyDidYouMean()` rewrites `query` and re-runs the search through the existing debounce path. The cached-titles source is a new `SearchDependencies.cachedRecipeTitles() async throws -> [String]` wrapper over the existing `RecipeStore` cache — no new persistence schema, no new REST surface. See CL-127 for the trigger threshold rationale + the band semantics.)*
- **AC-12.2** Filter chips. Above the results, a horizontal chip row exposes: ~~Category (default "All categories"; tap → menu of every WP category)~~ *(removed in T-636 / CL-105 — the Categories tab covers the canonical "browse all categories" surface; `filters.categoryID` is retained on the model with default `nil` for forward compatibility, no UI mutates it)*, Cook time (default "Any time"; tap → ~~`≤ 15 min` / `≤ 30 min` / `≤ 60 min` / `1 hr+`~~ *(amended in T-644 / CL-122 — the bucket menu is replaced by an Apple-Timer-style two-wheel min/max range sheet: left wheel "Min" / right wheel "Max", first entry of each wheel is "Any" (= nil bound), shared value list of 5/10/15/20/30/45/60/75/90/120/150/180/240 minutes. "Apply" button commits on dismiss; drag-down dismisses without applying; "Reset" link clears both wheels to Any. Chip-label states (no `≤` / `≥` symbol): both nil → `"Any time"`; only min → `"<min> or more"`; only max → `"<max> or less"`; both with `min != max` → `"<min>–<max>"` (en-dash); both with `min == max` → `"<value>"`. Inverted range (min > max) is allowed and silently returns empty results — no UI enforcement. `SearchFilters` slots become `cookTimeMinSeconds: Int?` + `cookTimeMaxSeconds: Int?` replacing the bucket enum; `apply()` becomes `(min ?? 0) <= totalSeconds <= (max ?? .max)`; `isAllDefault` updated accordingly. See CL-122 + REG-31.)*, and ~~a "Recently viewed" toggle~~ *(removed in T-636 / CL-105 — the Recent searches section in `IdleSuggestionsView` covers the dominant "go back to a recent thing" intent; `filters.recentlyViewedOnly` is retained on the model with default `false` for forward compatibility, no UI mutates it)*. ~~Filters are visible whether or not a query is active.~~ *(Amended in T-637 / CL-106: the chip row is hidden while `SearchViewModel.state == .idle` — the idle layout already renders the "Try" / "Recent" idle-suggestions surface, and adding a filter chip there is visually noisy. The chip row renders the moment a search transitions to `.searching` / `.results` / `.noResults` / `.offline` so the user can refine narrowing while results are coming back. No model change; gating is a `@ViewBuilder` switch in `SearchView`.)*
- **AC-12.3** Filters compose; changing one re-ranks the cached merge without a network round trip. Recipes with no category data or no parsed total-time are excluded by the corresponding chip (a MISS), so filter contracts can't be violated by missing metadata. *(Amended in T-637 / CL-106: the "missing = MISS" rule still applies, but the missing set is now hydrated from the network when the cook-time filter is active. `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` populates `lastTotalSecondsByRecipe` from the cache via `dependencies.totalSeconds(forRecipeIDs:)`; if the cook-time filter is on and the map is missing entries for items in the current result set, a follow-up hydration task fetches the JSON-LD `totalTime` via the existing `RecipePageFetcher` + `JSONLDRecipeParser` path — capped at 20 items (one REST page) so the fan-out stays bounded — and calls `reapplyFilters()` when hydration lands. Hydrated total times are written back into the cache via `dependencies.mergeDetail(_:)` so subsequent filter flips don't re-fetch. The initial result render is NOT blocked on hydration; the filter narrows progressively as data lands. See CL-106 for the cache-only-was-the-bug diagnosis and the fan-out-cap rationale.)*
- **AC-12.4** Recent searches persist. The last `RecentSearches.maxEntries` (10) successful queries are stored in UserDefaults (`dod.recentSearchesV1`), case-insensitively deduped and trimmed. In the idle empty state the user sees a "Recent" header + tappable chips; a separate "Try" header lists the top-5 categories by recipe count as one-tap suggestions. Tapping a recent chip re-runs the search through the standard debounce path. *Amended by CL-176 (T-779 / DUT-85, 2026-06-15): a recent is recorded only when the user **commits** a search — Return (`.onSubmit`) or keyboard dismissal (the search field losing focus) — via `SearchViewModel.commitRecentSearch()`, NOT on every debounced search. This fixes mid-typing pauses dropping partial queries ("ch", "chic", …) into Recent. The live as-you-type search and the AC-12.6 telemetry hash are unchanged (telemetry still fires per completed search via `sendSearchTelemetry`); curated-"Try" taps (REG-19) and queries under 2 characters are skipped.* *Amended by CL-193 (T-799 / DUT-112, 2026-06-20): the idle empty state gains a third section, **"Categories"**, rendered below "Try" — the full WP category list (sorted by recipe count, excluding "Latest Recipes" id 1590 and "Uncategorized") as tappable list rows (name · count · disclosure chevron) in the brand `surfaceElevated` card, mirroring the Categories-tab `categoryCard` (AC-19.1). Tapping a row pushes the category's recipes via the existing `.category` route → `CategoryRecipesView`, distinct from the "Try" chips which run an exact text search. Source: `browseCategories` on `SearchViewModel` reuses the `availableCategories` already loaded for the Try slate; the section hides while the list is empty. Phase 1 of folding the Categories tab into Search (US-16); Phase 2 (T-800) removes the now-redundant Categories tab.* *Amended by CL-195 (T-801 / DUT-114, 2026-06-20): three Search-idle polish tweaks — the search-bar placeholder is title-cased ("Search recipes" → "Search Recipes"); the "Try" section header reads "Try Searching"; and the "Categories" platter's corner radius goes 12 → 16pt (`DODSpacing.md`) to match the Try-pill capsule corner radius. Presentation-only.*
- **AC-12.5** Local search performance. The local ingredient pass for a typical cache (≤ 100 unsaved recipes per AC-1.6) returns within **200ms** on iPhone 13 baseline. Implementation note: a SwiftData `#Predicate` substring fetch against ~1000 normalized rows; no in-memory full-table scan.
- **AC-12.6** Telemetry contract unchanged. The recent-searches store keeps raw queries **local only**. Analytics continues to send only `StringHasher.sha256Hex(query)` per AC-3.6.

**Supersedes:** AC-3.2's "ingredient-body matching is **not required in v1**" remains the *spec-level* description of the REST contract; AC-12.1 specifies the locally-indexed augmentation.

### US-13 — Read + post a star rating
**As a** weekend cook,
**I want** to rate a recipe 1–5 stars and see what others rated it,
**so that** I can both contribute and triage the canon of recipes.

Added by the Phase 7 amendment (CL-21, 2026-05-24). Wires the app to WordPress Recipe Maker's existing rating system. A rating is technically a WP comment with `meta.wprm_comment_rating` set; from the user's perspective it's an independent star tap. Posting a rating may also leave a comment (US-14) but doesn't have to.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-13.1** Recipe detail header shows the WPRM rating summary (`/wp-recipe-maker/v1/rating/recipe/<id>`) as `<average star icon> · <count> ratings` when count ≥ 1. When count is 0, the summary is hidden — show only the inline 5-star input prompt "Rate this recipe."
- **AC-13.2** Tapping a star on the input fires the first-time guest-identity sheet if Keychain has no identity (US-15); otherwise immediately POSTs to `/wp-recipe-maker/v1/rating` with name + email from the Keychain identity, the star value, and the recipe id.
- **AC-13.3** After a successful POST, the input row collapses to "You rated this <stars>" with an "Edit" affordance; tapping Edit re-opens the star input and a subsequent POST overwrites the prior rating (WPRM dedupes by email).
- **AC-13.4** Network or server error: show inline "Couldn't save your rating — try again" with a retry button. Persisted rating state from Keychain so a stale rating shows on relaunch even before the network round-trip.
- **AC-13.5** Rating telemetry: new `AnalyticsEvent.recipeRated(recipeID:stars:)` event (constitution §9 amendment — see CL-21). No raw user text in the payload.

### US-14 — Read + post comments
**As a** weekend cook,
**I want** to read other cooks' comments and add my own,
**so that** I can share tips and learn from people who've made the recipe.

Added by the Phase 7 amendment (CL-21, 2026-05-24).

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-14.1** Recipe detail has an expandable "Comments (<count>)" section below the instructions. Collapsed by default; tapping expands and lazy-loads `/wp/v2/comments?post=<id>&per_page=10&_embed=author`. Newest-first.
- **AC-14.2** Each comment row shows: author name, optional avatar (from `author_avatar_urls`), relative date ("3 days ago"), comment body (HTML-stripped via existing `HTMLSanitizer`), and the comment's star rating (if `meta.wprm_comment_rating > 0`) — small inline stars under the body.
- **AC-14.3** A "Write a comment" CTA at the bottom of the section opens a composer sheet: text field (1000-char max), optional star row (defaults to user's existing rating from US-13 if any), Submit button. Submit fires the guest-identity sheet (US-15) if needed, then POSTs to `/wp/v2/comments` with `post`, `author_name`, `author_email`, `content`, and (if a star was chosen) `meta: { wprm_comment_rating: <stars> }`.
- **AC-14.4** Post-submit feedback: if the response status is `approved`, the new comment appears immediately at the top of the list with a brief "Posted" snackbar. If status is `hold` (default — moderation queue), show a "Your comment is awaiting approval" snackbar and do NOT prepend it to the visible list (per AC-14.2 we only render `approved`). Either way the composer dismisses.
- **AC-14.5** Read path tolerates pagination: a "Load more" button at the bottom fetches the next page when `X-WP-TotalPages` says there are more.
- **AC-14.6** Offline read: comments cached locally on previous fetches show with an "(offline)" pill — see DODPersistence change (Wave-1 sub 3). Posting offline is queued (best-effort) and shown as "pending — will send when online" — Sub 3's responsibility.
- **AC-14.7** Comment telemetry: new `AnalyticsEvent.recipeCommentSubmitted(recipeID:moderated:Bool)`. No raw comment text. The `moderated` field is true when WP returns `hold`, false when `approved`. (Constitution §9 amendment — see CL-21.)

### US-15 — Guest identity (name + email, no account)
**As any** user about to post,
**I want** to tell the app my display name and email **once**,
**so that** I don't have to re-enter it on every comment or rating.

Added by the Phase 7 amendment (CL-21, 2026-05-24). The guest-identity model — name + email, Keychain-only, no password — is the constitutional substitute for accounts; see constitution §9.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-15.1** First tap to rate (US-13) or comment (US-14) presents a non-dismissible sheet asking for "Display name" (1–40 chars, no validation beyond non-empty) and "Email" (basic format validation: contains `@` and `.`, rejects obvious garbage). Both required to proceed.
- **AC-15.2** Submitting the sheet writes both values to the iOS Keychain under service `com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp.guest` (account: `display-name` and `email`). The sheet then dismisses and resumes the pending rate/post action automatically.
- **AC-15.3** A future Settings affordance (out of scope for v1; tracked as CL-22) will let users edit or clear their guest identity. For v1.0, identity can only be cleared by uninstalling the app.
- **AC-15.4** No identity field is ever sent to TelemetryDeck. Name and email travel ONLY to dutchovendaddy.com over HTTPS.
- **AC-15.5** Keychain access uses the modern `Security` framework via a small wrapper in `DODSupport` (Wave-1 sub 3 builds it). Reads are synchronous + cheap; writes throw on Keychain error.

**Integration shipped:** Wave-2 wired US-13/14/15 into `RecipeDetailView` (new `RecipeDetailRatingsSection` + view-model state + composition-root wiring) in commit `<filled in post-commit>` (T-220).

### US-8 — First-launch onboarding
**As a** Weekend Cook on first cold launch,
**I want** a brief welcome explaining what the app does,
**so that** I'm oriented before I land on the feed.

Added by consultant-pass amendment (CL-17, 2026-05-23). Reverses the earlier CL-7 "no onboarding" decision after the consultant pass argued that a single-screen sheet costs almost nothing and helps first-time users recognize the bookmark-save and search affordances before they need them. *(The original CL-17 wording said "heart-save"; CL-38 / T-380 (2026-05-24) flipped the glyph everywhere in the saved-recipes context, so the rationale now reads "bookmark-save" to stay in lock-step with AC-8.1's user-facing copy.)*

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-8.1** On the first cold launch — gated by a single `UserDefaults` flag `dod.onboardingCompletedV1` — the app presents a single-screen modal sheet over the Feed. The sheet contains a friendly welcome line and exactly three bullets: "Browse the latest recipes from dutchovendaddy.com", "Search for what you're craving", and "Tap the bookmark to save any recipe for offline cooking". *Amended by CL-38 (T-380, 2026-05-24); the third bullet's "heart" wording is superseded so the onboarding affordance lines up with the post-T-380 glyph everywhere else. Original third bullet: ~~"Tap the heart to save any recipe for offline cooking"~~* *Amended by CL-159 (T-762 / DUT-68, 2026-06-12): the welcome sheet now shows **four** bullets. The third "save" bullet is reworded to the save-a-favorite-for-findability framing ("Save your favorites" / "Tap the bookmark on any recipe to find it again later.") — T-761 / CL-158 decoupled Save from the offline download, so the old "save … for offline cooking" wording is dropped. A fourth **iCloud Sync capability** bullet is added ("Sync across devices" / "Turn on iCloud Sync in Settings to keep your saved recipes on every device.") — informational only, with **no toggle** on the splash (the opt-in lives in Settings per AC-41.3). This single welcome sheet is now the ONLY first-launch sheet — the AC-41.2 iCloud-Sync opt-in sheet is removed (see CL-159).*
- **AC-8.2** A "Get cooking" primary button dismisses the sheet and sets `dod.onboardingCompletedV1 = true`. All future cold launches go straight to Feed with no sheet.
- **AC-8.3** iPad first launch shows the same single-screen sheet (same content, same flag) — no separate iPad onboarding flow. The sheet sizes appropriately for iPad via standard SwiftUI `.sheet` presentation; the flag is shared with iPhone since saves and UserDefaults are per-install (consistent with AC-5.7 / CL-5).

### US-9 — Home-screen widget
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** a home-screen widget showing today's featured recipe,
**so that** I can jump straight into a new recipe without opening the app and scrolling the feed.

Added by consultant-pass amendment (2026-05-23). Constitution §2 was amended in the same pass to bring a WidgetKit extension into v1.0 platform scope. Watch / Mac / Vision targets remain out. The widget ships as a separate `app-extension` target `DODAppWidget` (NSExtensionPointIdentifier `com.apple.widgetkit-extension`) embedded in the host app bundle.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-9.1** A "Today's Recipe" widget is available in the iOS widget gallery in both `systemSmall` (square) and `systemMedium` (wide) sizes. Small surfaces hero + title + total-time chip on a gradient overlay; medium surfaces hero on the left with a "New on DOD" eyebrow, title, excerpt, and total-time chip on the right. Large + Lock Screen accessory families are deferred to v2 (documented in `Marketing/TestFlight.md`). *Amended by CL-165 (T-768 / DUT-74, 2026-06-13): `systemLarge` is now supported — a hero-forward layout (`WidgetCard.FeaturedLarge`): a full-width hero photo on top, with the "Today on DOD" eyebrow + title + excerpt + total-time chip beneath it on the tint-aware container (no text-over-image, so it stays legible in Tinted/Clear without a scrim per AC-23.7). Lock Screen accessory families remain their own widget (US-22).* *Further amended by CL-166 (T-769 / DUT-75, 2026-06-13): the Featured eyebrow now reads "New on DOD" on both the medium and large faces (was "Today on DOD" — superseding the wording above and in the CL-165 note); the large layout pins its text block to natural height (`.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)`) and uses a `.headline` title, so long real-world titles/excerpts no longer clip.*
- **AC-9.2** Tapping the widget deep-links into the app via `dod://recipe/<id>` for a populated widget, or `dod://feed` for the placeholder. The app handles the URL in `RootView.onOpenURL`, switches the active tab to Feed, and pushes the recipe-detail screen (or clears the navigation stack for `dod://feed`). The push uses the cached `RecipeListItem` when available and otherwise falls back to the widget snapshot's own copy of title/hero/excerpt so the detail screen opens instantly even when the cache is cold.
- **AC-9.3** The widget timeline refreshes every 4 hours **or** sooner — the app calls `WidgetCenter.shared.reloadAllTimelines()` after every successful feed load so a fresh top-of-feed item appears within seconds of the user pulling-to-refresh. Data flows via the shared App Group `group.com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp`: `WidgetSnapshotStore` writes the top 5 list items (`id`, `title`, `excerpt`, `heroImageURL`, `canonicalURL`, `publishedAt`, `totalTimeDisplay`) to a UserDefaults key under that suite; the widget's `TimelineProvider` reads the same key. Snapshots carry a `version` tag — readers on a mismatched version return `nil` and the widget surfaces the placeholder per AC-9.4.
- **AC-9.4** When no snapshot exists (first launch, App Group unavailable in a non-provisioned build, or a version mismatch), the widget shows a placeholder layout that says "Open the app to see today's featured recipe here." rather than a crash or a blank panel. The placeholder is also what WidgetKit hands the gallery preview so the widget renders nicely while the user is still picking a size.

### US-10 — App Intents + Siri Shortcuts
**As any** user,
**I want** to ask Siri to open a recipe by name or start cooking,
**so that** I can hand-free my way into the app.

Added by consultant-pass tier-3 amendment (2026-05-23). Wires the existing recipe corpus into Apple's AppIntents framework so the app surfaces in Spotlight, Siri, the Shortcuts app, and Action Button automations without the user having to manually configure anything. No new analytics events — invocations rely on the existing `.recipeView` / `.cookModeStarted` events that fire whenever the corresponding view appears, regardless of entry vector (see constitution §9 note below).

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-10.1** Three `AppIntent`s are registered with the system: `OpenRecipeIntent(recipe:)`, `StartCookModeIntent(recipe:)`, and `OpenSavedRecipesIntent` (no parameters). The `recipe` parameter is a `RecipeEntity` whose `EntityQuery` resolves by id via `RecipeStore.recipeWithoutTouching(id:)` (the silent accessor — Siri surfacing must not pollute the LRU `lastViewedAt` ordering) and whose `suggestedEntities()` returns the union of saved + `recentlyViewed(limit:)` rows, deduplicated by id. Both store accessors are exercised by unit tests in `DODPersistenceTests.RecentlyViewedTests` (US-10).
- **AC-10.2** Each intent's `perform()` posts a `DeepLinkIntent` to the shared `DeepLinkDispatcher`; `RootView` observes the dispatcher and routes the intent into tab + NavigationStack state. The same URL grammar — `dod://recipe?id=<int>`, `dod://recipe/cook?id=<int>`, `dod://saved` — is also accepted by SwiftUI's `onOpenURL`, so any externally-launched URL behaves identically to an intent invocation. The `dod` scheme is registered in `project.yml` via `CFBundleURLTypes`. The pure parser (`DeepLinkIntent.parse(_:)`) lives in `DODSupport` and is locked by `DODSupportTests.DeepLinkIntentTests`.
- **AC-10.3** On every cold launch, `RootView.indexSpotlight()` writes the current saved + recently-viewed entities to `CSSearchableIndex.default()` as `CSSearchableItem`s (the iOS-17-compatible path; `indexAppEntities(_:)` requires iOS 18+). Tapping a Spotlight result re-launches the app via `onContinueUserActivity(CSSearchableItemActionType)` and routes through the same `handle(intent:)` path as Siri.
- **AC-10.4** `DODShortcuts: AppShortcutsProvider` registers all three intents with the system so they show up in the Shortcuts app and Spotlight automatically. Phrases include "Open ${recipe} in Dutch Oven Daddy", "Start cooking ${recipe} in Dutch Oven Daddy", and "Show my saved recipes in Dutch Oven Daddy". Per Apple's iOS 17 AppIntents guidance, every parameterized phrase contains `\(.applicationName)` — the framework rejects the build otherwise. Donation strategy: we rely on `AppShortcutsProvider` auto-discovery rather than per-detail-open `IntentDonationManager` calls (iOS 17 honors the static phrase list at install time, so per-view donations are redundant for the three top-level surfaces shipped here).

**Constitution §9 note:** intent invocations do NOT add new tracked events. `OpenRecipeIntent` lands on the detail screen, which fires the existing `.recipeView` event from `RecipeDetailViewModel.onAppear()`. `StartCookModeIntent` additionally triggers the existing `.cookModeStarted` event via the auto-presentation path. App Privacy posture is unchanged.

### US-11 — Live Activity for active Cook Mode timer
**As a** user actively cooking,
**I want** a Live Activity for the running Cook Mode timer,
**so that** I can leave the app to grab ingredients and come back at the buzzer without re-opening Cook Mode.

Added by consultant-pass amendment (2026-05-23, Tier 3). Builds on US-7 / AC-7.* — the Live Activity is a per-step companion to the inline ``CookTimer``, not a replacement. Constitution §2 was amended in the same pass to call out ActivityKit (iOS 16.1+) as the platform surface Cook Mode timers use.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-11.1** Given Cook Mode is on a step whose text parses to a duration (per `StepTimerParser`), when the user taps Start on the inline `CookTimer`, then a Live Activity is created with `recipeTitle`, `recipeID`, `totalSeconds`, and an initial `ContentState(remainingSeconds: totalSeconds, stepText: <current step text>, isPaused: false)`. The card appears on the Lock Screen and, on iPhone 14 Pro and later, in the Dynamic Island.
- **AC-11.2** While the timer is running, the Live Activity is updated every second with the new `remainingSeconds`; pausing the inline timer flips `isPaused` to true on the next update so the lock-screen UI can dim the progress arc and label the state.
- **AC-11.3** The Live Activity ends when (a) the countdown hits zero, (b) the user taps Reset, (c) the user starts a new step's timer (the previous activity is replaced, not stacked), or (d) Cook Mode itself is exited via the Done button or `endCookMode`.
- **AC-11.4** On hosts where ActivityKit isn't available (`iOS < 16.1`) or where the user has disabled Live Activities system-wide (`ActivityAuthorizationInfo().areActivitiesEnabled == false`), Cook Mode behaves exactly as it did pre-US-11 — the inline timer still runs, no Lock Screen card appears, and no errors surface to the user.

### US-16 — Tab bar refinement (Saved promoted, bookmark icon)
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** the "Saved" tab to live closer to the home tab and use a bookmark icon,
**so that** my saved recipes feel like a real shelf I return to (not a passing favorite) and are within easier thumb reach.

Added post-Phase 6 (2026-05-24). Aesthetic + ergonomic refinement; no new data, no new screens.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-16.1** Tab order on the bottom bar is **Recipes → Categories → Saved → Search** (Saved moves from position 4 to position 3; Search moves from position 3 to position 4). The `AppTab.allCases` order is the single source of truth and is what changes. *Amended by CL-194 (T-800 / DUT-113, 2026-06-20): the **Categories tab is removed** — its browse list moved into the Search idle view as a list-rows section below the "Try" chips (CL-193). The bottom bar is now **Recipes → Saved → Search** (3 tabs); `AppTab.allCases` drops `.categories`. The category-recipes screen (`CategoryRecipesView`) is unchanged — it is now reached by tapping a category row in Search rather than via the tab + `CategoryListView` (both removed).*
- **AC-16.2** The "Saved" tab uses SF Symbol `bookmark` when unselected and `bookmark.fill` when selected. Selection-aware variants follow the same pattern as existing system tabs (no custom asset).
- **AC-16.3** The in-recipe Save affordance (per `AC-4.7`) also uses `bookmark` / `bookmark.fill`, matching the Saved tab icon (AC-16.2) and every other saved-recipes surface. *Amended by CL-38 (T-380, 2026-05-24); supersedes the earlier carve-out from T-310 that kept the in-recipe heart in v1 — the "follow-up if it feels inconsistent" hedge in the original wording is the lineage being executed here. Original: ~~"The in-recipe Save affordance (`AC-4.7`, navigation-bar heart) is **unchanged in v1** — only the tab icon flips. Revisiting the in-recipe icon is a follow-up if it feels inconsistent in user testing; left out to keep this change reversible and surface a single decision at a time."~~*
- **AC-16.4** Telemetry: `AppTab.telemetryName` mapping is unchanged (`"saved"` stays `"saved"`); the change is purely visual + ordering, so existing screen-view event counts remain comparable across the change.
- **AC-16.5** L4 snapshot tests cover the tab bar in **light** appearance, iPhone 13 + iPad 12.9", with each tab selected. Baselines are updated as part of the implementing PR (intentional visual change). *Amended by CL-115 (T-730, 2026-05-30) — the dark-appearance tab-bar baselines are dropped because iOS 26's translucent tab-bar material renders non-deterministically off-screen (~0.2% pixel match run-to-run on the same machine), so it can never be a stable pixel baseline; the tab bar's order / icons / labels stay pinned by `DODAppUnitTests/AppTabTests` + `SmokeTests.test_tabBarOrderMatchesSpec`, and the light snapshots keep the rendered-chrome cover. *Amended by CL-194 (T-800 / DUT-113, 2026-06-20): with the Categories tab removed, `TabBarFixture` re-declares 3 tabs; the 2 `categoriesSelected` baselines are deleted and the remaining 6 (feed / saved / search × iPhone 13 / iPad 12.9") are re-recorded for the 3-tab layout.* Original: ~~"L4 snapshot tests cover the tab bar in both light and dark appearance, iPhone 13 + iPad 12.9", with each tab selected."~~*
- **AC-16.6** L3 UI smoke test asserts the third tab from the left opens Saved (was Search) and the fourth opens Search (was Saved), guarding against accidental re-ordering in a future refactor. *Amended by CL-194 (T-800 / DUT-113, 2026-06-20): with the Categories tab removed, the bar is 3 tabs — `SmokeTests.test_tabBarOrderMatchesSpec` now asserts `tabButtons.count == 3` and the order Recipes (0) → Saved (1) → Search (2); the L5 `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests` index-based Search lookup moves from `tabButtons[3]` to `tabButtons[2]`.*

### US-17 — Home-screen widget for saved recipes
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** a home-screen widget that shows my saved recipes,
**so that** I can jump straight into one I've already bookmarked without opening the app and scrolling to the Saved tab.

Added post-Phase 6 (2026-05-24). Builds on the US-9 widget infrastructure (`WidgetSnapshotStore`, `WidgetDeepLinkParser`, `WidgetCard`, App Group container). This story adds a **second** widget alongside the existing today's-featured one; it does not replace it.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-17.1** A new home-screen widget kind, `SavedRecipesWidget`, is added to `DODAppWidgetBundle` alongside `FeaturedRecipeWidget`. The widget's display name is "Saved Recipes" and its description is "Quick access to your saved recipes." *Amended by CL-40 (T-400, 2026-05-25); supersedes the earlier widget-gallery description copy. Original: ~~"Your saved recipes, one tap from a cook."~~*
- **AC-17.2** Supported sizes are `.systemSmall` (1 saved recipe) and `.systemMedium` (3 saved recipes). `.systemLarge` is **out of scope for v1** — revisit only if user testing surfaces demand. *Amended by CL-165 (T-768 / DUT-74, 2026-06-13): `.systemLarge` is now supported — up to **5** saved recipes in the same thumbnail-row list as medium (`WidgetCard.SavedLarge`; the entry view shares `savedListBody(maxRows:)` between medium=3 and large=5). The snapshot cap `SavedRecipesWidgetSnapshotConfig.maxEntries` is raised 3 → 5 so the host writes enough rows; small/medium still take `prefix(1)`/`prefix(3)`. Supersedes CL-26's small+medium-only deferral.*
- **AC-17.3** Widget content is sourced from a new snapshot file (`SavedRecipesWidgetSnapshot`) written to the App Group container by the host app whenever the saved set changes (observe `SavedStore` from `DODApp`). Snapshot carries the N most-recently-saved recipes (small=1, medium=3) with title + cached hero-image filename + canonical URL + recipe ID + saved-at timestamp. *Amended by CL-163 (T-766 / DUT-72, 2026-06-13): the `heroImageFilename` is now actually populated — earlier builds hardcoded it to `nil` (a documented T-322 deferral), so the Saved widget always rendered the gradient placeholder instead of the recipe photo. `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.toSnapshotEntry` now emits `WidgetImageBridge.filename(for:)` for any saved recipe whose hero bytes are cached (`heroImageCached`), which `RecipeStore.cacheImage` has already mirrored into the App Group container (AC-21.2). The widget reads the local file and renders the full-color photo — the same path the Featured widget uses; an uncached recipe still falls back to the gradient placeholder (AC-21.3).* *Further amended by CL-167 (T-770 / DUT-76, 2026-06-13): the `heroImageFilename` is now emitted **unconditionally** from the hero URL (the `heroImageCached` gate is dropped), and `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.publish()` **actively prefetches + bridges** the hero bytes for saved recipes whose photos aren't cached yet — the feed-save case (a recipe saved from the feed without ever opening detail, where `RecipeStore.cacheImage` never ran), which the CL-163 gate left permanently photoless. The prefetch mirrors `LiveFeedDependencies.publishWidgetSnapshot`: detached so the save round-trip isn't blocked on the network, gated on `!heroImageCached` so only the missing photos are fetched, routed through `ImageLoader` + `RecipeStore.cacheImage` (which writes the App Group bridge file), then a second reload so the freshly-bridged photos render. Wired into both save paths via `AppDependencies.savedWidgetPublisher()` (recipe-detail + `TabStack.saveFromCard`).* *Further amended by CL-169 (T-772 / DUT-79, 2026-06-13): fixed the Saved widget's filename→URL resolver. `SavedRecipesWidgetEntryView` had its own hand-rolled `heroImageURL(forFilename:)` that appended the filename to the App Group container **root**, missing the `widget-images/` subdirectory that `WidgetImageBridge.writeImage` writes to (and that the Featured widget reads from via `WidgetImageBridge.fileURL`). So even with the filename set (CL-163) and the bytes actively bridged (CL-167), the Saved widget looked one directory too high (`…/<sha>.img` instead of `…/widget-images/<sha>.img`), never found the file, and rendered the gradient for every row — which is why the Latest widget showed photos but the Saved widget never did. The resolver now delegates to `WidgetImageBridge.fileURL(forFilename:)` — the single canonical resolver both widgets share, whose subdirectory inclusion is locked by `WidgetImageBridgeTests.fileURLReturnsContainerPlusSubdirectoryPlusFilename`.*
- **AC-17.4** Tapping a recipe row in the widget opens its detail via the existing `dod://recipe/<id>` deep link (US-9 / `WidgetDeepLinkParser`). Tapping the widget chrome (outside any recipe row) opens the Saved tab via a new `dod://saved` deep link.
- **AC-17.5** Empty state: when no recipes are saved, the widget renders a placeholder ("Save a recipe to see it here") that tap-targets to the Saved tab (`dod://saved`). The widget never crashes or shows broken images for missing data.
- **AC-17.6** Timeline: the widget reloads at most once every 15 minutes from snapshot changes; the host app forces a reload via `WidgetCenter.shared.reloadTimelines(ofKind:)` whenever `SavedStore` writes or removes a recipe. No network calls from the widget extension itself.
- **AC-17.7** L4 snapshot tests cover empty / 1-saved / 3-saved states on both small + medium sizes, both light + dark appearances. Baselines committed.
- **AC-17.8** L1 unit tests cover: snapshot encode/decode round-trip, max-entries cap (small=1 / medium=3), version-mismatch rejection, and the new `dod://saved` deep-link parse case. Existing `WidgetDeepLinkParserTests` is extended; new `SavedRecipesWidgetSnapshotTests` lives next to the existing `WidgetSnapshotTests`.
- **AC-17.9** Telemetry: a new event `widgetOpened(kind: "saved" | "featured", recipeID: Int?)` replaces the implicit "widget deep link consumed" log. Constitution §9 allowlist updated in the implementing PR. Existing today's-featured tap continues to deep-link the same way; only the analytics shape generalizes.

### US-18 — Light/dark mode appearance polish
**As any** user,
**I want** every surface in the app to look intentional in both light and dark mode at every Dynamic Type size,
**so that** the app feels finished regardless of my system appearance.

Added post-Phase 6 (2026-05-24). Constitution §7 already mandates WCAG AA contrast in both modes — this story scopes the **audit + targeted fixes** to land the mandate everywhere it isn't already enforced by an existing snapshot test.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-18.1** Every top-level screen from US-1 through US-15 (Feed, Categories list, Category detail, Search, Recipe detail, Saved, Cook Mode, Onboarding, plus the comments + ratings section added by US-13/14/15) has L4 snapshot coverage in **both** light and dark appearances on iPhone 13 baseline. Gaps surfaced by the audit are filled by the implementing PR before any visual change is made (so the diff is provable, not opinion).
- **AC-18.2** Every reusable component in `DODDesignSystem/Components/` has L4 snapshot coverage in both appearances at default + AX5 Dynamic Type sizes. Same rule: existing gaps filled first, fixes proven by the diff.
- **AC-18.3** Audit checklist is captured in a new `specs/dod-ios-app/appearance-audit.md` (sibling to `accessibility-audit.md` and `performance-audit.md`). Each surface + component row records: light pass/fail, dark pass/fail, notes on any fix applied, baseline snapshot reference.
- **AC-18.4** Any contrast failure (WCAG AA, computed against the actual rendered tokens — not against the design system's stated values) is resolved either by adjusting the token in `DODDesignSystem.Colors` or by overriding at the offending site with a documented reason. No silent overrides.
- **AC-18.5** Reduce-Transparency and Reduce-Motion respected in both appearances (already required by constitution §7; the audit verifies it).
- **AC-18.6** The audit is allowed to surface *no* code changes — a clean audit closes the story. The deliverable is the audit document plus any fixes the audit prompted, not a guaranteed list of fixes.

### US-19 — Categories tab visual modernization

> **Retired by CL-194 / T-800 (DUT-113, 2026-06-20):** the Categories tab was removed (its browse list moved into the Search idle view, CL-193). The ACs below describe the tab as it shipped; the category-recipes screen they reference (`CategoryRecipesView`) is KEPT and is now reached from Search. `CategoryListView` + `CategoryListViewModel` were deleted.
**As a** Recipe Hunter,
**I want** the Categories tab to feel like a current-iOS surface and to let me jump to a category by name,
**so that** scanning ~30+ categories doesn't read like a 2018 dictionary and I can find "Soups" without scrolling.

Added post-Phase 8 (2026-05-24). The Categories tab (US-2) was untouched by the CC-9 visual-density pass — that work only re-laid the four *grid* surfaces. The categories list still uses `.plain` style with a hand-rolled chevron + count `HStack`, which now reads stale next to the modernized Feed / Search / Saved. This story is a **Categories-only layout pass** that swaps in iOS-stock containers; it touches no design tokens and leaves the underlying data contract (US-2) intact.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-19.1** The Categories list uses SwiftUI's `.insetGrouped` list style so rows render inside a system-grouped card with iOS-standard rounded corners, inset, and separators — replacing the current `.plain` flat-list look. Each row is a `NavigationLink`-shaped cell with the system disclosure indicator instead of the hand-rolled `Image(systemName: "chevron.right")` in the existing `HStack`. *Amended by CL-177 (T-781, 2026-06-15): the `.insetGrouped` `List` is REPLACED by a `ScrollView` + a single brand rounded card (`categoryCard` — `DODColor.surfaceElevated` fill, rows split by `DODColor.surfaceDivider` hairlines, clipped to a `DODSpacing.sm` `RoundedRectangle`) so the `DODScreenHeader('Categories')` title + `DODSearchField` scroll above the categories without the List section-grouping squaring off the first cell's top corners or adding its top inset. The row composition — name + count + system disclosure chevron — and the per-row accessibility label are preserved.*
- **AC-19.2** Each row displays the category name on the leading side and the recipe count on the trailing side rendered as a `Text` in `DODColor.labelSecondary` (the system "value" treatment), with the disclosure chevron after it. No new design-system tokens; the visual ramp is built from the existing `DODType` / `DODColor` / `DODSpacing` set per CL-31 / CL-32.
- **AC-19.3** ~~A native `.searchable` field is added to the Categories list, scoped to the list (placement `.navigationBarDrawer(displayMode: .automatic)` — the iOS standard).~~ *(Amended in T-648 / CL-126: the `.searchable` modifier is **removed**; the search bar is now the shared `DODSearchField` (`DODDesignSystem.Components.DODSearchField`) slotted into a `VStack(spacing: 0)` above the `List` so the Categories-tab search bar matches the Search-tab bar visually — both render `DODColor.surfaceElevated` brand brown filled into a `Capsule(style: .continuous)` shape per REG-32. The deliberate trade: the bar no longer slides into a nav-bar drawer the way `.searchable` did — it sits as a sticky element above the List in exchange for visual unity with Search.)* Filtering matches `category.name` case-insensitively; clearing the field restores the full list. The filter is purely client-side over the already-loaded `categories` array — no new network call, no view-model state machine beyond a `@State searchText`. *Amended by CL-177 (T-781, 2026-06-15): the `DODSearchField` moves from the sticky `VStack` above the `List` into the `ScrollView` as scrolling content (above the `categoryCard`); its border + soft shadow now come from the `DODSearchField` component itself (the DUT-25 affordance was folded in), so the Categories field matches the Search field by construction.*
- **AC-19.4** US-2's existing acceptance criteria (AC-2.1 alphabetical order, AC-2.2 name + count, AC-2.3 tap → category recipes, AC-2.4 zero-count hidden, AC-2.5 error state with Retry) are **not regressed**. The implementing PR's tests pin each of AC-2.1..AC-2.5 against the new layout. The Categories *recipes* sub-screen (`CategoryRecipesView`, AC-2.3) is out of scope for this story — only the list of categories themselves is restyled.
- **AC-19.5** L4 snapshot coverage exists for the new layout on iPhone 13 baseline + iPad 12.9" in both light and dark appearances, at default Dynamic Type. The four existing `CategoryListViewSnapshotTests` baselines (light/dark × default/AX5 on iPhone 13) are re-recorded as part of this PR; new iPad-12.9" baselines (light + dark, default Dynamic Type) are added. AX5 on iPad is not added — list rows don't visually differ from iPhone at AX5 in a way the existing iPhone AX5 baseline doesn't already cover.
- **AC-19.6** Accessibility per constitution §7 / CC-1: the existing `.accessibilityLabel("\(category.name), \(category.count) recipes")` per-row label is preserved; the new search field has a default `.searchable` VoiceOver label ("Search"); the empty result state when no categories match the typed query shows an inline secondary-label "No categories match '<query>'" message (no full `EmptyState` takeover — the field stays visible).

### US-20 — Search filter chips use semantically clearer SF Symbols
**As a** Recipe Hunter using the Search tab,
**I want** the filter chips to use SF Symbols that read as "filter by this attribute" rather than "navigate into a container,"
**so that** the affordance matches the action (the category chip narrows results in place; it does not push a new screen).

Added post-Phase 9 (2026-05-24). The Search tab's category filter chip (and the matching "Try" category-suggestion pills in the idle state) currently render with the `folder` SF Symbol. The glyph reads as "open this folder" — a navigation affordance — but tapping the chip opens a filter menu and re-ranks the existing results in place per AC-12.3. Swapping to `tag.fill` aligns the glyph with the action: WordPress categories *are* taxonomy terms (CL-3), and `tag.fill` is the iOS-stock glyph for "filter by taxonomy" (Reminders' tag filter, Files' tag column, Photos' people-and-pets tag chips all use it). This is a **pure SF Symbol swap** — no layout change, no token change, no behavior change.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-20.1** In `FilterChipRow.categoryChip` the `systemImage` argument to `chipLabel(...)` changes from `"folder"` to `"tag.fill"`. The chip's text, menu contents, `isOn` state, and accessibility label (`"Category filter, \(selectedCategoryName)"`) are unchanged. The selected/unselected fill state (cast-iron-brown capsule when a category is picked, surface-elevated otherwise) is preserved verbatim.
- **AC-20.2** In `IdleSuggestionsView` the "Try" section's `pill(text: category.name, systemImage: "folder")` call site changes its `systemImage` argument from `"folder"` to `"tag.fill"` so the idle-state category suggestions match the filter chip glyph. The pill's text, action, accessibility label (`"\(text), suggestion"`), and surface-elevated capsule are unchanged.
