- **AC-20.3** The `questionmark.folder` glyph used by the `.noResults` `EmptyState` (line ~74 of `SearchView.swift`) is **not** changed by this story — that glyph denotes a generic search-not-found state, not a category-filter chip, and the iOS-stock pattern there is `questionmark.folder` or `magnifyingglass`. Out of bounds; called out explicitly to prevent scope creep.
- **AC-20.4** L4 snapshot coverage exists for `FilterChipRow` in both selected and unselected states across light + dark appearances at default Dynamic Type on iPhone 13 baseline (4 PNGs total). The existing T-332 full-screen `SearchViewSnapshotTests` baselines are owned by T-335 and are **not** committed by this PR — focused chip-only baselines are added in a new `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` file to lock the iconography without colliding with T-335's wider deliverable.
- **AC-20.5** US-12's existing acceptance criteria (AC-12.1 ingredient-aware ranking, AC-12.2 filter chips above results, AC-12.3 filters compose without network, AC-12.4 recent searches + idle suggestions, AC-12.5 200ms local-pass budget, AC-12.6 telemetry contract) are **not regressed**. The implementing PR's existing `SearchViewModelTests` + `SearchFiltersTests` + `SearchResultMergerTests` + `RecentSearchesTests` suites still pass against the new view; no view-model or filter-logic code is touched.

### US-21 — Featured-recipe widget renders the actual hero image (Latest Recipe rename)
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** the home-screen featured widget to show the actual recipe hero image (not a placeholder glyph), and to be labeled "Latest Recipe" so I know it reflects the newest post,
**so that** my home screen looks like a real recipe widget and the name matches what it actually shows.

Added 2026-05-24 (post-Phase 9). Refines the existing US-9 "Today's Recipe" widget on two axes:

1. **Display-name rename** — "Today's Recipe" → "Latest Recipe." The widget already reflects whatever sits at the top of the WP feed (i.e. the newest post), not a daily-curated pick. The "Today's" name implied a cadence the widget never honored; "Latest" matches what it actually shows. CL-36 captures the rationale.
2. **Real hero image render** — the widget currently shows the knife-and-fork placeholder glyph (`fork.knife` over a gradient) instead of the recipe's hero image. The widget's `WidgetCard.Hero` view supports an image URL but the snapshot wire format carries only a remote `heroImageURL` — feeding that to `AsyncImage` inside the widget extension would trigger a network fetch on every timeline reload, which constitution §9 implicit and AC-17.6's "no network calls from the widget extension itself" forbid by analogy. The fix is a **file-export image bridge** so the widget reads cached bytes from the shared App Group container instead. CL-35 captures the bridge choice (file-export vs shared-SwiftData-container vs widget-side URLSession) and why the file-export option wins.

US-9's existing acceptance criteria (AC-9.1 small + medium sizes, AC-9.2 deep-link routing, AC-9.3 4-hour timeline + app-driven reload, AC-9.4 placeholder fallback) are **not regressed**. AC-21.5 explicitly pins them.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-21.1** The featured-recipe widget's `.configurationDisplayName(_:)` is "Latest Recipe" (was "Today's Recipe"). Description copy stays unchanged ("See the latest Dutch Oven Daddy recipe right on your home screen.") — only the display name changes. Users picking the widget from the iOS gallery see the new name; existing widgets installed before the rename pick up the new name on the next system widget-gallery refresh (system-managed, not app-controlled).
- **AC-21.2** When the host has cached a recipe's hero image bytes (via the existing `RecipeStore.cacheImage(url:bytes:)` path used by the saved-recipes pre-download AC-5.2 and by any in-app feed/detail image load), those bytes are **also** written as a file inside the shared App Group container under a deterministic filename derived from the image URL. The filename is plumbed into the widget snapshot the host writes after every successful feed load (US-9 / AC-9.3 — the existing `WidgetSnapshotStore.write` call site in `LiveFeedDependencies.publishWidgetSnapshot(items:)`).
- **AC-21.3** The featured-recipe widget extension's `WidgetCard.Hero` view resolves the snapshot's filename into a `file://` URL pointing at the App Group container and renders the image via `AsyncImage`. The widget extension makes **no** network calls. When the filename is `nil` (first launch, app group missing, image bytes not yet cached for the top-of-feed recipe), the widget falls back to the existing knife-and-fork gradient placeholder — same view, no visual change for the empty case.
- **AC-21.4** Image bridge eviction stays aligned with the existing image cache budget (NFR-2 / 200 MB). When `RecipeStore.evictImagesIfNeeded()` deletes a `CachedImage` row, the corresponding App Group file is also deleted so the two stores never drift. The bridge is a write-through cache, not an independent store.
- **AC-21.5** US-9's existing ACs (AC-9.1 through AC-9.4) are **not regressed**. The display-name change does not alter the widget kind, the supported families, the deep-link grammar, the timeline cadence, or the placeholder fallback. Existing snapshot baselines for `WidgetCard.Placeholder` and `WidgetCard.Small/Medium` with `heroImageURL: nil` continue to render identically (no diff). New baselines cover the populated-with-real-image case.
- **AC-21.6** L1 unit tests cover the image bridge: writing image bytes via the host store also writes the file to the App Group container; the filename is deterministic for a given URL; eviction removes both the SwiftData row and the file; reading a filename from a snapshot resolves to a valid `file://` URL inside the container; absent files fall back to nil. L4 snapshot baselines for the featured widget's small + medium populated states are re-recorded against the real-image render path (was: hero gradient; now: actual image bytes from the bridge under test fixtures).

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- The host-side App-Group file write happens inside `RecipeStore.cacheImage(...)` — bytes flow through the existing single-writer entry point so there's no second pathway to keep in sync.
- The widget extension makes **no** network calls in any of its code paths (still true with AC-21.3 — `file://` is a local read, not a network fetch).
- No new analytics events. The widget tap still fires `widgetOpened(kind: "featured", recipeID:)` per AC-17.9.
- Saved widget bridge consumption — T-322's `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher` left `heroImageFilename: nil` with an explicit follow-up note ("Future work: write image files to the App Group container on `preDownloadImages` and use that filename here. T-321 (widget extension) will need to read the file once it exists."). This story builds the bridge but **wires only the featured widget**. A follow-up entry in `tasks.md` covers the saved-widget consumption so both widgets eventually render real images — same bridge, two consumers.

### US-22 — Latest-recipe lock-screen widget
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** a Lock Screen widget that shows the latest Dutch Oven Daddy recipe,
**so that** I can see what just dropped without unlocking my phone, and one tap takes me straight to it.

Added post-Phase 9 (2026-05-24). Builds on US-9's snapshot infrastructure (`WidgetSnapshot`, `WidgetSnapshotStore`, `WidgetDeepLinkParser`, App Group container). This story adds a **third** widget alongside the existing `FeaturedRecipeWidget` (US-9, home-screen, hero-image-driven) and `SavedRecipesWidget` (US-17, home-screen, list-driven) — it does not replace either one. The lock-screen rendering pipeline is fundamentally different from the home-screen one (monochrome, text-only, much smaller surface area), so it gets its own `WidgetConfiguration` rather than extending an existing widget's `supportedFamilies` list. See CL-37 for why `.accessoryRectangular` is the only lock-screen family in scope.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-22.1** A "Latest Recipe" widget is available in the iOS widget gallery in the `.accessoryRectangular` family for the Lock Screen (and on iPadOS 17+ for the lock-screen-equivalent locked surface). `.accessoryCircular` and `.accessoryInline` are explicitly out of scope (CL-37). The widget is registered as a new kind in `DODAppWidgetBundle` after `FeaturedRecipeWidget` and `SavedRecipesWidget`; existing widget IDs are not renamed and existing user installs are unaffected. *Amended by CL-168 (T-771 / DUT-77, 2026-06-13): `.accessoryCircular` now ships — but as a SEPARATE static "Saved" shortcut widget (`SavedLockScreenWidget`, a `bookmark.fill` glyph → `dod://saved`), not as a circular rendering of the latest recipe (CL-37's "no good single-glyph recipe payload" reasoning still stands for the recipe; a bookmark shortcut is a different, valid single-glyph payload). `.accessoryInline` remains out of scope. See AC-22.7.*
- **AC-22.2** The widget renders **text only** — recipe title (1–2 lines, truncated cleanly) over a one-line short description (excerpt, truncated to fit). No image rendering, no chips, no decorative glyphs (`.widgetAccentable` is the styling lever for monochrome rendering; iOS controls the actual tint per Lock Screen wallpaper). The host app reuses the **same featured-widget snapshot** (`WidgetSnapshot`, US-9 `WidgetSnapshotStore`) that the home-screen widget reads — no new snapshot file, no new host-side observer, no new App Group key.
- **AC-22.3** Tap target on a populated widget is `dod://recipe/<id>` opening the recipe detail (reuses the existing `WidgetDeepLinkParser` from US-9 — no new parser case is added). Tap target on the empty-state placeholder is `dod://feed` opening the Feed tab. Both URLs are already locked by `WidgetDeepLinkParserTests`; no parser changes ship with this story.
- **AC-22.4** Empty state (no recipe cached yet — first launch, App Group unavailable in a non-provisioned build, or version mismatch) renders placeholder text — `"Dutch Oven Daddy"` headline + `"Open the app to see the latest recipe."` body — and tap-targets to `dod://feed`. The placeholder is also what WidgetKit hands the gallery preview so the widget renders nicely while the user is still browsing the lock-screen widget picker.
- **AC-22.5** L4 snapshot coverage exists for the new `.accessoryRectangular` layout in three states (populated, populated-with-long-title, empty) under the system-default `vibrant` rendering mode. Lock-screen widgets render monochrome — light + dark snapshot semantics do not differ the same way home-screen widgets do (the system handles the wallpaper-aware tinting at present-time, not at render-time). A single rendering pass per state is the L4 baseline; iOS gallery preview is verified manually on the iPhone 17 simulator. Lives in a new `LockScreenWidgetSnapshotTests.swift` file alongside the existing `SavedWidgetSnapshotTests.swift` to keep `SnapshotTests.swift` under SwiftLint's file-length cap.
- **AC-22.6** Lock-screen widget readability is verified across a representative Lock Screen wallpaper set (dark wallpaper, light wallpaper, photo wallpaper) — the system's wallpaper-aware tint pass is applied at present-time and cannot be snapshot-tested through environment injection, so the verification is manual. The widget face uses semantic colors only (`.primary` / `.secondary` from text-style defaults, no hardcoded `.white` / `.black` / hex literals on any rendered surface), and `.widgetAccentable()` is applied on the entry view so the system tint pass treats the rendered text as tintable content rather than chrome. Title uses `.lineLimit(2)` with default `.tail` truncation; excerpt uses `.lineLimit(1)`. The `containerBackground(for: .widget)` closure is `Color.clear` so the system's `AccessoryWidgetBackground` rendering pass handles the wallpaper-aware background. Verified by [T-396](tasks.md) / [CL-47](clarifications.md) parallel audit.
- **AC-22.7** A second Lock Screen widget — **"Saved Recipes"** (`SavedLockScreenWidget`) — is available in the `.accessoryCircular` family (CL-168 / T-771 / DUT-77). It is a **static shortcut**: a single `bookmark.fill` glyph (the design-system `WidgetCard.LockScreenCircularBookmark`) inside the system's `AccessoryWidgetBackground` disc, opted into the present-time monochrome tint via `.widgetAccentable()`. It carries no per-recipe data and reads no snapshot, so its `TimelineProvider` returns a single fixed entry with a `.never` refresh policy (no App Group read, no host-side observer). Tapping it opens the app to the **Saved tab** via `dod://saved` — the same deep link the home-screen `SavedRecipesWidget` chrome uses (US-17 / CL-29), already covered by `WidgetDeepLinkParser` (no new parser case). Registered as a fourth kind in `DODAppWidgetBundle` after the three existing widgets; existing widget IDs are unchanged. L4 coverage: `test_lockScreenWidget_circularBookmark` pins the glyph at the ~76×76 `.accessoryCircular` frame (the `AccessoryWidgetBackground` disc + the system tint are present-time chrome, not part of the snapshotted primitive); the `dod://saved` tap routing is the on-device human checkpoint.
### US-23 — Widget readability under iOS 18+ "Clear" and "Tinted" home-screen appearances
**As any** user who has applied a home-screen tint or Clear icon style in iOS 18+,
**I want** the Dutch Oven Daddy widgets to stay legible and on-brand under those appearances,
**so that** the widgets don't turn into a wash of unreadable shapes the moment I customize my home screen.

Added 2026-05-24 (post-Phase 10). iOS 18 introduced home-screen "Tinted" (user-picked accent color tints all icons and widgets) and "Clear" (translucent, luminance-based) icon/widget appearances on top of the existing Standard / Dark pair. The WidgetKit rendering pipeline passes the active appearance to the widget extension as a `WidgetRenderingMode` environment value (`.fullColor`, `.accented`, `.vibrant`) and the system applies a desaturation pass to any content that hasn't been explicitly opted-in or opted-out via `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(_:)`. Today the app's two home-screen widgets (US-9 / US-21 `FeaturedRecipeWidget`, US-17 `SavedRecipesWidget`) declare no rendering-mode preference and rely on a mix of brand asset-catalog colors + a handful of hardcoded `Color.black` / `Color.white` literals — so the audit's first job is to find out which surfaces actually fail and which already render correctly.

Framed per CL-39 as **audit + targeted fixes** — same shape as US-18 / CL-30. The audit document is the deliverable; any code change is incidental to what the audit surfaces. Constitution §7 already mandates WCAG AA contrast across appearance modes; this story extends that mandate to the iOS 18+ widget appearances surface-by-surface.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-23.1** Every home-screen widget kind × every supported size × every iOS 18+ widget appearance mode (Standard, Dark, Tinted, Vibrant) has a known render outcome documented in a new `specs/dod-ios-app/widget-appearance-audit.md` (sibling to `appearance-audit.md`). Surfaces covered: `FeaturedRecipeWidget` (small + medium + placeholder) and `SavedRecipesWidget` (small + medium + empty). Lock-screen / accessory families (US-22 / T-370, merged via PR #26) are explicitly out of scope for this story — lock-screen accessory widgets use a different system rendering pipeline (system-monochromatic vibrancy on the Lock Screen, not the home-screen Tinted/Clear pipeline this story audits). A parallel "lock-screen appearance" T-393 follow-up entry surfaced by the audit doc is the right framing if the lock-screen surface ever needs its own audit; this story's bounded scope keeps it focused on the home-screen pipeline alone.
- **AC-23.2** Any widget surface that fails the audit (text or key visual element becomes hard to read in Tinted or Vibrant mode) gets fixed in the implementing PR if the fix is bounded to widget-card-only code: hardcoded `Color.white` / `Color.black` literals replaced with system-semantic colors (`.primary`, `.secondary`, `Color(.systemBackground)`), and individual subviews annotated with `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.fullColor)` (e.g. the food-photo hero, where the brand experience is the real image) or `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.accented)` (e.g. the placeholder glyph + gradient composition, where the tint should sweep through coherently). Fixes that require new design tokens, a new asset catalog, or a snapshot-infra change beyond the existing iPhone-fixed-frame harness are deferred to T-391+ follow-up tasks. *Amended by CL-164 (T-767 / DUT-73, 2026-06-13): the widget **background** is now owned solely by `containerBackground(for: .widget)` — the inner `.background(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` that `WidgetCard.Medium` / `Placeholder` / `SavedSmall` / `SavedMedium` / `SavedEmpty` (and `SavedRecipesWidgetEntryView.mediumBody`) painted is removed. In `.accented` / `.vibrant` (Tinted/Clear) mode the system flattened that solid inner fill into a tint silhouette that hid the tinted container and broke the Apple-News look (tinted background + white text + full-color images). With the inner fill gone, the container background tints and the hero/thumbnail images keep `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.fullColor)`. Full-color (Standard) mode is visually unchanged — the container background is the same `surfaceElevated` color the inner fill used. The small Featured widget stays full-bleed photo. The L4 `WidgetCard` snapshot harness wraps each rendered component in `.background(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` to simulate the container (so Standard/dark baselines are byte-stable); the true Tinted/Clear appearance is the on-device human checkpoint per AC-23.7, since the system accent pass can't be reproduced in the snapshot host.*
- **AC-23.3** L4 snapshot baselines for the widget views in Tinted (Vibrant) rendering mode are added wherever the existing snapshot harness can host them. The `swift-snapshot-testing` 1.x environment-injection path supports `.environment(\.widgetRenderingMode, .accented)` on a SwiftUI host on iOS 18+, so the new tests are wrapped in `if #available(iOS 18, *)` and skip cleanly on iOS 17 runners. Existing Standard + Dark baselines (`test_widgetCard_*` in `SnapshotTests.swift` + `SnapshotTests+AppearanceAudit.swift`, `test_savedWidget_*` in `SavedWidgetSnapshotTests.swift`) are NOT re-recorded — the new tests are additive, mirroring the T-330 / T-360 baseline-extension pattern.
- **AC-23.4** Constitution §7 WCAG AA contrast is pinned across all four widget appearance modes for any surface that renders user-readable text (title, excerpt, eyebrow, placeholder copy). Where the audit cannot machine-verify contrast (PNG-pixel inspection isn't part of the snapshot harness), the audit doc routes the verification to the same Accessibility Inspector + human-review step `appearance-audit.md` already documents — that step is the human checkpoint, not a duplicate audit deliverable.
- **AC-23.5** US-9, US-17, US-21, US-22 existing acceptance criteria (AC-9.1..AC-9.4, AC-17.1..AC-17.9, AC-21.1..AC-21.6, AC-22.1..AC-22.5) are **not regressed**. The audit may surface code changes that touch `WidgetCard` source, but no rename, no snapshot wire-format change, no `widgetURL` grammar change, and no analytics event change is in scope here. Any fix must leave the existing committed baselines for Standard light + Standard dark identical (the modifier additions render byte-for-byte the same in Standard mode — `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.fullColor)` is a no-op outside Tinted/Vibrant per Apple's docs). US-22's lock-screen widget surfaces (`LatestRecipeLockScreenWidget`, `WidgetCard.AccessoryRectangular*`) are likewise untouched — the lock-screen rendering pipeline doesn't intersect this audit's scope.
- **AC-23.6** **A clean audit closes the story.** If the investigation surfaces no surfaces that fail under Tinted/Vibrant rendering — for example, if the existing brand asset-catalog colors already produce a legible monochromatic treatment under the system's automatic desaturation pass and the hardcoded literals turn out to live only on overlay layers that the system also tints correctly — then the audit document + the new Tinted-mode snapshot baselines (which exist purely as a regression net) ship as the complete deliverable, with no code changes to `WidgetCard*.swift`. This mirrors AC-18.6 / CL-30 explicitly. The deliverable is the audit document plus any fixes the audit prompted, not a guaranteed list of fixes.
- **AC-23.7** Text rendered over an image (e.g. recipe title over the `WidgetCard.Hero` photo, or any overlay glyph over the fallback gradient) must remain legible under `widgetRenderingMode == .accented` (Tinted + Clear) **without depending on the system's tint mapping for the foreground color literal**. Two-part requirement: (a) the overlay text must sit over an explicit dark scrim band (not just a gradient that fades to a hardcoded opacity-on-photo) whose opacity is calibrated to provide WCAG-AA-equivalent luminance contrast against a default-group flattening (since `containerBackground` is stripped in `.accented` mode, the scrim is the only contrast scaffold the foreground can rely on); (b) the overlay text must be opted into the accent group via `.widgetAccentable(true)` so it picks up the user's tint color rather than collapsing into the same default-group color plane as the scrim. Pin added by CL-46 / T-394 (Featured widget) and re-affirmed by CL-46 / T-395 (Saved widget audit verdict — clean by construction because the saved variants render text NEXT TO the `Hero` thumbnail, not OVER it, so no text-over-image surface exists in `WidgetCard+Saved.swift` today). The mandate is enforced by-construction on the saved-widget surfaces (`WidgetCard.SavedSmall`, `WidgetCard.SavedMedium`, `WidgetCard.SavedEmpty`, `WidgetCard.SavedListRow`) and re-applies to any future widget surface — featured or saved or otherwise — that introduces a text-or-glyph-over-image composition. Verifiable by spinning up the iPhone simulator, picking a bright wallpaper (Settings → Wallpaper → any light hue), tinting the widget (long-press home screen → Edit → tint controls), and confirming any overlay text on any widget face is legible. The Tinted/Vibrant baselines under `__Snapshots__/WidgetCardTintedAppearanceSnapshotTests/` are the regression net; the on-device verification is the human checkpoint constitution §7 mandates pre-TestFlight.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events. No new widget kinds. No change to the snapshot wire format (`WidgetSnapshot.Entry`, `SavedRecipesWidgetSnapshot.Entry`) or the deep-link grammar (`dod://recipe/<id>`, `dod://saved`, `dod://feed`).
- The lock-screen widget (US-22 / T-370, merged via PR #26) is text-only by design and uses the lock-screen accessory rendering pipeline (`accessoryRectangular` family), which renders in the system's monochrome accessory-vibrancy mode regardless of home-screen appearance. It does not participate in the home-screen Tinted/Clear pipeline this story audits; the audit document calls out a parallel "lock-screen appearance" T-393 follow-up entry as the right framing if any lock-screen surface ever needs its own audit.
- The `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(_:)` SwiftUI modifier and the `WidgetRenderingMode` environment value are both iOS 18+ APIs. The host app's deployment target is iOS 17 (constitution §2), so any modifier added by the implementing PR must be wrapped in `if #available(iOS 18, *)` (or, if the wrapper hoists cleanly into a `View` extension, the extension can gate internally). iOS 17 widgets continue to render exactly as they do today — the modifier is a no-op on the older runtime.

### US-24 — Categories tab brown surface to match recipe-card visual language
**As a** Recipe Hunter,
**I want** the Categories tab to use the same brand brown that anchors the recipe cards on the Recipes (Feed) tab,
**so that** moving between Feed and Categories feels like one continuous app surface instead of a system-grey Settings-style detour.

Added 2026-05-25 (post-Phase 10, round 4). T-340 / US-19 modernized the Categories list to `.insetGrouped` + `.searchable`, which adopted the iOS-stock grouped-background appearance (cream `DODColor.surface` derived from the system grouped background in light mode, near-black in dark). That brought the layout in line with current-iOS conventions but stranded the Categories surface as the only top-level tab whose background does not carry the brand palette. The Feed tab's recipe cards anchor the visual language with the cast-iron-brown time-chip overlay on every card; this story extends that brown onto the surface around the Categories list rows + the `.searchable` field's container area so the two tabs read as one continuous experience.

This is a **Categories-only surface-color pass** that amends T-340's layout choice. It touches no design tokens (`DODColor.castIronBrown` already exists per the original brand palette and is the same token the recipe-card time chip, the offline banner, the snackbar, and the search filter chip already use), no list-style choice (still `.insetGrouped`), no `.searchable` placement, and no row content composition. CL-44 captures the surface-vs-cell-level tinting decision and the considered alternative.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-24.1** ~~The `CategoryListView` loaded-state list applies `DODColor.castIronBrown` as its scroll-content surface via `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden) + .background(DODColor.castIronBrown)`. This paints the surface around the inset-grouped row cards (the area formerly rendered by the system grouped background) and the visual container area behind the `.searchable` field in the `.navigationBarDrawer` placement. The token is the same `castIronBrown` already used by `RecipeCard`'s time chip overlay (`Capsule().fill(DODColor.castIronBrown.opacity(0.85))` at `Components/RecipeCard.swift:104`), the `OfflineBanner`, the `Snackbar`, and the Search tab's selected filter chip — no new asset, no new token.~~ **Amended by CL-54 / T-560 (2026-05-26):** the `CategoryListView` loaded-state list paints its scroll surround with the default `DODColor.surface` token (post-T-520 contract — `#F9F6EF` light / `#42210B` dark) via `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden) + .background(DODColor.surface)` — the same pattern Feed (`FeedView.swift:26`), Saved (`SavedView.swift:18`), and Search (`SearchView.swift:25`) already use. The `.insetGrouped` row cells keep their system-default fill (`UIColor.secondarySystemGroupedBackground`) so row text contrast carries forward unchanged. The token swap (`castIronBrown` → `surface`) is the smallest diff that makes every top-level tab's backdrop match: the T-520 color overhaul re-tinted `surface` to the warm cream / dark brown palette the user actually wanted across the app, so the per-tab `castIronBrown` override CL-44 chose for the pre-T-520 world now produces visual *inconsistency* rather than cohesion. CL-54 documents the supersession of CL-44's decision. *Amended by CL-177 (T-781, 2026-06-15): with the `.insetGrouped` `List` replaced by a `ScrollView` + `categoryCard` (CL-177), the scroll surround stays `DODColor.surface`; the brand brown moves onto the card itself — `categoryCard` fills `DODColor.surfaceElevated` (the same row-cell brown from T-647 / CL-125) instead of the system grouped-background fill.*
- **AC-24.2** The inset-grouped row cards themselves are NOT tinted brown — they keep their system-default cell fill (white in light, dark earth in dark) so the row text (`DODColor.label`, system primary) retains its existing contrast and the established row-card affordance is preserved. The user-visible change is the surface AROUND the cards, not the cards. CL-44 documents this surface-vs-cell decision and the cell-level alternative that was considered.
- **AC-24.3** The system `.searchable` field stays readable in both light and dark mode. The field's chrome is a system-tinted glass material that iOS layers on top of the scroll surface; against the dark `castIronBrown` (`#3D2B1F` in both light and dark — the token does not vary by appearance per its colorset definition) the system raises the field's material to maintain contrast. No custom search-field styling is introduced; if the rendered baselines surface a contrast failure, a constitution §7 follow-up T-431 entry is the right framing per CL-44.
- **AC-24.4** US-2's existing acceptance criteria (AC-2.1 alphabetical order, AC-2.2 name + count, AC-2.3 tap → category recipes, AC-2.4 zero-count hidden, AC-2.5 error state with Retry) AND US-19's (AC-19.1 `.insetGrouped` list style, AC-19.2 row composition, AC-19.3 `.searchable` filter, AC-19.4 US-2 not regressed, AC-19.5 L4 snapshot coverage matrix, AC-19.6 accessibility) are **not regressed**. The implementing PR's existing `CategoriesTests` L1 unit tests (the `filtered(categories:matching:)` pure helper) pass against the unchanged filter logic; the snapshot baselines are re-recorded to reflect the new surface color and reviewed PNG-by-PNG before commit. The loading + error states (`ProgressView`, `EmptyState`) are NOT amended — only the loaded-state list surface picks up the brown.
- **AC-24.5** L4 snapshot baselines exist for the new surface treatment on the existing matrix the T-340 implementing PR established: iPhone 13 light + dark at default Dynamic Type, iPhone 13 light + dark at AX5, iPad 12.9" light + dark at default Dynamic Type (six PNGs total). All six baselines are re-recorded as part of this PR — the surface color affects every cell in every cell. The Categories *recipes* sub-screen (`CategoryRecipesView`) is out of scope for this story and its snapshot baselines are untouched.
- **AC-24.6** Accessibility per constitution §7 / CC-1: the existing `.accessibilityLabel("\(category.name), \(category.count) recipes")` per-row label is preserved; the `.searchable` field's default VoiceOver label ("Search") is preserved; row text contrast (`DODColor.label` on `surfaceElevated` cell) is preserved because the surface change does not paint the cells themselves. Reduce-Transparency is unaffected — `castIronBrown` is a solid color, not a material.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events. No new design tokens. No change to the `CategoryListViewModel` or any L1 unit test in `CategoriesTests`. The implementing PR's diff is bounded to `Packages/DODFeatureCategories/Sources/DODFeatureCategories/CategoryListView.swift` (one `.background(...)` modifier addition) plus the six re-recorded snapshot baselines.
- The `castIronBrown` token does not vary between light and dark mode (per its colorset definition: `#3D2B1F` in both appearances). This is intentional — the brand brown is meant to read as the same brown regardless of system appearance. The surrounding system materials (the inset-grouped cell fill, the `.searchable` field's glass background) DO vary by appearance, so the brown reads as a darker accent in light mode (against white cells) and as a same-tone-of-dark surround in dark mode (against the dark-earth cells).
- This story explicitly amends T-340 / CL-32's "lean into the system style, not invent" framing on one axis (the surface color is now a brand override instead of the system grouped background) and preserves it on every other axis (list style, row composition, search field, chevron, separators). CL-44 explains why the surface color is the right place to introduce the brand override and why the cell fill is the wrong place.

### US-25 — Saved Recipes widget gallery description reads naturally
**As a** Returning Reader browsing the iOS widget gallery,
**I want** the Saved Recipes widget's gallery description to read like natural iOS-stock widget copy,
**so that** I don't second-guess what the widget does when I'm picking it from a list of candidates.

Added 2026-05-25 (post-Phase 10). The original `.configurationDescription(_:)` string set when US-17 / AC-17.1 shipped — "Your saved recipes, one tap from a cook." — reads awkwardly in the widget gallery. The "one tap from a cook" construction tries to be cute but parses oddly ("a cook" reads as a noun-with-article rather than the intended verb), and the iOS-stock widget-gallery copy convention (compare Apple's stock Reminders, Photos, Notes widget descriptions) leans on short, direct, action-noun phrasing. This story rewrites the string to a natural-reading alternative. CL-40 captures the choice + alternatives considered. **Pure user-facing string change** — no schema bump, no behavior change, no snapshot wire-format change. The L4 widget-face baselines (`SavedWidgetSnapshotTests`) are unaffected because the description string lives in the iOS widget gallery UI, not on the rendered widget face itself.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-25.1** `SavedRecipesWidget.description(_:)` in `Widget/SavedRecipesWidget.swift` is rewritten to "Quick access to your saved recipes." (per CL-40). The `.configurationDisplayName("Saved Recipes")` line, the `Self.kind` identifier, the `containerBackground` treatment, the `.supportedFamilies([.systemSmall, .systemMedium])` declaration, and the `.contentMarginsDisabled()` modifier are all unchanged — the only edit in `SavedRecipesWidget.swift` is the description string literal.
- **AC-25.2** US-17's existing acceptance criteria (AC-17.1 widget identity — now amended; AC-17.2 supported sizes, AC-17.3 snapshot wire format, AC-17.4 deep-link routing, AC-17.5 empty state, AC-17.6 reload contract, AC-17.7 L4 snapshot coverage, AC-17.8 L1 unit coverage, AC-17.9 telemetry) are **not regressed**. The implementing PR touches only the description string literal; no view-model, snapshot writer, deep-link parser, timeline provider, or analytics code is changed.
- **AC-25.3** No new snapshot baselines are recorded by this story — the widget-face render (the `SavedRecipesWidgetEntryView` composition that `SavedWidgetSnapshotTests` pins) does not include the gallery description, and the description string is rendered exclusively by iOS in the widget picker (a system-managed surface that is not part of our snapshot harness). Existing `SavedWidgetSnapshotTests` baselines continue to render byte-for-byte identically against the new string. AC-17.1's amendment is verified by source inspection (the literal in `SavedRecipesWidget.swift`) and by manual sim-gallery verification on iPhone simulator before merge.

### US-26 — Recipe detail action affordances are not duplicated
**As a** reader of a recipe,
**I want** the Save and Share affordances to live in a single, predictable place, and the rating composer's primary action to sit where my thumb expects it,
**so that** the bottom-right of the screen isn't cluttered with a sticky duplicate of the nav-bar buttons, and submitting a comment + rating reads as a normal iOS sheet form.

Added 2026-05-25 (post-Phase 10). This story is a **two-part polish** that amends two earlier decisions:

1. **Removes the sticky `RecipeDetailFloatingActions` row.** T-302 (Phase 6 recipe-detail polish) introduced a floating Save + Share button stack anchored to the bottom-trailing of `RecipeDetailView` so the affordances stayed reachable while scrolling. CL-42 captures the amendment: the nav-bar Save (AC-4.7, now bookmark glyph per T-380 / CL-38) + Share (AC-4.8) are sufficient on their own, and the sticky duplicate clutters the bottom of the screen without earning its space. The two affordances already exist exactly once in the nav bar; the bottom-trailing stack is a second copy of the same action and reads as visual noise next to the scrollable content (ingredients, instructions, related, comments).
2. **Swaps the Submit/Cancel button positions in `CommentComposer`.** The pre-T-410 layout (`HStack { Cancel; Spacer(); Submit }`) followed the iPhone-stock HIG convention (Cancel leading, primary trailing — like Mail's compose sheet). The user's backlog ask was literal: "Swap 'Submit' and 'Cancel' button positions." CL-41 documents the deviation: the swap honors the user's literal request and renders `HStack { Submit; Spacer(); Cancel }` so Submit sits at the leading edge and Cancel sits at the trailing edge. The cancel role (`role: .cancel`) is preserved on the Cancel button so VoiceOver + the system back-gesture still treat it as the cancel affordance — the swap is purely visual. CL-41 walks through alternatives (keep HIG-compliant ordering and only tighten spacing; render as `ToolbarItem` pair; match `confirmationDialog`'s implicit ordering) and explains why the literal swap is the chosen path.

US-4 (recipe detail), US-13 (rating), US-14 (comments) acceptance criteria are **not regressed** — AC-26.4 explicitly pins them.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-26.1** Save + Share affordances exist exactly once on `RecipeDetailView`: in the navigation bar, per the existing AC-4.7 (Save, bookmark glyph) + AC-4.8 (Share, native share sheet). No additional duplicate of either action is rendered elsewhere on the screen.
- **AC-26.2** The `RecipeDetailFloatingActions` sticky bottom-trailing button stack added by T-302 is removed. `RecipeDetailFloatingActions.swift` is deleted from `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/`, and its call site in `RecipeDetailView.body`'s `.overlay(alignment: .bottomTrailing)` modifier is removed. The `floatingActionsOverlay` private property is removed from `RecipeDetailView`.
- **AC-26.3** In `CommentComposer.actions`, the Submit button (primary, `.borderedProminent` tint accent) sits at the **leading** edge of the actions row and the Cancel button (`.bordered`, `role: .cancel`) sits at the **trailing** edge — i.e. the visual positions of the two buttons are swapped relative to the pre-T-410 layout (which had Cancel leading + Submit trailing per the iPhone HIG default). The button styles, accessibility labels (`onSubmit` / `onCancel` callbacks, the `role: .cancel` semantic that VoiceOver and the system back-gesture still honor), `isSubmitting` disabled state, "Submitting…" label swap, and `canSubmit` gate are byte-for-byte preserved. See CL-41 for the rationale: the swap honors the user's literal "swap Submit and Cancel positions" request and is documented as a deliberate departure from the iPhone-stock pattern Mail / Reminders / Calendar compose sheets follow.
- **AC-26.4** US-4 acceptance criteria (AC-4.7 nav-bar Save, AC-4.8 nav-bar Share, AC-4.9 offline-aware, AC-4.10 VoiceOver), US-13 acceptance criteria (AC-13.1..AC-13.5 rating summary + input + submit + telemetry), US-14 acceptance criteria (AC-14.1..AC-14.7 comments list + composer + telemetry), and US-7 (AC-7.1 Cook Now CTA stays visible inside the recipe detail scroll content) are **not regressed**. The implementing PR's existing `RecipeDetailViewModelTests`, `RecipeDetailRatingsRenderingTests`, `RecipeDetailRatingsViewSnapshotTests`, and `CommentComposer` snapshot tests stay green; baselines are re-recorded against the new layout per AC-26.5.
- **AC-26.5** L4 snapshot coverage is re-recorded for the surfaces this story changes: (a) `CommentComposer` empty + filled + filled-dark baselines in DesignSystem (the actions row layout changed); (b) all three `RecipeDetailRatingsSection` baselines in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail` (`test_section_emptyState_renders`, `test_section_loadedState_renders`, `test_section_identityGated_rendersComposer`) — the inline composer's button row sits at the bottom edge of every section snapshot and re-records on every snapshot whose `sizeThatFits` frame catches it. The `RecipeDetailView` itself does not have a direct L4 snapshot baseline in the repo today; the AC-26.2 removal is verified by the build + the AC-26.4 regression suite. New baselines for the recipe detail screen without the sticky bar are out of scope for this PR — adding a `RecipeDetailViewSnapshotTests` is a separate follow-up if the screen-level coverage is ever wanted (it would belong in `App/Tests/` per the T-332 / T-335 precedent, not in the feature package).

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events. No new widget kinds. No deep-link grammar changes. No snapshot wire-format changes.
- The Save + Share data path (`viewModel.toggleSaved`, `viewModel.didShare`, `viewModel.canonicalURL`, `.sensoryFeedback(.success, trigger: viewModel.isSaved)`) is unchanged — only the second-copy UI is removed.
- The `CommentComposer` is the only composer surface with both a Cancel and a Submit button. The inline `userRatingBar` Submit-only button in `RecipeDetailRatingsSection` has no Cancel pair (the user can simply not tap Submit) and is untouched by AC-26.3.

### US-30 — App-wide background + foreground color overhaul
**As a** Returning Reader using the app in both light and dark mode,
**I want** the screen background and card surfaces to match the brand's updated palette (warm cream / pure white in light mode, deep brown / near-black brown in dark mode),
**so that** the app reads cohesively against Dutch Oven Daddy's brand and the dark mode is visibly deeper without sacrificing text legibility.

Added 2026-05-26 (round-6 backlog item 9). This story is an **app-wide background + foreground tone re-tint** that maps user-specified hex values to the existing `DODColor.surface` (screen-wide backdrop) and `DODColor.surfaceElevated` (cards / sheets above surface) semantic tokens. CL-51 captures the mapping (the existing palette has no token literally named "background" or "foreground" — the closest analogues are `Surface` and `SurfaceElevated`, whose doc-comments in `Colors.swift` already pin the semantic role) plus the WCAG AA contrast verification against the unchanged text tokens. Text colors (`DODColor.label`, `DODColor.labelSecondary`) and brand accents (`CastIronBrown`, `BurntOrange`, `WarmGold`, `Accent`, `Charcoal`, `DarkEarth`) are explicitly NOT touched — the overhaul is bounded to the two surface tokens. **Asset catalog only:** no Swift source edits. **L4 baselines re-recorded:** every snapshot baseline whose rendered surface includes `Surface` or `SurfaceElevated` is re-recorded — that's most of the DesignSystem suite, the feature snapshot tests, and the widget appearance suite. Text-only or icon-only baselines (lock-screen widget rectangular populated / empty, icon glyphs without a surface backdrop) are byte-identical and not re-recorded. `accessibility-audit.md` contrast table updated with the new hex values.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-30.1** `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Resources/Colors.xcassets/Surface.colorset/Contents.json` Any-Appearance entry uses `#F9F6EF` (R `0xF9` / G `0xF6` / B `0xEF`, alpha `1.000`, sRGB) and Dark-Appearance entry uses `#42210B` (R `0x42` / G `0x21` / B `0x0B`, alpha `1.000`, sRGB). Both entries preserve `idiom = "universal"` and the same JSON shape the asset catalog already used.
- **AC-30.2** `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Resources/Colors.xcassets/SurfaceElevated.colorset/Contents.json` Any-Appearance entry uses `#FFFFFF` (R `0xFF` / G `0xFF` / B `0xFF`, alpha `1.000`, sRGB) and Dark-Appearance entry uses `#553724` (R `0x55` / G `0x37` / B `0x24`, alpha `1.000`, sRGB) (amended by CL-55 / T-570 — lifted the original `#281F19` ~8% in brightness to `#5A3520` so the elevated surface visibly rises above the `#42210B` Surface background per Apple's dark-mode "card rises above background" depth pattern; further amended by CL-59 / T-610 — Spencer's round-7 spec calls for `#553724` on list cells + `.searchable` field backgrounds specifically, and because `SurfaceElevated` is the token those surfaces consume, the dark hex moves the remaining ~3 brightness points (`#5A3520` → `#553724`) at the token level rather than introducing a dedicated `CellSurface` token; the ~3-point shift is barely perceptible on other consumers (chips, cards, sheets), so a single token tweak keeps the design system simple and the change scope minimal; WCAG AA verified against unchanged `Label` dark text). Same shape constraints as AC-30.1.
- **AC-30.3** No edits land outside the two asset catalogs. Specifically: `Label.colorset`, `LabelSecondary.colorset`, `Charcoal.colorset`, `BurntOrange.colorset`, `CastIronBrown.colorset`, `WarmGold.colorset`, `Accent.colorset`, `DarkEarth.colorset`, `Cream.colorset`, and `CreamSubtle.colorset` are byte-identical pre- and post-T-520. The `Colors.swift` enum is unchanged (no new tokens, no renames). No source-level `DODColor` usage is rewritten — every call site that already reads `surface` / `surfaceElevated` automatically picks up the new hex values via the asset catalog.
- **AC-30.4** WCAG AA contrast is verified against the unchanged text tokens — both with the new dark-mode background (`#42210B`) and the new dark-mode foreground (`#281F19`). Specifically: `Label` dark (`#E6DECF`) on Surface dark contrast ratio ≥ 4.5:1, `Label` dark on SurfaceElevated dark ratio ≥ 4.5:1, `LabelSecondary` dark (`#A8A39A`) on Surface dark ratio ≥ 4.5:1, `LabelSecondary` dark on SurfaceElevated dark ratio ≥ 4.5:1. Light-mode pairs trivially pass against `#F9F6EF` / `#FFFFFF` (off-white / pure white). The `accessibility-audit.md` contrast table is updated with the new hex values in the same PR so the audit file stays in sync (constitution §6, §11).
- **AC-30.5** Every L4 snapshot baseline whose rendered surface includes the `Surface` or `SurfaceElevated` token is re-recorded as part of the implementing PR. The reviewer spot-checks a representative cross-section of re-recorded PNGs (≥ 5, covering DesignSystem components, a feature view, and a widget face) to confirm the new colors render as expected before commit. Text-only or icon-only baselines that do NOT render either surface token (e.g. `LockScreenWidgetSnapshotTests` rectangular populated / empty — the lock-screen pipeline uses `Color.clear` as `containerBackground` and defers to `AccessoryWidgetBackground`) are byte-identical and not re-recorded.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events. No new widget kinds. No deep-link grammar changes. No snapshot wire-format changes. No `DODColor` token additions or removals (per AC-30.3). No `Colors.swift` source edits.
- `Cream` and `CreamSubtle` are NOT touched even though their names suggest a background role — in this codebase `Cream` is the foreground text color used on dark brand surfaces (CastIronBrown buttons, Snackbar, OfflineBanner, RecipeCard time chip), not a screen background. Re-tinting `Cream` would break those text-on-brand-dark overlays. CL-51 walks through the mapping decision.
- `Surface` is the screen-wide backdrop in this codebase (per `Colors.swift`'s doc-comment "Default screen background") and `SurfaceElevated` is the card/sheet surface above it (per `Colors.swift`'s doc-comment "Card / sheet surface above ``surface``"). The user's "background" / "foreground" intent maps cleanly to these two tokens.

### US-29 — Search-tab polish bundle
**As a** Recipe Hunter using the Search tab,
**I want** the Search tab's idle state and no-results state to behave the way I expect — "Try" suggestions populate the search field, "Recent" has a one-tap wipe, the no-results glyph reads as "not found," and the redundant "All categories" jump is removed,
**so that** Search reads as a focused find-something surface instead of a half-categorized detour.

Added 2026-05-26 (post-Phase 10, round 6). Bundles five small Search-tab polish items the user flagged in round-6 backlog. The bundle is one PR because each item touches `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchView.swift` (or its `RecentSearches` helper) and the snapshot baseline churn is shared — splitting would re-record the same `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` artifacts five times. CL-49 captures the five decisions, including the AC-20.3 reversal (the no-results glyph is changed after all) and the tag-search "no recipes match" root cause (it isn't a tag-search feature — there's no tag taxonomy in code per CL-3; the misread came from the "Try" category pill setting both a category filter AND a query, which caused the filter to drop every newly-fetched REST result whose category map hadn't been hydrated yet from the local cache).

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-29.1** In `IdleSuggestionsView`'s "Try" section, tapping a category pill calls `viewModel.setSearchText(_:)` (or equivalent — the `SearchViewModel.query` setter is the existing path) with the category's `name`, and does **not** set `viewModel.filters.categoryID`. The pill's text, accessibility label (`"\(text), suggestion"`), and surface-elevated capsule are unchanged. Behavior: tapping "Beef" populates the search field with "Beef" and runs a normal text search through the existing debounce path, the same as if the user had typed the word — the filter chip row's "All categories" stays selected, and the result set is whatever REST returns for the query. This implicitly fixes the round-6 backlog "tag search returns no results" report (see CL-49 for the root-cause analysis: the pre-fix path set a category filter against a not-yet-hydrated `categoryIDsByRecipe` map, which dropped every REST result because the filter requires an explicit category-ID match). *(Amended in T-637 / CL-106: the "Latest Recipes" pill — a real WP category, id 1590, slug `latest-recipes`, surfaced in `topCategorySuggestions` via the count-based top-5 — is special-cased. Case-insensitive name match against "Latest Recipes" (or id match against 1590) routes the tap to a new `SearchViewModel.surfaceLatestRecipes(limit:)` async method that fetches the N most-recent posts via `dependencies.fetchLatestRecipes(limit:)` (default `limit: 5`, fetched as `ceil(limit * 1.5)` to over-fetch for the article-drop pass), instead of running `client.search(query: "Latest Recipes")` which returned garbage (the literal text is a generic English phrase that matches the boilerplate of unrelated article posts). The Latest-Recipes fetch does NOT persist a recent search and does NOT send a search-telemetry hash — mirrors the REG-19 / CL-66 / T-670 curated-tap exclusion intent.)* *(Amended in T-639 / CL-117: the Try section's pill source is now `viewModel.displayedTrySlate` (not `topCategorySuggestions`), a per-cold-launch shuffled slate held lazily on the viewmodel — stable across view re-creates within a session. Visible count is a fixed constant of **6** (bumped from today's 5: one pinned + five shuffled). "Latest Recipes" is **always the first pill** — filtered out of the rotation pool, shuffled remainder, then prepended unconditionally so the canonical "what's new" affordance never disappears between launches. The per-pill tap-handler routing in `SearchView.onCategoryTap` is unchanged — Latest Recipes still fires `surfaceLatestRecipes()` via the same id-1590 / case-insensitive-name discriminator. See new AC-29.7 for the rotation contract.)* *(Amended in T-642 / CL-120: the "result set is whatever REST returns for the query" clause is **strengthened** by the new title-precision contract — the surfaced result set is now whatever REST returns **filtered to titles that contain the query** (or a plural/Levenshtein-1 variant). Tapping the "Beef" pill returns only posts whose titles contain "Beef" / "Beefs" / a one-edit-distance neighbor — body-only matches are dropped client-side, so the "tap a pill, see related recipes" promise reads tightly (the user sees "Beef Skillet Quick" but never "Cast Iron Bacon Wrapped Pickles" which happens to mention beef in its body). See REG-29 + the AC-12.1 amendment for the full filter contract.)* *(Amended in T-643 / CL-121: tapping a category pill whose name matches a known WP category (e.g. "Dessert Recipes", "Beef and Red Meat Recipes", "Side Dish Recipes") now ALSO fans out a parallel `?categories=<id>&per_page=100` fetch and unions those posts into the result set on top of the title-match path — so the user sees both the title-bearing matches AND every recipe in the named category. The pill-tap path itself is unchanged (still `selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)` → `viewModel.query = name` → debounced `performSearch()`); the merge happens one layer down where Path A (title) + Path B (category) union via `async let`. The "Latest Recipes" pill keeps its T-637 special case — it routes through `surfaceLatestRecipes(...)` which never enters `performSearch()` and never fires Path B. Restores the "tap Dessert Recipes, see ~53 desserts" navigation flow T-642 inadvertently broke. See REG-30 + the AC-12.1 amendment for the full merge contract.)*
- **AC-29.2** The idle-state `IdleSuggestionsView` "Recent" section header is rendered as a row with the "Recent" label at the leading edge and a "Clear All" button at the trailing edge. Tapping "Clear All" invokes `viewModel.clearRecentSearches()`, which calls the existing `RecentSearches.clear()` method (wipes the `dod.recentSearchesV1` `UserDefaults` key per the existing `RecentSearches.clear()` implementation) and updates `SearchViewModel.recentSearches` to an empty array on the next observation tick. The button has accessibility label `"Clear all recent searches"`. The button only renders when the Recent section is visible (i.e. recents is non-empty); when there are no recents, the section header is not rendered at all (consistent with the existing conditional in `IdleSuggestionsView`).
- **AC-29.3** The `.noResults` `EmptyState` (`SearchView.swift` line ~74) renders the `questionmark.circle` SF Symbol instead of `questionmark.folder`. Title (`"No recipes match '\(viewModel.query)'"`), message (`"Try a different word or clear a filter."`), and surrounding state-machine logic are unchanged. CL-49 captures the explicit reversal of AC-20.3's `questionmark.folder` carve-out (`questionmark.circle` is the iOS-stock "I can't find what you asked for" glyph — Settings' search, Mail's search, Notes' search all use it). *(Amended in T-649 / CL-127: when the merged result set settles with fewer than 3 items — either `.results` with 1–2 hits OR `.noResults` (0 hits) — AND the viewmodel's `SearchSuggestionEngine` returned a non-nil suggestion, a tappable "Did you mean: <suggestion>" banner renders **above** the empty-state glyph (or above the sparse result rows in the `.results` case). The banner uses `DODColor.accent` with an underline so it reads as a one-tap rescue affordance; tap → `viewModel.applyDidYouMean()` rewrites `query` and re-runs through the existing debounce path. The banner is gated on `state == .results || state == .noResults` so it never flashes during the `.searching` transition. The empty-state copy and glyph are unchanged when the banner appears — both surfaces co-exist, the banner being the soft escape hatch above the "no match" hard signal. See AC-12.1 amendment + CL-127 for the suggestion-engine contract.)*
- **AC-29.4** ~~The "All categories" affordance is removed from the Search tab. The pre-T-500 `FilterChipRow.categoryChip` `Menu` content rendered a `Button("All categories") { filters.categoryID = nil }` as the first menu item. After T-500, the user clears the category filter by either (a) re-tapping the currently-selected category in the menu to deselect it (no in-place behavior change for that path — the menu still lists every category), or (b) navigating to the Categories tab via the bottom-nav (the existing top-level affordance for browsing categories). The `Button("All categories")` first-row entry inside the `Menu` is deleted; the loop that renders `Button(category.name) { filters.categoryID = category.id }` for each `category` in `categories` remains unchanged. The chip's label still reads "All categories" via `selectedCategoryName` when no category is picked — that's the label text on the chip itself, not the menu's first row, and is preserved so the chip remains self-describing.~~ *(Superseded in T-636 / CL-105: the entire `FilterChipRow.categoryChip` is now removed — the Categories tab in the bottom nav is the only "browse by category" affordance for Search; `filters.categoryID` is retained on the model defaulted to `nil` for forward compatibility but no UI mutates it. The menu-row-removal sub-decision this AC documented is now moot because the menu itself is gone.)*
- **AC-29.5** US-3 acceptance criteria (AC-3.1..AC-3.7 — debounce, empty state, telemetry, offline) and US-12 acceptance criteria (AC-12.1..AC-12.6 — ingredient index, filter chips, recent history, suggestions, local-pass budget, telemetry contract) are **not regressed**. The implementing PR's existing `SearchViewModelTests`, `SearchFiltersTests`, `SearchResultMergerTests`, and `RecentSearchesTests` suites stay green. New L1 coverage: a test asserting `viewModel.clearRecentSearches()` empties both the in-memory `recentSearches` array and the underlying `RecentSearches` store (mirrors the existing `clearRemovesAll` test in `RecentSearchesTests`). Snapshot churn: `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests`'s four baselines re-record because the menu lost its "All categories" first row (the chip itself doesn't change visually in the closed state, but the menu's expanded content is part of the chip's iconography lock — verify by re-running the test suite); if all four PNGs come out byte-identical (the menu-content delta sits outside the captured chip frame), no re-record is needed.
- **AC-29.6** L4 snapshot review is performed for any `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` or `SearchViewSnapshotTests` baseline whose pixel output drifts from this PR. Reviewer pages through each re-recorded PNG visually before commit to confirm the changes match the intent (glyph swap reads as expected; "Clear All" button renders inside the section header without crowding; no "All categories" stray row inside the chip's menu).
- **AC-29.7** *(Added in T-639 / CL-117; amended in T-640 / CL-118 — see the "cache rule" sentence; amended in T-641 / CL-119 — see the "exclusion set" sentence)* The Search-tab idle "Try" section renders a **rotating slate** of **exactly 6** category pills. The slate is computed on first access of `SearchViewModel.displayedTrySlate` via `pickTrySlate(from: topTrySlatePool, visibleCount: 6, using: SystemRandomNumberGenerator())` and **cached only when it reaches the full visible count** of `Self.trySlateVisibleCount` (= 6); subsequent reads return the same cached slate within a single viewmodel lifetime (stable across `IdleSuggestionsView` re-creates / tab switches / navigation). The `topTrySlatePool` accessor applies an **exclusion-set filter** at the pool boundary — `availableCategories.filter { !Self.excludedTryPoolSlugs.contains($0.slug.lowercased()) }` — so junk WP categories that don't produce a useful exploratory search are dropped before the shuffle. The set holds canonical lowercase WP slugs (the slug is byte-stable across category renames, the visible `name` is editor-mutable, and the `id` is per-install) and is extensible — add a new slug to remove a future junk category from the rotation in one place. Today the set is `["uncategorized"]` (the live WP REST returned 28 categories on 2026-05-30 with Uncategorized at the bottom; the filter shrinks the pool from 28 → 27, comfortably wide for the 5-shuffle). The "only-cache-full-slate" rule is the T-640 / CL-118 amendment that closes the cold-start cache race: the view appears before `loadCategoriesIfNeeded()` resolves, so the first read can fire while `availableCategories` is still empty → `pickTrySlate(...)` returns the single synthesized `[Latest Recipes]` pill. Without the guard that partial slate would cache forever and lock the user to one pill for the session; with the guard, the partial slate is returned without being cached and SwiftUI's `@Observable` triggers a re-render on the `availableCategories` mutation → `IdleSuggestionsView` re-reads `displayedTrySlate` → cache miss → the full N-pill slate computes and caches. Once cached at the full count, the stable-within-session contract holds exactly as before (the shuffle does not re-fire on subsequent reads). The rotation source pool is `SearchViewModel.topTrySlatePool` — the top-30 WP categories by recipe count (widened from the existing `topCategorySuggestions` top-5). The Latest-Recipes category (id 1590, name `"Latest Recipes"`) is **always the first pill**: filtered out of the source pool before shuffling, the remaining 5 slots filled from `pool.shuffled().prefix(5)`, then Latest-Recipes prepended. If the source pool does not contain Latest-Recipes (REST anomaly), the helper synthesizes `Category(id: 1590, name: "Latest Recipes", slug: "latest-recipes", count: 0)` in-line so the pin contract holds unconditionally. If the source pool returns fewer than 5 non-pinned items, the helper tops up by **deterministic repetition** of whatever the pool produced (each repeated pill still fires the correct per-category tap handler) — never renders a short row. If the pool is empty, the slate is `[latestRecipes]` only (one pill) — degrades to the most-valuable affordance rather than an empty section. The shuffle uses Swift's system RNG which is per-process-seeded by the kernel — different on every cold launch with no extra wiring. The pure helper `static func pickTrySlate(from pool: [DODDomain.Category], visibleCount: Int, using rng: inout RandomNumberGenerator) -> [DODDomain.Category]` is exposed on `SearchViewModel` for unit-test seamability (an injectable `RandomNumberGenerator` lets tests pin "same seed + same pool = same slate" determinism without `Array.shuffled()`'s system-RNG dependency). The per-pill `dod.search.tryPill.latestRecipes` accessibility identifier (T-638 / CL-107) still resolves on the pinned pill because the `isLatestRecipesCategory(_:)` check in `IdleSuggestionsView` is unchanged. The `topCategorySuggestions` public property is preserved on the viewmodel for backward compatibility but is no longer consumed by `IdleSuggestionsView`.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events. No new design tokens. No new deep-link cases. No snapshot wire-format changes. The diff is bounded to `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchView.swift`, `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchViewModel.swift` (one new public method `clearRecentSearches()`), and the re-recorded `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` baselines if their PNGs drift.
- The round-6 backlog "tag search 'No recipes match'" report is fixed transitively by AC-29.1 (the "Try" pill no longer sets a category filter, which was the root cause). There is no separate tag-search feature to fix or remove — the WordPress data source surfaces only categories in v1 per CL-3, and the codebase contains zero references to a `tag` taxonomy outside of the `tag.fill` SF Symbol used for category chips. CL-49 documents the root cause + the misread.
- AC-20.3's "the empty-state `questionmark.folder` glyph stays" carve-out is explicitly reversed by AC-29.3. The carve-out's rationale at the time was that the `?` carried the meaning rather than the folder shape, so swapping the folder to a circle was unimportant; the round-6 user report contradicted that — users in fact read `questionmark.folder` as "this is in some folder I haven't found" rather than as "this isn't anywhere." `questionmark.circle` reads more cleanly as "not found, period."

### US-31 — Recipe detail supports per-render serving-count scaling
**As a** Returning Reader cooking for more or fewer people than the recipe was written for,
**I want** to tap a stepper near the recipe's "Serves 4" line and watch the ingredient quantities re-render with cook-friendly fractions (½ cup × 1.5 → ¾ cup, not 0.75),
**so that** I don't have to do the math by hand at the counter when scaling a 4-serving recipe up to 8 or down to 2.

Added 2026-05-26 (round-3 backlog item, graduated). Pure presentation logic — the source `Recipe` model is untouched; the multiplied quantities live only in the rendered ingredient row text. CL-52 captures the fraction-rendering precision (eighth-cup canonical set: `{1/8, 1/4, 1/3, 1/2, 2/3, 3/4, 7/8}` + whole numbers), the snap tolerance (1/16 = 0.0625 — the cook-grade half-distance between 1/8 increments), the warning-threshold rationale (>12 servings on a typical 5-quart home dutch oven), and the call-site decision (a single `FractionRenderer` utility in `DODSupport` that the recipe-detail view and Cook Mode drawer both consume so the displayed quantities agree). Source `RecipeIngredient.text` is a single free-text field (e.g. `"½ cup flour"` or `"1 1/2 pounds cod fillets"`) with no separately-typed amount/unit fields — the renderer parses the leading quantity, multiplies, and re-renders in place. Lines without a leading quantity (e.g. `"Salt and freshly ground black pepper (to taste)"`) pass through verbatim. No new analytics events, no new persistence schema, no new wire-format fields.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-31.1** `RecipeDetailView`'s ready body renders a `RecipeServingsScaler` row immediately under the `RecipeDetailMetaPills` (the meta row that already carries prep / cook / total time and the source servings count per AC-4.1). The row shows a person glyph, the literal text `"Serves \(userServings)"` as the primary label, the source `"Recipe makes N."` as a secondary line when `userServings != sourceServings`, and a SwiftUI `Stepper` control bound to `userServings`. The stepper's `−` / `+` controls advance by 1 per tap.
- **AC-31.2** The stepper accepts integer values in the closed range `1...24`. Tapping `−` at 1 or `+` at 24 is a no-op (SwiftUI `Stepper` clamps by construction). The view model's `setUserServings(_:)` clamps any out-of-range argument to the same bounds, so a programmatic set never leaks an invalid value.
- **AC-31.3** Default value = the recipe's source servings count, sourced from the JSON-LD `recipeYield` field (per AC-4.11) and surfaced on `Recipe.servings`. When the recipe detail finishes loading (i.e. `loadState` reaches `.ready`), `RecipeDetailViewModel.resetServingsToSourceIfFirstLoad()` syncs `userServings` to `sourceServings` if the user has not yet manually changed it. A `recipeYield`-less recipe (rare — `Recipe.servings == nil` after a parse) falls back to `RecipeDetailViewModel.defaultServings = 4` so the stepper always has a defined starting value.
- **AC-31.4** Ingredient rows render with their leading quantity multiplied by `userServings / sourceServings` (the view model's `servingsScaleFactor`). The multiplication happens at render time inside the `RecipeDetailView.ingredientsSection` `ForEach` body by calling `FractionRenderer.scale(ingredient.text, by: factor)` and passing the result as `IngredientCheckRow.displayText`. The same `displayText` shim is reused by the Cook Mode ingredients drawer in `CookModeView.ingredientsDrawer` (the host passes `viewModel.servingsScaleFactor` as `ingredientScaleFactor` to `CookModeView.init`), so the two surfaces never disagree on what a serving is.
- **AC-31.5** Quantities re-render with cook-friendly precision per CL-52: integers render as integers (`"1 tablespoon salt"` × 2 = `"2 tablespoon salt"`, not `"2.0 tablespoon salt"`); pure fractions render as their Unicode vulgar glyph (`"½ cup flour"` × 1.5 = `"¾ cup flour"`); mixed numbers render as `"<whole> <fraction>"` (`"2 ½ cups milk"` × 2 = `"5 cups milk"`, dropping the now-zero fraction); decimal sources snap to the canonical fraction set (`"1.5 lbs cod"` × 2 = `"3 lbs cod"`). The eighth-cup canonical set is `{1/8, 1/4, 1/3, 1/2, 2/3, 3/4, 7/8}` + whole numbers; the snap tolerance is 1/16 (= 0.0625), so any remainder within that band of a canonical value rounds to it. Sub-tolerance remainders with a non-zero whole render whole-only ("1.04 cups" → "1 cups"). The 12+ golden L1 cases in `FractionRendererTests` pin the table per constitution §6 L1 mandate.
- **AC-31.6** When `userServings > 12` (the `FractionRenderer.dutchOvenServingWarningThreshold` constant), a non-blocking caption renders directly beneath the stepper row reading: `"Most home dutch ovens (5-quart) cap out around 12 servings. Consider doubling the recipe in two batches instead."` with an `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` glyph in `DODColor.burntOrange`. The caption is informational — it does NOT disable the stepper, does NOT toggle a confirmation sheet, does NOT alter the scaling math. CL-52 captures the threshold rationale (5-quart capacity is the most common home-cook size on Amazon and Lodge's catalog; ~12 servings of a typical stew, chili, or braise is the upper density a 5-quart pot holds without overflowing during simmer).
- **AC-31.7** Changing the stepper value never clears `RecipeDetailViewModel.checkedIngredientIDs`. The check set is keyed on `RecipeIngredient.id` (a stable UUID minted at parse time), and the source ingredient array is not mutated — only the rendered `displayText` swaps. A user who has checked off "salt" at 4 servings and bumps to 8 still sees "salt" checked, just with a doubled quantity in its display.
- **AC-31.8** Scaling is pure presentation. The source `Recipe.servings` integer is never overwritten by user interaction; `RecipeIngredient.text` is never overwritten; `RecipeStore`'s cached copy is never touched. A user who scales the recipe, closes the screen, and re-opens it sees the recipe at its source serving count again — there is no "scale persistence" feature in v1. (Persisting the last-used scale per recipe is a v1.x follow-up if users ask for it; the spec leaves the door open by virtue of `userServings` living on the view model, not the model layer.)

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events. No new design tokens. No new persistence schema. No new wire-format fields. No deep-link grammar changes.
- The `FractionRenderer` utility lives in `DODSupport` (not in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail`) because Cook Mode's ingredients drawer (`CookModeView`) is a second consumer — keeping the renderer in the support layer means both surfaces import it without `DODFeatureRecipeDetail` depending on its own internals. Two surfaces, one canonical fraction table, one shared L1 test suite.
- `RecipeIngredient.text` stays a single free-text field. The JSON-LD `recipeIngredient` payload from WPRM is a single string per ingredient with quantity + unit + name interleaved (e.g. `"720 g bread flour"`, `"1 1/2 pounds cod fillets (cut into 2-3 inch uniform chunks)"`) — there is no structured `amount` + `unit` + `name` decomposition to lean on. The renderer parses the leading quantity by walking the head of the string for an integer / decimal / fraction / mixed-number prefix; everything from the first non-quantity character to the end is appended verbatim. Lines that don't begin with a digit or a Unicode vulgar fraction (e.g. `"Salt and freshly ground black pepper (to taste)"`, `"melted butter (for dipping)"`) return verbatim — the renderer never invents a quantity.
- The Cook Mode `ingredientScaleFactor` parameter defaults to `1.0` so any future direct caller of `CookModeView` (Live Activity intent, App Intent deep link, future widget surface) still works without passing the scale.

### US-32 — Settings page reachable from gear icon in Recipes nav
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** a Settings screen reachable via a gear icon on the trailing edge of the Recipes tab's nav bar,
**so that** I have a discoverable entry point for app-wide preferences (measurement units, About info, version).

Added 2026-05-26 (round-4 backlog item, graduated). v1 of this story ships a **skeleton** Settings surface: the gear-icon entry point plus a `.insetGrouped` list rendering the three rows the backlog described (metric-units toggle, About Me link, version footer). The actual ingredient-conversion implementation behind the toggle and the WP REST fetch behind the About Me link are deliberately deferred to follow-up tasks T-551 (metric units conversion) and ~~T-552 (About Me fetch)~~ (T-552 superseded 2026-05-31 by T-738 / CL-134 — see DUT-14 / Linear; the About copy is now embedded verbatim + Ned's photo bundles as a local asset rather than fetched from the WP REST `/about-me/` endpoint). CL-56 captures the skeleton-first decision and the rationale for bundling SettingsView inside DODFeatureFeed instead of creating a new Swift package.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-32.1** A gear icon (`Image(systemName: "gearshape")`) is rendered on the trailing edge of the Recipes (Feed) tab's nav bar, opposite the leading "Recipes" title. Tapping it presents `SettingsView` (push via NavigationLink so the existing back button works). The icon uses the default toolbar tint and carries the accessibility label `"Settings"`.
- **AC-32.2** `SettingsView` renders as a `List` with `.listStyle(.insetGrouped)` and `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden) + .background(DODColor.surface)`, matching the visual treatment of the Categories tab per CL-54 / T-560 (consistent brand surface across the app).
- **AC-32.3** The list contains three sections: (1) a Toggle row labeled "Use metric units" bound to a UserDefaults-backed flag (key `dod.settings.useMetricUnits`); (2) a NavigationLink row labeled "About Dutch Oven Daddy" pushing a destination view showing ~~"Coming soon — fetched from /about-me/" (the live WP REST fetch is T-552)~~ *(amended in T-738 / CL-134, 2026-05-31)* the embedded About Ned copy from DUT-14 (verbatim, single paragraph) alongside Ned's photo bundled in the app's asset catalog as `AboutNed` (`App/Assets.xcassets/AboutNed.imageset/`, source `https://www.dutchovendaddy.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/Ned-Dutch-Oven-Daddy-square.jpg`); (3) a footer reading `"v\(version) (\(build))"` sourced from `Bundle.main.infoDictionary["CFBundleShortVersionString"]` and `Bundle.main.infoDictionary["CFBundleVersion"]`.
- **AC-32.4** Toggling the "Use metric units" Toggle persists the new value to `UserDefaults.standard` under key `dod.settings.useMetricUnits` and is read back on subsequent app launches. No other surface in the app reads this flag yet (the actual ingredient conversion is T-551 — this PR ships only the toggle UI + persistence so the follow-up can wire it in without view churn).
- **AC-32.5** US-4 (recipe detail rendering) is not regressed — no recipe-detail code path is touched. The existing tab navigation (US-1 / US-2 / US-3 / US-5) is not regressed — the gear icon adds a toolbar item to the Feed tab without changing root-tab routing.
- **AC-32.6** *(Added in T-738 / CL-134, 2026-05-31 — DUT-14 graduation; amended by CL-135 / T-741, DUT-18; amended again by CL-146 / T-749, DUT-55.)* The About destination view renders the verbatim DUT-14 intro copy ("Hi I'm Ned, the Dutch Oven Daddy! I'm a full-time computer nerd and part-time cook. My passion is cast iron cooking with tips, tricks, and delicious recipes. I love using my recipes to bring together family and friends. I believe everything is made better in cast iron!") alongside the bundled `AboutNed` image asset in a ~~magazine-sidebar layout (120pt leading image clipped to a `RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: DODSpacing.sm)` + paragraph wrapping to its right via `HStack(alignment: .top)`)~~ *(amended by CL-135 / T-741, DUT-18 — the right-side wrap did not read well on the live build, so the layout switches to the centered-above arrangement DUT-14 explicitly named as the fallback)* vertically-stacked centered layout (`VStack(alignment: .center)`: a 160pt image centered horizontally on top, clipped to a ~~`RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: DODSpacing.sm)`~~ *(amended by CL-146 / T-749, DUT-55 — now a `Circle()`, matching the app's avatar register per AC-44.15)* `Circle()`, with the copy below it at full width, leading-aligned). Body copy uses `DODType.body` + `DODColor.label`; container uses `DODColor.surface`. **CL-146 / T-749 amendment (DUT-55).** Below the intro paragraph the view renders three additional story paragraphs (Ned's origin story — the gifted skillet + recovery meal-prep, the growth of his cast-iron community activity, and the TV appearances + classes), each as its own `Text` in a leading-aligned `VStack(spacing: DODSpacing.md)` so the paragraph breaks read cleanly. The whole hero + copy stack is wrapped in a `ScrollView` so the longer copy + 160pt photo don't clip on shorter devices (the pre-T-749 plain top-aligned `VStack` + `Spacer` clipped the tail on an iPhone SE). The intro copy + the three story paragraphs are each pinned by an L1 test that asserts the verbatim strings so any future paraphrase trips CI.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events, no new design tokens, no new persistence schema, no new wire-format fields, no new deep-link cases. The skeleton ships zero behavior beyond the toggle's UserDefaults round-trip and the placeholder About Me push; ~~the WP REST fetch (T-552) and ingredient conversion (T-551) are explicit follow-ups~~ ingredient conversion (T-551) is the only remaining follow-up (T-552 superseded by T-738 / CL-134 per DUT-14 — copy is embedded verbatim + Ned's photo bundles as a local asset).
- `SettingsView` lives in `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/` rather than a new `DODFeatureSettings` package — CL-56 walks through the rationale (one screen, one entry point, project.yml churn deferred until Settings grows enough to warrant extraction).

### US-33 — Search-tab affordance refinements: brand-orange Clear All, per-term recent removal
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** the Search tab's "Clear All" button to use the same brand-orange color the Recipes-tab gear icon uses, and to be able to long-press a single recent search to remove just that term without wiping the whole list,
**so that** the Search tab's affordances match the rest of the app's visual language and I can prune individual recents the way iOS users prune individual items everywhere else (Mail, Messages, Photos).

Added 2026-05-27 (round-7 backlog items, graduated). Two small Search-tab polish items bundled into one PR: (1) the "Clear All" text in `RecentSearchesView` switches from `DODColor.castIronBrown` to `DODColor.accent` so it matches the gear icon (US-32 / T-550) which inherits the app-level `.tint(DODColor.accent)` from `RootView`; (2) each recent-search pill gets a `.contextMenu` with a single destructive "Clear" item (trash icon) that calls a new view-model method `removeRecentSearch(_:)` to remove only the long-pressed term from the persistent recents store. CL-57 captures the brand-color match rationale and the per-term context-menu pattern.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-33.1** The "Clear All" `Button` rendered by `IdleSuggestionsView.recentsSection` (in `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchView.swift`) uses `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.accent)` instead of `DODColor.castIronBrown`. The bulk wipe-all behavior (US-29 / AC-29.2 / CL-49.2 — calls `viewModel.clearRecentSearches()`) is unchanged.
- **AC-33.2** Each recent-search pill in `IdleSuggestionsView.recentsSection`'s `FlowLayout` has a `.contextMenu` modifier containing one `Button(role: .destructive)` with `Image(systemName: "trash")` and `Text("Clear")`. Long-pressing a pill surfaces the menu; tapping "Clear" calls the view-model's new `removeRecentSearch(_:)` method with the pill's query string.
- **AC-33.3** `SearchViewModel.removeRecentSearch(_:)` removes only the matched term from the `UserDefaults`-backed `RecentSearches` store (case-insensitive match, mirroring `RecentSearches.record(_:)`'s dedupe rule) and updates the view-bound `recentSearches` array. If the matched term is the only entry, the "Recent" section disappears on the next observation tick (same path as `clearRecentSearches()`). If no term matches, the call is a no-op.
- **AC-33.4** US-3 (text-query search), US-12 (recents persistence + filter chips + ingredient index), US-29 (round-6 Search-tab polish bundle) are not regressed.
