The two-character debounce path, the recents-persist-across-VM-instances contract, the bulk Clear All affordance, the `questionmark.circle` no-results glyph, ~~the `tag.fill` chip iconography~~ *(amended in T-636 / CL-105 — the `tag.fill`-bearing `categoryChip` no longer exists; the surviving filter chip is `cookTimeChip` with `clock`)*, the category-pill-into-query-field path are all unchanged.
- **AC-33.5** (Added in T-636 / CL-105.) The per-pill long-press `.contextMenu`'s destructive `Button(role: .destructive)` inside `IdleSuggestionsView.recentsSection` carries `.tint(.red)` so the SF Symbol trash icon and the "Clear" title both render red. Without the explicit tint the icon would inherit the ancestral `DODColor.accent` (orange — same path AC-33.1's "Clear All" uses for its brand-orange treatment) while the role's automatic styling would render the title in red, producing a visually mismatched menu item. `.tint(.red)` composes with `role: .destructive` (it overrides the inherited app-level accent at the button scope without fighting the role's text-color treatment); `.foregroundStyle(.red)` on the Label was considered and rejected because it would override `role: .destructive`'s automatic styling rather than composing with it.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events, no new design tokens, no new persistence schema, no new wire-format fields, no new deep-link cases. `DODColor.accent` and `DODColor.castIronBrown` both already exist (Colors.swift lines 20 and 24); the change is a one-token swap at one call site. `RecentSearches` gains one new public method (`remove(_:)`) modeled on the existing `record(_:)` / `clear()` shape.
- Round-7 backlog "Color refinements" and "Long-press context menus" items graduate as a single bundle because both touch `SearchView.swift`'s `RecentSearchesView`, both are S/XS scope, and bundling avoids two near-identical PRs against the same view.

---

### US-35 — Recipe detail supports explicit download for offline use
**As a** weekend cook headed to a campground with no signal,
**I want** to pre-stage a recipe on my phone without committing to saving it,
**so that** the recipe is on-device for the trip but isn't cluttering my Saved tab when I get home.

Added 2026-05-27 (round-7 backlog item, graduated). Pure UX add — a third nav-bar action on recipe detail, semantically distinct from Save (AC-4.7 / AC-5.1) and Share (AC-4.8). Save is "I want to remember this for later"; Download is "I want to use this without network" — a pre-cook stash that survives cache eviction without occupying a slot on the Saved tab. The two flags are independent — a recipe can be downloaded without being saved, or saved without an explicit download tap ~~(already pre-downloaded per AC-5.2)~~. CL-61 captures the UX-distinction-from-Save rationale and the idempotent "already downloaded" behaviour. *Amended by CL-158 (T-761, 2026-06-12): the two flags are now coupled in the download→save direction — **Download also saves** (download = save + offline pin), while **Save still does not download**. A merely-saved recipe is no longer "already pre-downloaded": saving is a lightweight favorite (findability), and the offline guarantee is owned by Download.*

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-35.1** A third nav-bar action sits between Save (`bookmark` / `bookmark.fill`, AC-4.7) and Share (`square.and.arrow.up`, AC-4.8), rendered with the SF Symbol `square.and.arrow.down`. Accessibility label is "Download for offline use" when not yet downloaded, "Downloaded for offline use" once the recipe carries a `downloadedAt` timestamp. Tap target follows the same `DODSpacing.md` rhythm as the other two toolbar items. *Amended by CL-172 (T-775 / DUT-81, 2026-06-14): the action is now a **toggle**. When `downloadedAt != nil` the glyph is `square.and.arrow.down.fill` tinted `DODColor.burntOrange` and tapping **removes** the download (`RecipeDetailViewModel.removeDownload()` → `RecipeStore.removeDownload(id:)`, "Download removed" snackbar, a11y label "Remove download"); when not downloaded it stays the outline `square.and.arrow.down` (label color) and tapping downloads. This supersedes the always-outline glyph + the "Already downloaded" re-tap snackbar (AC-35.3 / AC-35.4) — re-tapping a downloaded recipe now un-downloads it.* *Amended by CL-175 (T-778 / DUT-84, 2026-06-14): removing a download (the toggle's remove direction) while **offline** first presents a confirmation (Keep Download / Remove Download) per AC-35.8; online removal stays immediate.*
- **AC-35.2** Tapping the download button (a) routes the recipe payload (id, title, ingredients, instructions, meta — already in `RecipeStore` via the existing JSON-LD merge path AC-4.11) through a new "offline pinned" flag (`downloadedAt: Date?` on `CachedRecipe`) so the row survives LRU eviction even when `isSaved == false`, and (b) downloads the hero image at full resolution (`heroImageLargeURL` if available, otherwise `heroImage`) via `ImageLoader` + `RecipeStore.cacheImage(url:bytes:pinnedToSavedRecipeID:)` so the image bytes are pinned from cache eviction the same way AC-5.2's save-driven pre-download pins them. The hero-image pin reuses the existing `pinnedToSavedRecipeID` field on `CachedImage` (the field name is historical — its semantic is "this row is pinned from eviction"; both saved and explicitly-downloaded recipes share the same pin path).
- **AC-35.3** A transient snackbar confirms the action: "Recipe downloaded for offline use" on a first-time download, "Already downloaded" when the user taps download on a recipe whose `downloadedAt != nil` ~~OR `isSaved == true` (the latter because saves auto-download per AC-5.2 — the recipe is already on-device)~~. The snackbar auto-dismisses after the existing `RecipeDetailView.snackbar` 3-second sleep. *Amended by CL-158 (T-761, 2026-06-12): the `isSaved == true` branch is removed — Save and Download are decoupled, so a merely-saved recipe is NOT "already downloaded"; tapping Download on it performs a real first-time download. "Already downloaded" keys only on the real `downloadedAt` pin.* *Amended by CL-172 (T-775 / DUT-81, 2026-06-14): the "Already downloaded" copy is no longer reachable from the detail button — it is now a toggle (AC-35.1), so tapping a downloaded recipe **removes** the download ("Download removed" snackbar) instead of re-confirming. The `.alreadyDownloaded` outcome remains an internal idempotency guard on `downloadForOffline`, but the button never triggers it.* *Amended by CL-181 (T-785, 2026-06-19): the first-time snackbar copy is now content-type aware — "Article downloaded for offline use" when `recipe.isArticle` (`kind == .article`), else "Recipe downloaded for offline use" (recipes byte-identical). The remove ("Download removed") and error toasts are unchanged; they never named the type.*
- **AC-35.4** Download is idempotent: tapping download on an already-downloaded recipe is a no-op semantically — the snackbar surfaces the "Already downloaded" copy per AC-35.3, the `downloadedAt` timestamp is left at its existing value (not refreshed), and no second hero-image fetch is issued. The button's accessibility label flips to "Downloaded for offline use" so VoiceOver users know the state. The button is NOT visually disabled — re-tapping is a safe no-op so the user discovers the state via the snackbar rather than via a dead button. *Amended by CL-172 (T-775 / DUT-81, 2026-06-14): superseded — the button is now a stateful toggle (AC-35.1), not a re-tappable no-op. Tapping a downloaded recipe performs the inverse action (remove download via `RecipeStore.removeDownload(id:)`); the store-level idempotency (no `downloadedAt` refresh, no second image fetch) still holds for the download direction, and `removeDownload` is itself idempotent (no-op if already not downloaded).*
- **AC-35.5** Download and Save are independent flags on the cached row. A recipe can be in any of four states: (1) neither saved nor downloaded — the default after a detail open, lives in LRU and may be evicted; (2) downloaded only — `downloadedAt != nil`, `isSaved == false`, pinned from LRU; (3) saved only — `isSaved == true`, `downloadedAt == nil`, pinned from LRU per the existing AC-5.1 path; (4) both — `isSaved == true && downloadedAt != nil`, pinned. Unsaving a recipe that was *also* explicitly downloaded keeps it pinned (the `downloadedAt` flag survives the `toggleSaved` toggle); explicitly removing the download is **out of scope for v1** (a future "Remove download" affordance is tracked as a backlog item in CL-61's deferred-work section). *Amended by CL-158 (T-761, 2026-06-12): tapping **Download** now ALSO sets `isSaved = true` (via `RecipeStore.markSaved`) — download = save + offline pin — so a fresh Download lands in state (4) "both", and state (2) "downloaded only" is reachable only by later **unsaving** a downloaded recipe. The four-flag model + the unsave-keeps-`downloadedAt` rule are otherwise unchanged.* *Amended by CL-172 (T-775 / DUT-81, 2026-06-14): the deferred "Remove download" affordance is now **graduated** — `RecipeStore.removeDownload(id:)` clears `downloadedAt` (reverting to state (3) "saved only", which it always is post-CL-158), surfaced both on the recipe-detail toggle (AC-35.1) and the Saved-tab long-press "Remove Download" menu item (AC-34.6). The hero image stays pinned (the pin is the saved-offline contract, AC-4.9 — un-download ≠ unsave); states (1)/(2) "downloaded only" remain unreachable in normal flow since download always saves.*
- **AC-35.6** No new analytics event in v1. The existing `recipeSaved` / `recipeUnsaved` events fire only on the Save path (unchanged); the download path is local-only and intentionally not telemetered to keep the constitution §9 allowlist tight. A future story can add a `recipeDownloaded(recipeID:)` event if usage signals emerge.
- **AC-35.7** *(Added by CL-158 / T-761, 2026-06-12)* Save and Download are decoupled in both directions. Tapping **Download** also saves the recipe (AC-35.5 amendment): `LiveRecipeDetailDependencies.downloadForOffline` calls `RecipeStore.markSaved(id:)` (idempotent — no-op if already saved) after `markDownloaded`, the view model flips `isSaved = true`, and the saved-recipes widget snapshot is republished. This holds even on the re-download (`.alreadyDownloaded`) path, so recipes downloaded under the pre-T-761 behavior get their bookmark backfilled ("downloaded recipes still save"). Tapping **Save** (the bookmark, AC-4.7 / AC-5.1) is a lightweight favorite: it sets only `isSaved` via `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)`, never sets `downloadedAt`, and does **not** force a full-resolution hero-image pin — the recipe is added to the Saved tab for findability, not pinned for guaranteed offline use. The success snackbar reads "Saved." The explicit Download action (AC-35.2) remains the sole path that guarantees offline availability.
- **AC-35.8** *(Added by CL-175 / T-778 / DUT-84, 2026-06-14)* Removing a download while the device is **offline** first presents a confirmation, because the removal would strand the recipe (no network to re-fetch its text/hero image until service returns). A native alert — title "You're offline", body "If you remove this download, you won't be able to open this recipe until you're back online. You can always download it again once you're connected." — offers **Remove Download** (destructive) and **Keep Download** (`.cancel`, the bold default). It is shown **only when offline**; when online, removal is immediate with no prompt (re-downloadable in a tap), exactly as AC-35.1 / AC-34.6 describe. Both remove surfaces gate identically: the recipe-detail toggle (`RecipeDetailViewModel.toggleDownload()` raises `showOfflineRemoveDownloadWarning` when `!dependencies.isOnline()`, else calls `removeDownload()`) and the Saved-tab "Remove Download" menu (`SavedViewModel.requestRemoveDownload(id:)` stashes `pendingOfflineRemoveDownloadID` when offline, else removes); both present the shared `View.offlineRemoveDownloadAlert(isPresented:onRemove:)` (DODDesignSystem) so copy + button roles are identical. Connectivity reads the existing `NetworkMonitor` seam (`SavedDependencies.isOnline()` newly routes to it; the detail VM already did). **Unsave is intentionally not gated** (DUT-84 decision): Save is a lightweight bookmark, not an offline pin (AC-35.7), and a download survives an unsave (AC-35.5), so unsaving never strands a guaranteed-offline copy.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new wire-format fields, no new deep-link cases, no new design tokens. The schema change is additive: `CachedRecipe` gains an optional `downloadedAt: Date?` field, which qualifies as a lightweight migration per `Packages/DODPersistence/MIGRATION.md` rule 1 (additive optionals don't require a transform). SwiftData's `MigrationPlan` already declares `lightweight(fromVersion: SchemaV2, toVersion: SchemaV3)`; bumping the V3 schema in place is the simplest path because V3 only landed in round 4 and no production migration has run against it yet — the additive field rides as part of V3.
- Implementing PR: T-620.

### US-34 — Recipe cards support long-press save via context menu
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** to long-press any recipe card and tap "Save" from the context menu,
**so that** I can add a recipe to my Saved tab without opening it first.

Added 2026-05-27 (round-7 backlog item, graduated). Pure UX add — same save code path as the AC-4.7 / AC-5.1 detail-screen bookmark tap (`RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)`), just exposed as a second entry point from the list surfaces. ~~The menu shows "Save" with the `bookmark.fill` glyph; CL-60 captures the decision to **not** branch to "Unsave" in v1 (each Feed/Search/Saved/Categories card would need a per-row `isSaved` query that the feature view models don't currently carry — the always-"Save" path is idempotent in practice because tapping Save on an already-saved recipe is a no-op safe failure per `RecipeStore.toggleSaved`'s LRU-eviction contract on the saved → unsaved transition only).~~ *Amended by CL-103 / T-634 (2026-05-29): the helper is state-aware (saved card → "Unsave" + outline `bookmark`; unsaved card → "Save" + `bookmark.fill`). The Saved tab passes `isSaved: true` for every card; Feed/Categories/Search pass `isSaved: false` with a TODO marker. See AC-34.6.*

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-34.1** Long-pressing a `RecipeCard` (in Feed, Search results, Saved list, or Category recipes list) presents a SwiftUI `.contextMenu` with a single "Save" item using the `bookmark.fill` SF Symbol. The menu is the standard iOS context menu — preview includes the card itself per SwiftUI's default behaviour.
- **AC-34.2** Tapping the "Save" menu item invokes the same save code path as the recipe-detail nav-bar bookmark tap (AC-4.7 / AC-5.1) — `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` against the recipe's id. The host caches the `RecipeListItem` first via `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` so the toggle has a row to mutate (relevant on the Categories + Search surfaces where a freshly-fetched REST result may not have been hydrated by a detail open yet).
- **AC-34.3** After the save lands, the host kicks the saved-recipes widget snapshot publisher (`SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.publish()`) so the home-screen widget timeline refreshes the same frame the user expects — mirrors the `LiveRecipeDetailDependencies.publishSavedWidgetSnapshot()` contract on the recipe-detail screen save.
- **AC-34.4** ~~The context menu does NOT branch to "Unsave" when the recipe is already saved (CL-60). Tapping Save on an already-saved recipe is a no-op safe failure — the SwiftData row's `isSaved` toggles back to `false`, the LRU eviction path kicks in per the existing `toggleSaved` contract, and the user sees no visible regression because the menu copy stays "Save" regardless of cached state. A future story can add the per-row `isSaved` query if user feedback surfaces the asymmetry.~~ *Amended by CL-103 / T-634 (2026-05-29): the context menu IS state-aware — see AC-34.6. The Saved tab passes `isSaved: true` for every card; Feed/Categories/Search pass `isSaved: false` with a TODO marker until a per-row lookup is wired (the CL-60 plumbing-cost trade-off still applies at those surfaces).*
- **AC-34.5** The existing `recipeCardTap` modifier (the navigation-tap surface, REG-DOD-LIST-SCROLL) is not regressed — tapping the card still pushes the detail screen; the long-press menu is additive. Per SwiftUI's standard composition, `.contextMenu` does not eat the surrounding tap gesture.
- **AC-34.6** *(Added by CL-103 / T-634, 2026-05-29)* The `recipeCardContextMenu(isSaved:onToggle:)` helper renders a state-aware `Label`: `isSaved: true` → text "Unsave" + `Image(systemName: "bookmark")` (outline); `isSaved: false` → text "Save" + `Image(systemName: "bookmark.fill")`. The single menu `Button` invokes the supplied `onToggle` closure, which routes through `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` per AC-34.2's seam — confirmed to toggle in both directions, so tapping "Unsave" on a saved card correctly transitions the row to `isSaved == false`. The Saved tab (`SavedView`) passes `isSaved: true` for every card because every card in the tab is saved by definition; Feed (`FeedView`), Categories (`CategoryRecipesView`), and Search (`SearchView`) pass `isSaved: false` with a TODO comment pending per-row state plumbing. *Amended by CL-104 / T-635 (2026-05-29): on the Saved tab, the context-menu Button action calls `viewModel.optimisticallyRemove(id: recipe.id)` **before** `onSave?(recipe)` so the card disappears instantly from the `LazyVGrid` (the store toggle bubbles asynchronously through `TabStack` without a completion callback; without the optimistic local removal the card lingered until the next `.task` cycle / tab switch). See AC-5.3.* *Amended by CL-162 / T-765 (DUT-71, 2026-06-13): the Feed / Categories / Search TODO is **resolved** — all three card surfaces now pass a **state-aware** `isSaved`. Each view model owns a `savedRecipeIDs: Set<Int>` hydrated from the new `RecipeStore.savedRecipeIDs()` (via each feature's `…Dependencies.savedRecipeIDs()`, default `[]` so fakes keep compiling), refreshed on every appear so a save made on another surface reflects; the card passes `isSaved: viewModel.savedRecipeIDs.contains(item.id)`, and the long-press action applies an optimistic local flip (`applyOptimisticSaveToggle(id:)`) before `onSave?` so the label is correct on re-open. This fixes the prior bug where an already-saved recipe showed "Save" on these tabs and tapping it silently **un**-saved it. The Saved tab path (CL-104) is unchanged. *Amended by CL-172 (T-775 / DUT-81, 2026-06-14): `recipeCardContextMenu` gains optional `isDownloaded: Bool = false` + `onRemoveDownload: (() -> Void)? = nil`. When both are set, a second menu Button — "Remove Download" + `square.and.arrow.down.badge.xmark` tinted `DODColor.burntOrange` — renders beneath the Save/Unsave item, invoking `onRemoveDownload`. Only the **Saved tab** passes them (`isDownloaded: downloadedIDs.contains(recipe.id)`, `onRemoveDownload` → `SavedViewModel.removeDownload(id:)` which optimistically clears the badge then routes `SavedDependencies.removeDownload(id:)` → store); Feed/Categories/Search omit the download branch pending the same per-row-state work this CL-162 resolution did for `isSaved`.* *Amended by CL-175 (T-778 / DUT-84, 2026-06-14): the Saved-tab "Remove Download" action routes through `SavedViewModel.requestRemoveDownload(id:)`, which — when **offline** — presents the AC-35.8 confirmation before removing (online stays immediate). `removeDownload(id:)` remains the underlying optimistic-clear + store-write worker.*

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events, no new design tokens, no new persistence schema, no new wire-format fields, no new deep-link cases. Implementing PR: T-590. The card surface lives in `RecipeCard.swift` (DODDesignSystem); the per-call-site wiring lives in each feature view (Feed/Search/Saved/Categories) per CL-60's per-call-site placement decision.
- Snapshot tests don't capture `.contextMenu` (the menu pops as a system overlay outside the snapshot host), so no new L4 baselines are recorded for the menu itself — the existing card baselines continue to lock the card's visual identity unchanged.

### US-37 — Recipes & Articles tab renders both recipe posts and article posts from WordPress
**As any** reader who taps a roundup-style post like "Best Dutch Oven Recipes (30+ Tried and Tested Favorites)",
**I want** the post to open as an article (HTML body) instead of vanishing or popping back with "Recipe unavailable,"
**so that** every published post on dutchovendaddy.com is reachable from the app, not just the WPRM-instrumented ones.

Added 2026-05-27 (round-7 backlog item, graduated). Two coupled changes: (1) the "Recipes" tab is renamed to **"Recipes & Articles"** in both the bottom-tab label and the screen's nav title, and (2) posts whose recipe-detail fetch + JSON-LD parse fails are classified as **articles** and rendered with their HTML body (sanitized via the existing `HTMLSanitizer` + a new minimal `ArticleBodyExtractor`), rather than being hidden from lists per the pre-existing AC-1.7 / CL-9 contract. The article path **reverses CL-9's "hide on missing JSON-LD" decision** for the dominant case (post exists, JSON-LD missing, HTML body extractable) and preserves the auto-pop `.unavailable` path only as a final fallback (both JSON-LD and article-body extraction failed — i.e. the post page genuinely cannot be rendered).

CL-63 captures the rationale, the lineage to CL-9 + CL-10, the `PostKind` domain mapping, the article-body extraction strategy (read the same HTML page the JSON-LD parser already fetches; find the `entry-content` / `article` / `main` container; sanitize via `HTMLSanitizer.plainText(from:)` for v1 — rich HTML rendering is a v1.x follow-up), and the deliberate scope exclusions (no Cook Mode CTA on articles, no save-for-offline semantics specific to articles in v1, no separate article tab, no separate widget surface).

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-37.1** The "Recipes" tab is renamed to **"Recipes & Articles"** in `FeedView.navigationTitle` (drives the screen's nav-bar title). *Amended by [CL-65](clarifications.md#cl-65-us-37--t-660-2026-05-27) (T-660, 2026-05-27): the bottom-tab label and the nav title are now two different strings — the bottom tab bar reads `AppTab.tabLabel` ("Recipes", short) while the screen header reads `AppTab.title` ("Recipes & Articles", full). The tab-bar split is required because the system tab-bar item has a fixed ~80pt width on standard iPhone widths and renders "Recipes & Articles" as "Recipes & Arti..." with an ellipsis — the truncation is a real user-visible regression introduced by T-640. The short tab label matches the pre-T-640 wording and is unambiguously paired with the `house` glyph; the content semantics (recipes AND articles) are surfaced by the screen-header nav title once the user taps the tab. The original wording — "renamed... in both `AppTab.title` (drives the bottom-tab label per SwiftUI's `Label(systemImage:)` convention) and `FeedView.navigationTitle`" — was based on the assumption that one string drives both the tab label and the nav title; T-660 splits them so the tab label and the nav title are no longer coupled.* The tab's SF Symbol (`house`), telemetry name (`telemetryName == "feed"`), `AppTab.allCases` ordering (Recipes & Articles → Categories → Saved → Search per AC-16.1), and every other surface is unchanged. *Amended by CL-177 (T-781, 2026-06-15): `FeedView` drops `.navigationTitle` — the 'Recipes & Articles' title now renders as a `DODScreenHeader` (bold large `Text`) at the top of the feed ScrollView, scrolling up and away with the content instead of the native large-title minimize (the app-wide scroll-away header pattern; CL-177). The layout-toggle + Settings gear toolbar stay pinned; the tab-bar label / `house` glyph / telemetry are unchanged.*
- **AC-37.2** A new `PostKind` enum lives in `DODDomain` with cases `.recipe` (JSON-LD parse succeeded — the existing path) and `.article` (JSON-LD parse failed but the HTML body extracted as non-empty text — the new path). The kind is surfaced on the domain model used by recipe-detail rendering (implementation chooses between adding a `kind` property on `Recipe` and introducing a separate `Article` domain type — CL-63 documents the trade-off; either satisfies this AC). The classification fires inside the recipe-detail fetch path (currently `RecipeDetailViewModel.fetchAndParse()`) immediately after `parseJSONLD(...)` throws: if `ArticleBodyExtractor.extract(html:)` returns non-empty text, the post is classified as `.article` and the view model transitions to a new `LoadState.article(...)` case (or equivalent); otherwise the post falls through to the existing `LoadState.unavailable` path with the same snackbar + auto-pop behavior.
- **AC-37.3** When `LoadState` resolves to article, the recipe-detail screen renders a new `ArticleDetailView` (in `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/`) showing the post's hero image, title, published date, and HTML body content. The article view explicitly **does not** show the ingredients section, the Cook Mode CTA (US-7), the servings stepper (US-31), the rating summary (US-13), the comments composer (US-14), or the related-recipes strip (US-4 / AC-4.6) — those are recipe-specific affordances that have no meaning for an article. The nav-bar **Share** button (AC-4.8) is preserved (any post on the blog is shareable via its canonical URL). The nav-bar **Save** button (AC-4.7) is preserved as the affordance, but in v1 the save semantics for articles intentionally **don't** include the AC-5.2 5-second pre-download — see AC-37.6 and CL-63 for the rationale. *Amended by CL-184 (T-788, 2026-06-20, DUT-95): the article's published date renders as an absolute medium-style date — "Published Jun 1, 2026" (`ArticleDetailView.publishedDateFormatter`, `DateFormatter.dateStyle = .medium`, locale-aware) — NOT the former relative "Published X ago" (`style: .relative`). The visible text now equals the VoiceOver label by construction (the separate `.accessibilityLabel` is dropped as redundant). Recipe detail still shows no published date (article-only); comment timestamps stay relative.* *Re-amended by CL-185 (T-789, 2026-06-20, DUT-96): the article date moves from medium to long style ("Jun 1, 2026" → "June 1, 2026") per Ned, via the new shared `PublishedDateCaption` (which replaces `ArticleDetailView`'s private formatter). Recipe detail now ALSO shows the published date in the same long format (see AC-4.1).* *Amended by CL-200 (T-806, 2026-06-20, DUT-182): on iPad the article body is now capped to a centered ~700pt reading column (`View.readableContentColumn`, the shared helper from CL-198 / T-804) under the still-full-bleed hero, so prose stops stretching edge-to-edge on the wide canvas; iPhone (`.compact`) is byte-identical (the modifier returns the content unchanged, so the L4 `ArticleDetailViewSnapshotTests` baselines are unaffected). No two-up — an article is pure prose, unlike the recipe's Ingredients|Instructions.*
- **AC-37.4** The classification is sticky: once a post is classified as `.article` (via `RecipeStore.markJSONLDFailed(id:)`), subsequent list renders show it alongside recipes in the same `RecipeListItem` row format (hero + title + excerpt + total time per AC-1.3) — the kind difference shows only on the detail screen. The pull-to-refresh blocklist reset (the pre-existing `RecipeStore.clearBlocklist()` call site) is preserved so a newly-fixed post (JSON-LD added server-side later) can return to recipe rendering on the next detail open.
- **AC-37.5** AC-1.7 is amended to remove the "filtered out and not rendered" wording — articles are now rendered. AC-4.11 is amended to note that JSON-LD-less posts use HTML rendering, not the auto-pop. AC-5.8's "Saved tab" empty-state copy is unchanged ("Tap the bookmark on any recipe to save it for offline.") — the bookmark glyph + the Save semantics still match the user's mental model regardless of post kind. AC-16.1's tab-bar ordering is unchanged.
- **AC-37.6** Article save semantics in v1 are: tapping the bookmark on `ArticleDetailView` flips the row's `isSaved` flag via `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` (same code path as the recipe-detail bookmark per AC-5.1) AND caches the article's HTML body text in the existing `CachedRecipe` row (reusing the `ingredientsJSON` field as a generic text-body slot is **not** acceptable — see CL-63 for the storage discussion; the chosen path is a new `articleBodyHTML: String?` field on `CachedRecipe` since the `@Model` migration cost is bounded). The AC-5.2 5-second pre-download contract — full payload + hero image — is satisfied by article saves the same way it is for recipe saves: the article body is already in memory at save time (the fetch is what triggered the article classification), so persisting it is a single `try modelContext.save()` round-trip. The article's hero image pre-download path is the same `LiveSavedDependencies.preDownloadImages(forRecipeID:urls:)` call recipe saves already use. The Saved tab shows saved articles alongside saved recipes per AC-5.3, in the same row format.
- **AC-37.7** L1 unit tests cover: (a) `PostKind` classification — JSON-LD success → `.recipe`, JSON-LD failure + article-body extraction success → `.article`, both failures → `.unavailable` (the existing path); (b) the article-detail load-state transitions on the view model; (c) `ArticleBodyExtractor.extract(html:)` extracting non-empty text from a fixture rendered HTML page that contains an `entry-content` div. L4 snapshot baselines cover: (a) the renamed tab bar (re-record T-310's existing baselines), (b) the renamed nav title on the Feed tab (re-record any existing FeedView screen-level baselines), (c) `ArticleDetailView` in light + dark on iPhone 13 baseline at default Dynamic Type. The new baselines are bounded — the article path is testable via the L1 classification + L4 `ArticleDetailView` snapshot even if no article post is available in the live feed at PR verification time.
- **AC-37.8** US-1 (Feed / home feed), US-3 (Search), US-2 (Categories), US-5 (Saved) are **not regressed** by the article classification — articles render in the same list row format alongside recipes and tap-target to `ArticleDetailView` instead of `RecipeDetailView` via the same `RecipeRoute.recipe(item:)` push (the destination view branches internally on `LoadState.recipe` vs `LoadState.article`, so the route grammar doesn't need a new case). US-4 (Recipe detail) is not regressed — the existing recipe-rendering path is unchanged; only the failure-handling branch picks up article classification. AC-T2 (L3 smoke "feed shows at least one recipe with a non-placeholder hero image") is unchanged — articles also carry hero images per `RecipeListItem.heroImage`.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- No new analytics events. No new deep-link grammar (`dod://recipe/<id>` still routes to detail regardless of kind — the route is post-kind-agnostic). No new design tokens. No new widget surfaces. The persistence schema adds one optional column on `CachedRecipe` (`articleBodyHTML: String?`) for AC-37.6's article-save semantics; the SwiftData migration is lightweight (additive optional field, no rename, no required-field promotion).
- The HTML rendering in `ArticleDetailView` uses `HTMLSanitizer.plainText(from:)` for v1 — the article body is rendered as a single `Text(...)` with the sanitized plain text. Rich rendering (preserve `<h2>` / `<h3>` / `<ul>` / `<a href>` styling) is a v1.x follow-up; the v1 scope is "the user can read the article in the app without the post being hidden," and plain-text rendering with appropriate Dynamic Type satisfies the spec contract. CL-63 walks through the alternatives (NSAttributedString with `documentType: .html`, AttributedString markdown-style, third-party HTML-to-SwiftUI renderer).
- The article view's Cook Mode CTA is intentionally absent because articles have no `recipeInstructions` to walk. The servings stepper is intentionally absent because articles have no `recipeYield` to scale. The rating summary + comments composer are intentionally absent because v1's WPRM rating endpoint requires a WPRM-instrumented recipe id, and posts without JSON-LD `Recipe` blocks generally also lack the WPRM `recipe_id` post-meta the ratings client posts against; the comments endpoint *does* accept any `post=<id>` argument and would technically work on articles, but the empty-state UX is uneven enough that landing the surface in a follow-up story is the right scope discipline. CL-63 documents these exclusions.
- The article-classification reuses the pre-existing `CachedRecipe.jsonLDFailedAt` field as the kind discriminator: a non-nil `jsonLDFailedAt` row now means "this post is an article" rather than "this post is hidden from lists" (the pre-T-640 semantic). This is a deliberate field-semantic reframe rather than a new column — the alternative (introduce `CachedRecipe.kind: String?` enum-as-string) would add a column for information already encoded by the `jsonLDFailedAt` non-null state. CL-63 walks through this trade-off.

### US-36 — Settings page has standard rows for notifications, appearance, share defaults, cache management, and telemetry opt-out
**As a** Returning Reader,
**I want** the Settings page to expose the standard preferences I'd expect from any modern recipe app — notification toggle, appearance picker, default share format, a way to clear cached recipe images, and a telemetry opt-out —
**so that** the Settings surface feels complete instead of placeholder-thin, and so I can tune the app's behavior to my privacy + storage + visual preferences without leaving the app.

Added 2026-05-27 (round-7 backlog "Settings page expansion" item, graduated). T-550 shipped the Settings skeleton (gear icon + metric-units toggle + About link + version footer) per US-32; T-630 expands the surface with the five rows the backlog's audit-and-pick narrowed to: **Notifications** (toggle, UI-only in v1, T-631 follow-up wires APNs), **Appearance** (picker for Match System / Light / Dark, drives `.preferredColorScheme` on the app root), **Default Share Format** (picker between "Just the link" and "Link + recipe text", persisted for AC-6.2's `recipeShared` flow to consume), **Clear Cached Recipe Images** (button → `RecipeStore.clearImageCache()` → snackbar with freed-MB count), and **Share Anonymous Usage Data** (toggle, defaults ON per constitution §9, when OFF the `TelemetryDeckTransport` short-circuits so no events leave the device). CL-62 captures the per-row decisions (notifications UI-only for v1, Match-System default, link-only share default, cache-clear seam, telemetry-gate placement).

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-36.1 (Notification Settings group — amended T-750 / CL-147, DUT-56).** `SettingsView` renders a `Section` headed **"Notification Settings"** containing two toggles. **(1) "When New Recipes Drop"** (~~"Notify me when new recipes drop"~~ — renamed in T-750; the `dod.settings.notificationsEnabled` key + `settings-toggle-notifications` identifier are unchanged so the persisted preference survives the rename) bound to the UserDefaults-backed flag, defaults OFF. Flipping ON requests *local* notification authorization (`UNUserNotificationCenter`, not APNs — see US-42 / CL-100); a denied prompt reverts the toggle to OFF + surfaces a snackbar. **(2) "When Someone Replies to My Comment"** (T-750 / CL-147, identifier `settings-toggle-comment-reply-notifications`, key `dod.settings.commentReplyNotificationsEnabledV1`) — a persisted preference that, on enable, requests the same authorization (mirroring toggle 1: persist on grant, revert + snackbar on deny). **Delivery of comment-reply alerts awaits a server-side push trigger that does not exist in v1** (the same backend gap US-42 / DUT-15 covers for new-post pushes) — this ships the UI + the stored preference + the secured permission so the delivery path can light up later without a second permission round-trip. The section footer reads "Alerts are delivered on this device only." The two toggle setters live in `SettingsViewModel+Notifications.swift` (file_length split).
- **AC-36.2 (Appearance row — amended T-755 / CL-152, T-756 / CL-153).** `SettingsView` renders a `Picker` (labeled ~~"Appearance"~~ "App Appearance" per T-755 / CL-152, in the "Customization" group) with three options: "Match System", "Light", "Dark". Defaults to "Match System" (the system-default `colorScheme` continues to drive every screen until the user picks Light or Dark explicitly). The selection persists to UserDefaults under `dod.settings.appearance` as the raw value of an `AppearancePreference` enum (`"system"` / `"light"` / `"dark"`). `RootView` reads the persisted value via `@AppStorage` and applies `.preferredColorScheme(...)` to the main window. The accessibility identifier is `settings-picker-appearance`. **T-756 / CL-153 amendment (DUT-62).** The `SettingsViewModel.appearance` property (plus `temperaturePreference` + `voiceGender`) is an `@Observable` STORED property (was computed-over-store, which `@Observable` can't track — so the picker's selected-value label never updated live). The Settings sheet additionally carries its OWN `.preferredColorScheme(viewModel.appearance.colorScheme)` so it re-themes live when the picker changes (a `.sheet` does not inherit `RootView`'s `preferredColorScheme`, so without this the sheet only re-themed on close + reopen). `AppearancePreference.colorScheme: ColorScheme?` (`.system` → `nil` / `.light` → `.light` / `.dark` → `.dark`) is the shared mapping used by both `RootView` and `SettingsView`. *Amended by CL-160 (T-763 / DUT-69, 2026-06-13): the two explicit-scheme option **labels** gain brand personality — "Light" → **"Flour"**, "Dark" → **"Cocoa"** (the picker now reads "Match System" / "Flour" / "Cocoa"). Labels only — the `AppearancePreference` cases, their persisted `rawValue`s (`"system"` / `"light"` / `"dark"`), the `colorScheme` mapping, the default ("Match System"), and the `settings-picker-appearance` identifier are all unchanged, so a saved preference and the theming behavior are preserved.*
- **AC-36.3 (Default Share Format — UI row removed T-750 / CL-147, DUT-56).** ~~`SettingsView` renders a `Section` containing a `Picker("Default share format", selection: ...)` with two options: "Just the link" and "Link + recipe text".~~ *(The Settings UI row was removed in T-750 / CL-147 as part of the DUT-56 page cleanup — the picker read as random/unnecessary on the page and the `linkAndText` payload was never wired to a consumer.)* The `ShareFormatPreference` enum + the `SettingsViewModel.shareFormat` property + the `dod.settings.shareFormat` persistence key are **retained** (no consumer was removed — the persisted preference + its L1 round-trip tests stay intact) so the future link+text share feature can re-surface the control without re-introducing the type. Defaults to "Just the link" (the existing AC-6.2 behavior — share the canonical URL alone — is preserved).
- **AC-36.4 (Clear Cached Recipe Images row).** `SettingsView` renders a `Section` containing a `Button("Clear Cached Recipe Images", ...)` (not a toggle — this is a one-shot action). Tapping invokes a new `RecipeStore.clearImageCache()` method that purges every unpinned `CachedImage` row from SwiftData and deletes the corresponding App Group file bridge mirror via `WidgetImageBridge.deleteImage(for:)`. Pinned images (rows with a non-nil `pinnedToSavedRecipeID`) are preserved — those bytes belong to saved recipes (AC-5.2's offline pre-download contract) and the user expects to keep them. After the clear completes, the view-model computes the total bytes freed (sum of `bytes.count` across the deleted rows, returned by `clearImageCache()`) and surfaces a snackbar reading "Freed X.X MB of cached images." (or "Cache was already clear." when 0 bytes were freed). The accessibility identifier is `settings-button-clear-cache`.
- **AC-36.5 (Share Anonymous Usage Data row).** `SettingsView` renders a `Section` containing a `Toggle("Share anonymous usage data", ...)` row bound to a UserDefaults-backed `dod.settings.telemetryEnabled` flag. Defaults ON (matching constitution §9's existing posture — telemetry is opt-out, not opt-in). When OFF, the `TelemetryDeckTransport.send(_:)` path short-circuits before invoking `TelemetryDeck.signal(...)`, so no events leave the device. Toggling ON/OFF takes effect on the next `send(_:)` call (the flag is read at send-time, not cached). The accessibility identifier is `settings-toggle-telemetry`.
- **AC-36.6 (Privacy posture).** When AC-36.5's telemetry toggle is OFF, **no analytics events** are sent to TelemetryDeck — not `appOpen`, not `screenView`, not `recipeView`, not `recipeRated`, not `recipeCommentSubmitted`, not `widgetOpened`, not any future event. The short-circuit happens inside `TelemetryDeckTransport` (the production transport implementation), not inside the `Telemetry` facade — so `RecordingTelemetryTransport` (the test fixture) still records every event for L1 assertions, and the gate only applies to the production wire path. Constitution §9's allowlist is unaffected; the events themselves are unchanged in shape and identity, only their delivery to the upstream is gated by the user's opt-out.
- **AC-36.7 (Persistence).** Every row's persisted state round-trips through `UserDefaults` and is read back on subsequent app launches. The Notifications toggle, Appearance picker, Share Format picker, and Telemetry toggle each persist independently — toggling one row does not affect any other. The Clear Cache button has no persisted state of its own (it's an action, not a preference).
- **AC-36.8 (No regression to US-32 or earlier).** The existing US-32 rows (metric-units toggle, About Dutch Oven Daddy link, version footer) are not regressed — they continue to render in the same sections with the same persistence contracts. The gear-icon entry point on the Feed nav bar (AC-32.1) is unchanged. Tab navigation (US-1 / US-2 / US-3 / US-5) is not regressed. AC-6.2's existing `recipeShared` flow is not regressed when the Default Share Format flag is at its default ("Just the link") — the canonical URL share path is byte-identical to today.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- **No new analytics events.** AC-36.6 explicitly gates the existing event delivery; no new event types are introduced. The constitution §9 allowlist is unchanged.
- **No new design tokens.** The expanded Settings rows reuse the existing `DODColor.surface` / `DODColor.surfaceElevated` / `DODColor.label` / `DODColor.labelSecondary` tokens that US-32 / T-550 already applies. The picker / toggle / button styles inherit from the existing `.insetGrouped` list treatment.
- **No new persistence schema.** The five new UserDefaults keys (`dod.settings.notificationsEnabled`, `dod.settings.appearance`, `dod.settings.shareFormat`, `dod.settings.telemetryEnabled`) namespace under the `dod.settings.*` prefix US-32 established; no SwiftData schema bump is required. The `RecipeStore.clearImageCache()` method operates on the existing `CachedImage` rows and the existing `WidgetImageBridge` file mirror — no new entity, no migration.
- **No new wire-format fields.** Widget snapshot wire format, deep-link grammar, App Privacy questionnaire mapping — all unchanged. The telemetry gate is a client-side short-circuit; the TelemetryDeck SDK's wire format is unchanged when events are sent.
- **CL-62** captures the per-row decisions, the considered alternatives, and the coordination notes against T-620 (Download button) and T-640 (Recipes & Articles rename) running in parallel.

---

### US-38 — Recipes & Articles tab + Search tab support a gallery/list layout toggle
**As a** reader scanning the feed or my search results,
**I want** a toggle that flips the recipe-card layout between the current 2-column gallery and a denser single-column list,
**so that** I can quick-scan many recipes at a glance OR see the gallery's richer hero presentation depending on what I'm doing.

Added 2026-05-27 (round-7 backlog "Layout toggle" item, graduated). A new toolbar button on the **Recipes & Articles** tab (next to the gear icon) and on the **Search** tab flips a shared layout preference between two modes: **gallery** (the existing 2-column `LazyVGrid` per CC-9 — the default and pre-T-650 behavior) and **list** (a denser single-column layout — smaller hero thumbnail to the leading edge, title + 1-line excerpt to the right, total-time chip on the trailing edge). The same `@AppStorage("dod.recipeListLayout")`-backed value drives both surfaces, so flipping the layout on one tab flips it on the other. CL-64 captures the per-tap icon convention — per Spencer's explicit direction, the toggle button shows the **CURRENT** layout (gallery → `square.grid.2x2`; list → `list.bullet`), opposite of the typical iOS convention where the icon shows the destination.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-38.1 (Toggle button placement + icon convention).** Both `FeedView` (Recipes & Articles tab) and `SearchView` (Search tab) render a `ToolbarItem` on the trailing edge of the nav bar that hosts a `Button` whose `Image(systemName:)` reflects the **current** layout: `square.grid.2x2` when the persisted layout is `.gallery`, `list.bullet` when it is `.list`. Tapping the button flips the persisted layout to the other case (gallery → list, list → gallery). On the Recipes & Articles tab the toggle sits to the **leading side of the gear icon** on the trailing-edge `ToolbarItemGroup` (so the gear stays at the absolute trailing edge, preserving AC-32.1's gear placement); on the Search tab the toggle is the only trailing-edge toolbar item. The accessibility identifier is `feed-toolbar-layout-toggle` on `FeedView` and `search-toolbar-layout-toggle` on `SearchView`. The accessibility label reads "Layout, gallery" when in gallery (with the action hint "switch to list") and "Layout, list" when in list (with the action hint "switch to gallery") so VoiceOver users get the destination semantic even though the visible icon reflects the current state.
- **AC-38.2 (Persistence + shared state).** The layout preference persists via `@AppStorage("dod.recipeListLayout")` storing a `RecipeListLayout` enum's raw value (`"gallery"` or `"list"`). The default — for users on first launch or migrating from a pre-T-650 install — is `.gallery` (preserves CC-9's 2-column grid behavior). Both `FeedView` and `SearchView` read and write the same key, so toggling the layout on one tab is observed by the other on the next render. The flag also persists across app launches.
- **AC-38.3 (Gallery layout — unchanged from pre-T-650).** When the persisted layout is `.gallery`, both `FeedView` and `SearchView` render the existing `LazyVGrid` with `recipeGridColumns(horizontalSizeClass:)` — 2 columns on compact (iPhone portrait), 3 columns on regular (iPad and large iPhones in landscape) per CC-9. The cell rendering is the existing `RecipeCard` (hero image at top, title + excerpt + time chip below) — byte-identical to today.
- **AC-38.4 (List layout — new dense single-column variant).** When the persisted layout is `.list`, both `FeedView` and `SearchView` render the items in a single-column `LazyVStack` using a new `RecipeCard.ListRow` variant. Each row shows a small (~60pt square) hero thumbnail on the leading edge, the title + 1-line excerpt to the right (the excerpt is truncated to a single line — `.lineLimit(1)`), and the total-time chip on the trailing edge (when `totalTimeDisplay` is non-nil; absent otherwise). Rows are separated by a hairline `Divider` and use the existing `DODColor.surfaceElevated` background (same token the gallery cards use) so the visual identity matches across layouts. Tapping a row routes through the same `recipeCardTap { onSelect(item) }` path the gallery cells use, and long-pressing surfaces the same `recipeCardContextMenu { onSave?(item) }` Save menu (US-34 / AC-34.1). *Amended by CL-178 (T-782, 2026-06-15): the single-column `LazyVStack` is the iPhone (compact) layout only; on iPad (regular) the same `RecipeCard.ListRow` rows tile into a **2-column `LazyVGrid`** (via the new `adaptiveListRows` / `recipeListColumns` helpers) so the dense list fills the wider canvas instead of stretching to one full-width column, and `RecipeCard.ListRow` reads `@Environment(\.horizontalSizeClass)` to give the title **two lines** on iPad (one on iPhone) so the narrower half-width cells don't truncate recipe names. iPhone is byte-identical (the snapshot tests render `.sizeThatFits`, never `.regular`). `RecipeCard.ListRow` was split into `RecipeCard+ListRow.swift` for the file-length cap. Applies to `FeedView.listContent`, `SearchView.listResults`, and `SearchView+IngredientSection.ingredientList`.*
- **AC-38.5 (No regression to US-1 / US-3 / US-12 / US-37).** US-1 (Feed home feed) is not regressed — the default `.gallery` layout is the same `LazyVGrid` US-1 / CC-9 / T-300 specified. US-3 (Search) is not regressed — the default `.gallery` layout preserves the existing search-results grid. US-12 (filter chips + recent searches) is not regressed — the `FilterChipRow` and `IdleSuggestionsView` continue to render above the results regardless of the chosen layout. US-37 (Recipes & Articles tab name + article rendering) is not regressed — articles continue to render in the same `RecipeListItem` row format in either layout (the kind discriminator lives at the detail-screen level per CL-63, not at the list level). AC-1.3 (list row format) is preserved by `.gallery`; `.list` is an additive variant.
- **AC-38.6 (Testing).** L1 unit tests cover the `RecipeListLayout` enum's `@AppStorage` round-trip (default `.gallery` when the key is absent; `.list` after writing the raw value; `.gallery` after writing a malformed value — defensive fallback). L4 snapshot baselines cover both layouts in both feature views in both light + dark on iPhone 13 baseline at default Dynamic Type — `FeedViewSnapshotTests` and `SearchViewSnapshotTests` each gain a `_listLayout_light` and `_listLayout_dark` baseline alongside the existing `_loadedFeed_*` / `_searchResults_*` baselines (the existing baselines continue to lock the `.gallery` default; the new baselines lock the `.list` variant). A `RecipeCard.ListRow` standalone L4 baseline in `DODDesignSystem` snapshot tests pins the row's typography + thumbnail size + time-chip placement.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- **No new analytics events.** The layout toggle is a presentation-only flip — no `layoutChanged` event is emitted (the constitution §9 allowlist is unchanged).
- **No new design tokens.** `RecipeCard.ListRow` reuses the existing `DODColor.surfaceElevated` / `DODColor.label` / `DODColor.labelSecondary` / `DODColor.castIronBrown` / `DODColor.cream` tokens that the gallery `RecipeCard` already applies. The row's vertical padding + leading thumbnail size are new but stay within the existing `DODSpacing` token family (no new spacing constant).
- **No new persistence schema.** The `@AppStorage` key `dod.recipeListLayout` round-trips a `RecipeListLayout` enum's raw value — no SwiftData schema bump. The key is namespaced under `dod.*` (the same prefix the Settings keys use) for consistency, though it lives outside the `dod.settings.*` prefix because the toggle isn't a Settings-page preference (it's an in-tab control surfaced where the user is actively browsing).
- **No new wire-format fields, no new deep-link grammar, no new widget surfaces.** The toggle is bounded entirely to `FeedView` + `SearchView` + the new `RecipeListLayout` enum + the new `RecipeCard.ListRow` row variant.
- **CL-64** captures the icon-convention deviation from typical iOS (icon reflects current, not destination), the considered alternatives (destination-icon convention, segmented control, Settings-page row, separate per-tab persistence), Spencer's explicit direction, and the rationale for hosting the enum + key in `DODDesignSystem` rather than `DODFeatureFeed` (so `SearchView` doesn't need a feature-package dependency on Feed).
