### US-39 — Shopping list from saved recipes
**As a** home cook with saved recipes,
**I want** to combine the ingredients from one or more saved recipes into a single grouped shopping list — sorted by store aisle, with each item checkable as I shop — and share that list as iMessage text,
**so that** I can hand it to whoever's going to the store. The list lives entirely on my device; no blog account, no sync.

Added 2026-05-27 (round-3 dad backlog item, graduated per Spencer's round-8 batching note). A new local-only shopping-list surface inside the Saved tab. The flow: user taps a saved recipe (or several), picks "Add to shopping list," and the ingredients are appended to a single global list grouped by store aisle (Produce / Dairy / Meat & Seafood / Pantry / Spices / Bakery / Frozen / Beverages / Other). Each row is checkable with a strikethrough toggle ("I have this"), the whole list can be wiped via "Clear all," and the list can be shared as plain text through the iOS share sheet (iMessage / AirDrop / Mail / Notes / Copy — anything `UIActivityViewController` surfaces). Persistence is local SwiftData (a new `ShoppingListItem` `@Model`); the cross-recipe deduplication rule is **per-recipe rows** for v1 (3 recipes that all call for "yellow onion, diced" produce 3 separate rows, each labeled with its source recipe), the aisle classifier is a static keyword map for v1 (with a documented escalation path to a tagged-ingredient dictionary if telemetry shows a high miss rate), and the only telemetry is two new aisle-only events (`shoppingListItemAdded(recipeID:aisle:)` and `shoppingListShared(itemCount:)`) — raw ingredient text never leaves the device.

The full design (12 decisions covering aisle classifier, list lifecycle, quantity merging, cross-recipe dedup, "I have this" scope, share format, entry surfaces, persistence shape, analytics, empty-state copy, location-based-notifications deferral, and iPad layout) is captured in **CL-67 through CL-78**. Three deferred-by-design items are explicitly out of scope for v1: (a) multiple named lists ("Sunday dinner" vs "Camping trip"), (b) a persistent pantry inventory that pre-checks items the user said they already had, and (c) geofenced grocery-store reminders. Each of those has a documented activation trigger (user-feedback signal or telemetry threshold) for a future Tier-2 task.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-39.1 (Empty state).** When the shopping list contains zero `ShoppingListItem` rows, `ShoppingListView` renders the existing `EmptyState` component (the same one used by `SavedView`'s empty path per AC-5.8) with `title: "Your shopping list is empty"`, `message: "Tap a saved recipe and add its ingredients here"`, and `systemImage: "cart"`. The empty state respects Dynamic Type up to AX5 per CC-1 and matches the warm-cream + dark-brown palette in both light + dark per the existing `DODColor.surface` / `DODColor.label` tokens. The "Clear all" toolbar button is hidden when the list is empty (no destructive action to offer); the share toolbar button is also hidden in the empty state.

- **AC-39.2 (Add from a single saved recipe).** From the recipe-detail screen (any post with `kind == .recipe` per US-37 / AC-37.2), a new nav-bar action — SF Symbol `cart.badge.plus`, label "Add to shopping list" — sits to the trailing edge of the toolbar group alongside Save (AC-4.7), Download (AC-35.1), and Share (AC-4.8). Tapping iterates `recipe.ingredients` (the parsed JSON-LD `recipeIngredient` array per AC-4.2), classifies each via the static aisle keyword map (CL-67 / `IngredientAisleClassifier`), inserts one `ShoppingListItem` row per ingredient (recipe ID + raw ingredient text + classified `Aisle` enum + recipe-title sub-label + `addedAt` timestamp + `isChecked: false`), and presents a transient snackbar `"<N> ingredients added"` where `<N>` is the count just inserted. The button is disabled (greyed) when `recipe.ingredients` is empty (article posts per US-37 / AC-37.3 always have empty ingredients — the button is hidden entirely on `ArticleDetailView`).

- **AC-39.3 (Add from multiple saved recipes — bulk path).** The Saved tab gains a new trailing-edge toolbar button (SF Symbol `cart.badge.plus`, label "Build shopping list") that enters a selection mode: each `RecipeCard` (or `RecipeCard.ListRow` per AC-38.4 if the user is in `.list` layout) gains a small leading-edge checkbox circle; the user taps to select N saved recipes, then taps a confirm button ("Add N recipes →") in the same toolbar slot. The confirm path iterates each selected recipe's ingredients and appends rows the same way AC-39.2 does for a single recipe, then transitions out of selection mode and presents `"<total>` ingredients from <N> recipes added"`. The selection-mode toggle uses the per-row long-press menu established by US-34 / AC-34.1 as a secondary entry surface — long-pressing any saved recipe surfaces a "Add to shopping list" item alongside the existing "Save" item (when the card is in saved-tab context; the long-press menu's other entry points per AC-34.1 don't gain the new menu item, only the Saved tab's cards do).

- **AC-39.4 (Aisle grouping — render).** `ShoppingListView` renders `ShoppingListItem` rows grouped into sections by their `Aisle` enum value, in a deterministic store-walk order: Produce → Dairy → Meat & Seafood → Bakery → Frozen → Pantry → Spices → Beverages → Other. Each section uses the existing list-row treatment (`DODColor.surfaceElevated` background, `DODColor.label` text); the section header uses a new `AisleSectionHeader` component in `DODDesignSystem` showing the aisle name + an SF Symbol glyph per aisle (`Produce → leaf`, `Dairy → drop`, `Meat & Seafood → fish`, `Bakery → birthday.cake`, `Frozen → snowflake`, `Pantry → cabinet`, `Spices → flame`, `Beverages → cup.and.saucer`, `Other → cart`). Empty aisles (no rows in that bucket) do not render at all — the section list shows only aisles with at least one row. The "Other" bucket is the fallback for any ingredient whose tokenization does not match any keyword in the v1 static map; this bucket is intentionally permissive (catches unknown spices, brand names, regional terms) and the CL-67 escalation path activates if the "Other" share of rows exceeds the documented threshold.

- **AC-39.5 (Per-row "I have this" toggle).** Each `ShoppingListItem` row has a leading-edge tappable circle (SF Symbol `circle` when unchecked, `checkmark.circle.fill` when checked) bound to the row's `isChecked` boolean. Tapping flips the boolean, which (a) toggles a strikethrough text decoration on the ingredient + sub-label (`.strikethrough(true)` via `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.labelSecondary)`), and (b) persists the new value via `try modelContext.save()`. The toggle is **session-scoped only in semantic** (CL-71) — there is no pantry inventory in v1, no cross-list memory of "user always has olive oil" — but it **does persist** across app launches because the row itself is persisted (a checked row stays checked even after the user backgrounds the app). The single explicit "session" semantic is that the row's `isChecked` state is reset to `false` only by a deliberate user action (tapping the toggle again or clearing the list), never automatically. The accessibility identifier is `shopping-list-row-toggle`.

- **AC-39.6 (Bulk clear).** A "Clear all" toolbar button (SF Symbol `trash`, role `.destructive`) is rendered on the trailing edge of `ShoppingListView`'s nav bar whenever the list is non-empty. Tapping presents a `.confirmationDialog` with destructive copy ("Clear shopping list?" + "Clear all <N> items" destructive button + "Cancel"). Confirming deletes every `ShoppingListItem` row from SwiftData via a single `modelContext.delete(_:)` loop wrapped in one `try modelContext.save()` round-trip; the view returns to the AC-39.1 empty state on the next observation tick. The accessibility identifier is `shopping-list-toolbar-clear-all`.

- **AC-39.7 (Share via `UIActivityViewController` plain text).** A "Share" toolbar button (SF Symbol `square.and.arrow.up`) on the leading edge of `ShoppingListView`'s nav bar opens the system share sheet wrapped around `UIActivityViewController` with a single plain-text item built by `ShoppingListFormatter.shareText(_:)`. The text format is deterministic: a one-line header `"Shopping list — <date>"` (the date is `Date.now` formatted as `.dateTime.month(.abbreviated).day().year()`), one blank line, then per-aisle blocks rendered as the aisle name followed by `- <ingredient text> (<recipe title>)` lines (one line per row, checked-state-irrelevant — the share copy is always the full list, not just unchecked items, per CL-72). Aisles render in the same AC-39.4 order. The share sheet surfaces every iOS share target — iMessage, AirDrop, Mail, Notes, Copy, third-party apps — without the app needing a `MessageUI` framework dependency or any custom URL scheme. CL-72 captures the rationale.

- **AC-39.8 (Persistence across app launches).** Every `ShoppingListItem` row is persisted via SwiftData (the new `ShoppingListItem` `@Model` class declared in `DODPersistence`, added to the schema models array as the only V3 → V4 stage delta per CL-74). Closing and reopening the app preserves every row's `recipeID`, `recipeTitle`, `ingredientText`, `aisle` (the `Aisle` enum's raw value), `isChecked`, and `addedAt` timestamp. The persistence layer is the only source of truth — `ShoppingListView`'s view-model reads rows via `@Query` against the model context, sorted by `addedAt` descending within each aisle group; there is no in-memory cache that could drift from the persisted state. Reinstalling the app wipes the list (same trade-off as US-5 saves per CL-5 — no iCloud sync in v1).

- **AC-39.9 (iPad split-view layout).** On iPad (regular horizontal size class) `ShoppingListView` participates in the existing Saved tab's `NavigationSplitView` per CC-8 — the shopping list lives in the **content** column when the user activates it from the Saved tab's sidebar (a new "Shopping list" row at the top of the sidebar list, above the saved recipes), or as a destination of the per-recipe "Add to shopping list" nav-bar action from the detail column. On iPhone (compact horizontal size class) the shopping list is reached by tapping the new toolbar button on the Saved tab's nav bar (SF Symbol `cart`, leading edge); the list pushes onto the existing navigation stack and pops back via the standard back button. CL-78 captures the rationale for matching `SavedView`'s split-view shape rather than introducing a new top-level tab.

- **AC-39.10 (Analytics — aisle-only).** Two new telemetry events are added to the constitution §9 allowlist via the CL-75 amendment: (1) `shoppingListItemAdded(recipeID:aisle:)` — fires once per row inserted via AC-39.2 or AC-39.3 (so a 12-ingredient recipe add produces 12 events); the payload is the integer recipe ID + the `Aisle` enum's raw string (`"produce"` / `"dairy"` / `"meatSeafood"` / `"bakery"` / `"frozen"` / `"pantry"` / `"spices"` / `"beverages"` / `"other"`). (2) `shoppingListShared(itemCount:)` — fires once when the user taps "Share" per AC-39.7 and the `UIActivityViewController` is presented; the payload is the integer item count at share time. Both events route through the existing `TelemetryDeckTransport` and are gated by the AC-36.5 opt-out toggle the same way every other event is. **No raw ingredient text is ever sent** — the aisle string is a coarse-grained category (~9 possible values), not a per-ingredient identifier; the recipe ID is already telemetered via `recipeView` / `recipeSaved`. The "Other" aisle string is the only signal the static keyword map's coverage is improving or regressing over time, which is the CL-67 escalation trigger.

- **AC-39.11 (VoiceOver labels).** Each `ShoppingListItem` row's accessibility label combines the ingredient text + the aisle name + the recipe-title attribution, in that order: `"<ingredient text>, <aisle>, from <recipe title>"`. The leading toggle has an accessibility action `"Mark as already have"` (when unchecked) or `"Mark as still need"` (when checked); the SwiftUI `Toggle`'s implicit accessibility traits propagate `.isSelected` on the checked state. The "Clear all" button has the accessibility label `"Clear all shopping list items"` (the role-destructive trait is inherited from `Button(role: .destructive)`). The "Share" button uses `"Share shopping list"`. The empty-state image (`cart` glyph) has `.accessibilityHidden(true)` so VoiceOver reads only the title + message; the title is announced first as the screen's primary content per CC-1.

- **AC-39.12 (Privacy — no network egress beyond telemetry).** The shopping list feature does not POST, GET, or otherwise contact dutchovendaddy.com or any other network endpoint for the ingredient text, the list contents, the share payload, or the user's check state. The only network egress associated with this feature is the two TelemetryDeck events per AC-39.10 (aisle category + recipe ID + item count, no raw text). Adding shoppingListShared/Added events does **not** change the App Privacy questionnaire (constitution §9) — both events fall under the existing "Usage Data — Product Interaction" category, the only change to §9's allowlist is the addition of the two event names. **REG-23** locks this contract: an L1 test asserts that `ShoppingListView`'s view model and `ShoppingListItem` insertion path make zero `URLSession` / `WPRestClient` / `WPRMClient` calls; an L2 nightly test asserts the existing live-API integration suite continues to make exactly the GETs it makes today, with no new dutchovendaddy.com hostnames or paths appearing in the request log when the shopping list is exercised via a deterministic fixture.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- **Analytics §9 allowlist amendment.** Two new events (`shoppingListItemAdded(recipeID:aisle:)`, `shoppingListShared(itemCount:)`) join the existing allowlist. No raw user input strings; aisle is a coarse-grained ~9-value enum string. Constitution §9 is amended in the same PR cluster (T-687) to add the two event names to the explicit list; the App Privacy questionnaire mapping table is unchanged because both events fall under the existing "Usage Data — Product Interaction" row.
- **Persistence schema bump.** Schema V3 → V4 stage adds the new `ShoppingListItem` `@Model` to the models array. Per the SchemaV4.swift in-repo note, **adding a new `@Model` class is fundamentally different from adding an optional column to an existing class** — the new class introduces a new schema identity (not a duplicate-checksum collision risk), so the formal V3 → V4 stage is the correct path here, not the inline-additive trick the T-640 article-body column used. Migration is lightweight (the new entity has zero rows at upgrade time, and no existing entity is touched). CL-74 captures the migration discussion and the L1 migration test.
- **New design-system primitives.** `ShoppingListRow` and `AisleSectionHeader` join `DODDesignSystem/Components/`. Each gets a standalone L4 snapshot baseline (light + dark, default Dynamic Type) and the existing iPhone-13 + iPad-12.9 + AX5 baselines per the L4 contract.
- **Package placement.** A new `DODFeatureShoppingList` Swift package houses `ShoppingListView` + `ShoppingListViewModel` + the `IngredientAisleClassifier` consumer wiring. The package depends on `DODDomain` + `DODSupport` + `DODDesignSystem` + `DODPersistence` + `DODAnalytics`. The Saved tab's entry-point toolbar button lives in `DODFeatureSaved` (which gains an `import DODFeatureShoppingList` dependency); the recipe-detail nav-bar action lives in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail` (which also gains the dependency). CL-73 captures the package-vs-fold trade-off and the dependency wiring.
- **CL-67..CL-78** capture every design decision and the considered alternatives. **CL-68** (location-based notifications deferred), **CL-72** (single global list, no named lists), and **CL-71** (per-row session, no pantry inventory) are the three explicit-deferral entries; the rest document v1 paths.

---

### US-40 — Cook Mode supports hands-free Voice Mode with Siri voice commands
**As a** home cook with my hands covered in flour,
**I want** Cook Mode to read the current step aloud and let me say "next step / previous / repeat / pause" to Siri,
**so that** I can move through a recipe without touching (and smearing) my phone.

Added 2026-05-27 (round-3 dad backlog item "Cooking Voice Mode", graduated per Spencer's round-8 batching note). A new opt-in **Voice Mode** layer on top of the existing Cook Mode (US-7) + Live Activity (US-11) surfaces. When enabled, an on-device `AVSpeechSynthesizer` reads the current step's text aloud using the system-default voice + locale, re-reading automatically whenever the step changes (whether the user advances by tapping the existing Cook Mode controls or by a Siri voice command). Four App Intent-backed voice commands — **next step / previous step / repeat / pause** — drive Cook Mode hands-free. The synthesizer activates a `.playback` + `.duckOthers` audio session so background music/podcasts duck (not stop) while a step is read, and one new analytics event (`voiceModeToggled`) records adoption. Everything is on-device: no network round-trip, no subscription, no new privacy surface (parallel to the on-device idle-timer note in constitution §9). CL-79 captures the synthesizer + voice/locale + audio-session decisions.

**Delivery split (two tasks).** This story ships in two tasks. **T-690a** delivers only the standalone, unit-tested `VoiceReader` core (the `AVSpeechSynthesizer` wrapper + audio-session management + the injectable `SpeechSynthesizing` seam) — it touches no Cook Mode UI and registers no App Intents. **T-690b** wires `VoiceReader` into `CookModeView` / `CookModeViewModel` (the Voice Mode toggle + the re-read-on-step-change driver), registers the four `RecipeAppIntents`, and adds the `voiceModeToggled` analytics event. The acceptance criteria below are written against the full US-40 surface; each AC notes whether it lands in **T-690a** (the reader core) or **T-690b** (the Cook Mode wiring + intents) so the trace stays unambiguous across the two PRs.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- **AC-40.1 (Voice Mode toggle in Cook Mode — T-690b).** Cook Mode (US-7 / `CookModeView`) gains a Voice Mode toggle on its control bar — an SF Symbol `speaker.wave.2` button (filled `speaker.wave.2.fill` when Voice Mode is on) with accessibility identifier `cook-mode-voice-toggle` and accessibility label "Voice Mode" (action hint "read steps aloud" when off, "stop reading steps aloud" when on). Tapping flips a `@State`/view-model `isVoiceModeEnabled` boolean. Voice Mode is **off by default** every time Cook Mode is entered (it is not persisted across cook sessions — a deliberate v1 choice per CL-79 so the app never starts talking unexpectedly). Turning Voice Mode on immediately reads the current step per AC-40.2; turning it off calls `VoiceReader.stop()` and deactivates the audio session.
- **AC-40.2 (Read current step aloud — T-690a reader core; T-690b wiring).** When Voice Mode is on, the current Cook Mode step's instruction text is spoken via `VoiceReader.speak(_:)`, which wraps `AVSpeechSynthesizer` and uses the **system-default voice for the device's current locale** (`AVSpeechSynthesisVoice(language:)` resolved from `Locale.current`, falling back to the system default when no localized voice is available — see CL-79). The `VoiceReader` core (speak/stop/pause/resume + audio-session management) is delivered and unit-tested in T-690a; the wiring that feeds the current step's text into `speak(_:)` lands in T-690b.
- **AC-40.3 (Re-read on step change — T-690b).** Whenever the current step changes while Voice Mode is on — via the existing on-screen Next/Previous controls (AC-7.4), via a Siri voice command (AC-40.5), or via Cook Mode landing on its first step when Voice Mode is toggled on — `VoiceReader` is asked to speak the new step. Because `VoiceReader.speak(_:)` stops any in-flight utterance before starting the new one (AC-40.7), advancing two steps quickly never overlaps two voices; the user always hears the step they landed on. Reaching the Done state (AC-7.4 `isFinished`) speaks a short completion line ("All done — enjoy your meal") rather than a step body.
- **AC-40.4 (Pause / resume reading — T-690a reader core).** `VoiceReader` exposes `pause()` (pauses the current utterance at the next word boundary) and `resume()` (continues a paused utterance). The "pause" voice command (AC-40.5) calls `pause()`; issuing "repeat" or any navigation command after a pause calls `speak(_:)` afresh (which implicitly stops the paused utterance first). The pause/resume state machine is delivered + unit-tested in T-690a.
- **AC-40.5 (Four voice commands via App Intents — T-690b).** Four `AppIntent`s register Siri phrases that drive Cook Mode hands-free: **Next step** ("next step"), **Previous step** ("previous step" / "go back"), **Repeat** ("repeat that" / "what was that"), and **Pause** ("pause"). Each intent targets the active Cook Mode session: Next/Previous invoke the same `goNext()` / `goBack()` the on-screen controls use (so the step change flows through AC-40.3's re-read path), Repeat re-speaks the current step via `VoiceReader.speak(_:)`, and Pause calls `VoiceReader.pause()`. The intents donate to the system so Siri can surface them; the phrase set + the App Shortcuts registration is captured in T-690b's scope and traces to the existing deep-link / App Intent infrastructure (REG-10). The four intents land in **T-690b**, not T-690a.
- **AC-40.6 (Audio ducking — T-690a reader core).** Before the first utterance, `VoiceReader` activates the shared `AVAudioSession` with category `.playback` and option `.duckOthers` so other audio (music, podcasts) **ducks** (lowers volume) rather than stops while a step is read, then restores on `stop()` (the session is deactivated with `.notifyOthersOnDeactivation` so the ducked audio returns to full volume). The audio-session category + option choice is locked by CL-79. On platforms without `AVAudioSession` (the package's macOS `swift test` slice), the session calls are compiled out behind `#if os(iOS)` so the reader still builds and its state machine stays unit-testable. Delivered in **T-690a**.
- **AC-40.7 (Speaking a new step interrupts the prior utterance — T-690a reader core).** `VoiceReader.speak(_:)` stops any in-flight or paused utterance before enqueueing the new text (via `stopSpeaking(at: .immediate)` on the underlying synthesizer), so the reader never stacks overlapping voices when steps change rapidly. This is the core interruption contract the re-read path (AC-40.3) depends on and is delivered + unit-tested in **T-690a** with a mock synthesizer (asserting that speaking new text while already speaking issues a stop before the new speak).
- **AC-40.8 (Telemetry — `voiceModeToggled` — T-690b).** One new analytics event, `voiceModeToggled(recipeID: Int, enabled: Bool)`, fires when the user flips the Voice Mode toggle (AC-40.1) — `enabled: true` on turn-on, `false` on turn-off. The payload is the integer recipe ID (already telemetered via `recipeView` per the §9 allowlist) plus the boolean state; **no step text, no spoken content, and no transcript ever leaves the device** — the synthesis is fully on-device and the only egress is this coarse adoption signal. The event routes through the existing `TelemetryDeckTransport`, is gated by the AC-36.5 opt-out toggle like every other event, and joins the constitution §9 allowlist via the CL-79 amendment. The four voice commands (AC-40.5) emit **no** telemetry (a command is a navigation action already covered by the step-change UX, not a separately-tracked event). Lands in **T-690b** alongside the toggle.
- **AC-40.9 (Natural-voice quality by default — T-720).** When Voice Mode reads a step, the resolved voice is the **best installed quality tier** for the device language — `premium` (neural) preferred over `enhanced` preferred over `default` (the basic concatenative "robotic" tier). This supersedes AC-40.2's `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice(language:)` resolution, which returned the *default*-quality voice for a language **even when an `enhanced` Siri voice was already installed** — the confirmed cause of the round-9 "Cook Mode voice sounds like a robot" report. The selection enumerates the live `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice.speechVoices()` catalog and explicitly reaches past the compact tier. **Graceful degradation:** if the locale has no installed voices at all, the resolver falls back to the prior `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice(language:)` behavior, then to the platform default — so a no-voices locale degrades exactly as before, never worse. The premium/enhanced tiers ship with iOS for common locales (en-US Siri voices in particular); locales whose better tiers require an on-device download surface the **"Download more voices"** deep-link in Phase b (T-721). Delivered + unit-tested in **T-720** via the AVFoundation-free `VoiceSelector` over a value-type `VoiceDescriptor` catalog.
- **AC-40.10 (Gender preference — T-720).** The resolver honors a user **gender preference** (`male` / `female` / `unspecified`) read from `UserDefaults` key `dod.voice.preferredGenderV1` (default `female`, matching the platform default voice). The selection is **gender-primary, quality-secondary**: among language matches it prefers the requested gender, and within that prefers the highest quality tier; an `unspecified`-gender voice is an acceptable fallback (ranked above the opposite gender) so a natural neutral voice still beats a wrong-gender one when the requested gender has no installed voice. The **Settings → "Cook Mode voice" picker** that lets the user flip this preference **shipped in T-721** (2026-05-30, after T-703 / PR #72 + the design-tokens PR #86 landed): a Female / Male / No-preference `Picker` bound to `SettingsViewModel.voiceGender` over the same `VoicePreferenceStore` the engine reads (acc. id `settings-picker-voice-gender`). The advanced Phase-b controls — the per-locale specific-voice picker, the "Download more voices" deep-link, and adoption analytics — remain deferred under AC-40.12+ (CL-122). T-720 ships the engine + the preference store the picker binds to; the default-female preference means the quality upgrade (AC-40.9) lands immediately without any UI.
- **AC-40.11 (Preference persistence contract — T-720).** `VoicePreferenceStore` reads + writes the gender preference at the canonical `UserDefaults` key `dod.voice.preferredGenderV1`. Unset → `female`. An unrecognized stored value → `female` (forward-compatible default). The store is the single seam the Phase-b Settings toggle binds to; the engine reads it at every utterance so a preference change applies to the next step read without restarting Cook Mode. The preference is **device-local, never synced, never telemetered** — no new privacy surface (parallel to the AC-40.8 on-device-only posture; the gender choice never leaves the device).

**Constitution + spec notes:**

- **Phase a / Phase b split (T-720 / T-721 — added 2026-05-29 per round-9 backlog).** The round-9 "Cook Mode voice sounds robotic + male/female toggle in Settings" backlog item graduates as two tasks under this US. **T-720 (Phase a)** ships the voice-quality + gender-selection **engine** — the `VoiceSelector` algorithm, the `VoiceDescriptor` / `VoiceGender` / `VoiceQuality` / `VoicePreference` value types, the `VoicePreferenceStore`, and the `SystemSpeechSynthesizer` wiring that replaces the compact-voice resolution — with zero `SettingsView` / DesignSystem touch, so it carries no conflict with the open PRs #72 / #86. The default-female preference makes the quality upgrade land immediately with no UI. **T-721 (Phase b)** ships the **Settings → Voice picker** (gender toggle + automatic/specific-voice mode + "Download more voices" deep-link) once #72 + #86 land. AC-40.9..AC-40.11 are tagged T-720; the Phase-b picker ACs (AC-40.12+) are reserved. CL-109 captures the gender-primary-vs-quality-primary decision + the deferral rationale. *Amended by CL-196 (T-802 / DUT-115, 2026-06-20): the Preview button's fixed sample line — the `SettingsViewModel.voicePreviewSampleLine` constant, pinned by `SettingsViewModelVoiceTests` — changed from a "preheat your Dutch oven" line to a real recipe step ("Layer the noodles, then spread a third of the meat mixture evenly over the top.") per Spencer, since preheating a Dutch oven is not a real Dutch-oven technique.*
- **No new dependency, no new privacy surface.** The engine uses only `AVFoundation` (Apple-system; the same framework AC-40.2 already used) — no third-party TTS SDK (constitution §3 default-no respected). The gender preference is a single device-local `UserDefaults` boolean-equivalent; nothing is sent to TelemetryDeck and the App Privacy questionnaire is unchanged (parallel to AC-40.8).
- **Delivery split.** T-690a ships the `VoiceReader` core only (the `AVSpeechSynthesizer` wrapper + the `SpeechSynthesizing` protocol seam + audio-session management + L1 tests with a mock synthesizer). T-690b ships the Cook Mode wiring (toggle + re-read driver), the four `RecipeAppIntents`, and the `voiceModeToggled` analytics event. ACs are tagged above with their owning task; nothing in T-690a touches `CookModeView`, `CookModeViewModel`, `RecipeAppIntents`, or `AnalyticsEvent`.
- **On-device synthesis — no new network or privacy surface.** Speech synthesis uses `AVSpeechSynthesizer`, which runs entirely on-device. No audio, transcript, or step text is sent to any server. The only telemetry is the single `voiceModeToggled` adoption event (recipe ID + boolean) added in T-690b; the App Privacy questionnaire (constitution §9 / CC-6) is unchanged because the event falls under the existing "Usage Data — Product Interaction" row. Parallel to the on-device idle-timer note in §9 and the Live Activity payload note in §2.
- **Platform guards.** `AVSpeechSynthesizer` is available on both iOS and macOS, but `AVAudioSession` is iOS-only. `VoiceReader`'s audio-session activation/deactivation is wrapped in `#if os(iOS)` (mirroring the ActivityKit guard pattern in `SystemCookLiveActivityController` per constitution §2 / AC-11.4) so the package still compiles on the macOS `swift test` slice and the reader's speak/stop/pause/resume state machine stays unit-testable without real audio hardware.
- **Injectable synthesizer for testability.** `VoiceReader` depends on a `SpeechSynthesizing` protocol (methods `speak / stop / pause / continue`, plus an `isSpeaking` / `isPaused` state surface) rather than concretely on `AVSpeechSynthesizer`. The production path uses a thin adapter that conforms `AVSpeechSynthesizer` to the protocol; L1 tests inject a mock so the reader's state transitions are exercised with zero real-audio dependency. This mirrors the `CookLiveActivityController` protocol seam (US-11) that lets `CookModeViewModel` build + test on macOS.
- **Analytics §9 allowlist amendment (lands in T-690b).** One new event (`voiceModeToggled(recipeID:enabled:)`) joins the existing allowlist. No raw user input, no spoken content — only the recipe ID + a boolean. Constitution §9 is amended in the T-690b PR (not T-690a, which ships no analytics) to add the event name; the App Privacy questionnaire mapping table is unchanged (the event falls under the existing "Usage Data — Product Interaction" row).
- **CL-79** captures the `AVSpeechSynthesizer`-vs-alternatives decision, the system-default-voice + `Locale.current` choice (and the no-in-app-voice-picker v1 trade-off), the `.playback` + `.duckOthers` audio-session category/option rationale (ducking vs. stopping other audio, and why not `.ambient` / `.soloAmbient`), and the Voice-Mode-off-by-default + not-persisted decision.

---

### US-41 — Cross-device sync via CloudKit
**As a** Dutch Oven Daddy reader with an iPhone and an iPad (or a new device on upgrade),
**I want** my saved recipes to follow me across my Apple devices without me having to think about it,
**so that** I can save on my phone Saturday morning, walk to the kitchen, and pick up on my iPad — and my device still works exactly the same if I'm not signed into iCloud or if I decline sync.

Added 2026-05-28 (round-6 Spencer backlog item "Login + account system (Google + Apple + email)", graduated after the dad-led architecture conversation Spencer paused on per the round-6 capture). The original framing assumed a tri-provider OAuth UX (Google + Apple + email) with WordPress-side account creation; this US **replaces** that framing with a CloudKit private-database sync model — there is no "account" in our app, no OAuth providers, no password handling, and no login UI. The user's iCloud account *is* the sync identity, the CloudKit container is the sync surface, and a Settings → iCloud Sync toggle is the only opt-in. The four constitutional sections touched by the original OAuth framing (§1 / §3 / §4 / §9 — flagged in the round-6 backlog entry as "**Constitutional conflicts to surface BEFORE any code**") are amended in this same PR cluster, but the amendments are dramatically simpler than the OAuth variant: §1 acknowledges a v2 sync model without changing "no accounts in v1"; §3 adds `CloudKit.framework` (Apple-provided, no third-party dep) to the allow-list; §4 adds a "Synced records" data-boundary category; §9 adds four new analytics events without changing the App Privacy questionnaire (per CL-94 — CloudKit data is the user's, not ours). The CloudKit container identifier `iCloud.com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp` matches the bundle-ID prefix convention (App Store Connect provisioning lands in T-701, paired with the entitlements wiring per CL-93). CL-86 captures the considered alternatives (WordPress users / Firebase-Supabase / local-only + manual iCloud sync) and the trail to CloudKit; CL-87 documents why CloudKit replaces OAuth (and what a future Android port would need); CL-88 scopes which records sync (saved recipes only — `PersistentShoppingListItem` defer per CL-88 sub-decision since T-682's schema bump has not landed); CL-89 the opt-in flow; CL-90 the conflict-resolution rule; CL-91 offline behavior; CL-92 the "account deletion" semantics that satisfy Apple 5.1.1(v); CL-93 entitlements + container provisioning; CL-94 the App Privacy nutrition-label delta; CL-95 the privacy-policy paragraph; CL-96 the four analytics events + §9 amendment; CL-97 backward-compatibility for existing v1.0 users; CL-98 the Google Play / Android non-applicability deferral; CL-99 (added per CL-89's "future-self-friendly" trail) the CloudKit subscription strategy.

**Delivery split (nine tasks, T-700..T-708).** This story ships in nine PRs. **T-700** is this spec-only amendment — the docs that the implementation PRs cite. **T-701** lands the CloudKit container provisioning (App Store Connect setup + the two new entitlement keys `com.apple.developer.icloud-container-identifiers` + `com.apple.developer.icloud-services`) — no application code. **T-702** wires SwiftData → CloudKit sync via the iOS 17+ `ModelConfiguration(_:groupContainer:cloudKitDatabase:)` initializer (CL-88's chosen integration). **T-703** ships the Settings → iCloud Sync toggle (UI + persistence + view-model). **T-704** ships the first-launch opt-in prompt (modal sheet per CL-89). **T-705** ships the offline queue + retry + the "Last synced N ago" status surface in Settings (per CL-91). **T-706** ships conflict resolution per CL-90 + tests. **T-707** ships the four new analytics events + the constitution §9 amendment (per CL-96). **T-708** ships the L1 + L3 + L5 tests (E2E journey: enable sync → save a recipe → confirm upload via a mocked CloudKit container → disable sync → confirm DB cleared, per the AC-T5 / CL-58 L5 contract). The acceptance criteria below are written against the full US-41 surface; each AC notes which task owns it so the trace stays unambiguous across the nine PRs.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- **AC-41.1 (App works fully without iCloud sign-in — T-702 + T-703 + T-704).** Every existing v1.0 feature must function with **identical behavior** when the user is not signed into iCloud, when iCloud Drive is disabled for our app, or when the user explicitly declines sync at the opt-in prompt (AC-41.2). Feed (US-1), Categories (US-2), Search (US-3 / US-12), Recipe Detail (US-4), Save (US-5), Share (US-6), Cook Mode (US-7), Onboarding (US-8), Home-screen + Lock-screen widgets (US-9 / US-17 / US-22), App Intents + Siri Shortcuts (US-10), Live Activity (US-11), Comments + Ratings (US-13 / US-14 / US-15 — including the guest identity Keychain path), Settings (US-32 / US-36), Download for offline (US-35), Long-press Save (US-34), Recipes & Articles rendering (US-37 + US-38), Shopping List (US-39), and Voice Mode (US-40) all work the same way. The local SwiftData store + Keychain + UserDefaults paths are unchanged — sync is a **mirror**, not a replacement. **App Store rule trace: this AC satisfies App Store Review Guideline 5.1.1(ii) ("If your app doesn't include significant account-based features, let people use it without a login").** Verified by the existing L3 smoke + L5 seed journeys passing under a simulator with the user signed out of iCloud (per T-708's coverage).
- **AC-41.2 (Opt-in prompt on first launch after upgrade — T-704).** On the first cold launch after the user upgrades to the version that adds CloudKit sync (gated by a single `UserDefaults` flag `dod.cloudKitSyncOptInPromptShownV1`), the app presents a single-screen, dismissible modal sheet over whatever the user lands on (Feed for new users, last-tab-restored for existing users per the existing tab restoration behavior). The sheet contains a friendly headline ("Sync your saved recipes across devices"), one sentence of context ("Turn on iCloud Sync to see your saved recipes on every Apple device signed into the same iCloud account."), a primary "Turn on iCloud Sync" button, and a secondary "Not now" button. **Tapping primary:** sets `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled = true`, flips the flag, dismisses, and (if the user is signed into iCloud) begins the first sync round-trip; if the user is **not** signed into iCloud, the app instead presents the standard `CKAccountStatus`-based iOS prompt to sign in, and on account-status change the sync activates per the CL-89 retry. **Tapping secondary:** sets `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled = false`, flips the flag, dismisses, and never re-prompts unless the user explicitly toggles sync on from Settings later (AC-41.3) — the sheet does **not** re-prompt on subsequent cold launches. **Future iCloud sign-out + sign-in flow:** when the system detects `CKAccountStatus` transitions from `.noAccount` → `.available` (i.e., the user just signed into iCloud) and the user has previously tapped "Turn on iCloud Sync" (`dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled = true`), sync activates automatically without re-prompting; when the user signs out of iCloud entirely, the local mirror of CloudKit-backed data is cleared per AC-41.5 and the flag is preserved so a future sign-in resumes sync. The sheet's three-bullet structure + dismiss flag mirrors AC-8.1's onboarding pattern. **VoiceOver:** sheet headline and both buttons carry explicit `accessibilityLabel` strings; the sheet's "Not now" path is fully reachable via VoiceOver swipes. *Amended by CL-159 (T-762 / DUT-68, 2026-06-12): the first-launch iCloud-Sync opt-in sheet is **removed**. The first launch now shows exactly ONE sheet — the US-8 / AC-8.1 welcome sheet — which mentions iCloud Sync as a **capability** (a no-toggle informational bullet pointing to Settings); turning sync on is done **only** from the Settings → iCloud Sync toggle (AC-41.3). The `CloudKitOptInPromptGate` (the `dod.cloudKitSyncOptInPromptShownV1` flag) and the `CloudKitOptInSheet` view are deleted; the `-DODForceCloudKitOptInPrompt` UI-test override is gone too. Downstream references to "the opt-in prompt (AC-41.2)" in AC-41.1 / AC-41.10 / AC-41.11 now read as the Settings toggle (AC-41.3) — the informed-consent moment is the explicit Settings opt-in. The `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled` flag + the non-destructive enable/disable behavior (saved recipes stay local when sync is off) are unchanged.*
- **AC-41.3 (Settings → iCloud Sync toggle — T-703).** Settings (US-32 / US-36 / T-630) gains a new row "iCloud Sync" with a SwiftUI `Toggle` bound to a `@AppStorage("dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled")` boolean (default `false`, set to the AC-41.2 prompt's result on first run). The row sits between the existing "Notifications" row (T-630) and the "Appearance" row (T-630) per the Settings ordering documented in CL-62 (the constitution-amendment paragraph in §9 already cites the row order). The toggle has accessibility identifier `settings-icloud-sync-toggle` and accessibility label "iCloud Sync" (action hint "syncs your saved recipes across your Apple devices" when off, "stop syncing your saved recipes across devices" when on). **Toggling on:** activates sync (subject to `CKAccountStatus` — if not available, the row shows a sublabel "Sign into iCloud to enable sync" and tapping it routes to the system Settings → iCloud surface via the `UIApplication.openSettingsURLString` URL); fires `syncEnabled` per AC-41.9. **Toggling off:** disables sync **and** clears the local mirror of CloudKit-backed data per AC-41.5; fires `syncDisabled` per AC-41.9. The row also surfaces a secondary status label (per AC-41.7 — "Last synced 3 minutes ago" / "Sync paused — no network" / "Sync error — tap to retry") below the toggle. **T-750 / CL-147 amendment (DUT-56):** the iCloud Sync `Section` carries **no header** (the toggle row labels itself "iCloud Sync", so a `Section("iCloud Sync")` header was redundant — headers are reserved for multi-row groups), and the state description ("Saved recipes stay on this device." / the across-devices copy) renders as the **section footer** rather than inside the toggle row. **T-752 / CL-149 amendment (DUT-58):** the iCloud Sync rows (`CloudSyncRows` — toggle + Status row) now live inside a shared **"Data & Privacy"** `Section` alongside Clear Cached Recipe Images + Share Anonymous Usage Data (in that order, between the Tools and About sections). The standalone iCloud footer is gone; its state-neutral essence ("Saved recipes sync across your devices when iCloud Sync is on.") folds into the combined Data & Privacy section footer. The state-dependent VoiceOver `accessibilityHint` is retained on the toggle. **T-757 / CL-154 amendment (DUT-63):** every toggle flip is confirmed via a **custom branded `CloudSyncConfirmationDialog`** (a dimmed backdrop + a centered `DODColor.surface` card with a filled `DODColor.castIronBrown` primary button carrying `DODColor.cream` white text, and a plain Cancel) presented via an `.overlay` driven by `cloudSyncConfirmationRequest`, superseding CL-89's system `.alert`. The swap fixes (a) the orange button text (system alerts inherit the app's orange `.tint` with no per-button override) and (b) a race where the system alert's `isPresented`-binding cancel-on-dismiss synchronously reverted the async confirm so the toggle never flipped. The view-model confirm/cancel/request flow is unchanged. **T-759 / CL-156 amendment (DUT-65):** the per-toggle confirmation is removed entirely — the toggle now flips **directly** via `SettingsViewModel.setCloudSyncEnabled(_:)` with no popup (superseding CL-89's "confirm every flip" + CL-154's dialog). Safe because toggling is non-destructive in both directions (disabling switches the same local store to local-only; saved recipes stay on-device) and deferred to next launch, and the one-time AC-41.2 first-launch opt-in sheet remains the informed-consent moment.
- **AC-41.4 (Saved recipes sync via the CloudKit private database — T-702).** `CachedRecipe` rows where `isSaved == true` are mirrored to the user's CloudKit private database via the iOS 17+ `ModelConfiguration(_:groupContainer:cloudKitDatabase:)` initializer (per CL-88 — SwiftData's built-in `cloudKitDatabase: .private(...)` adapter, **not** a custom `CKModifyRecordsOperation` pipeline). The synced fields are exactly the fields the user explicitly cares about being mirrored: `id`, `title`, `excerptText`, `canonicalURLString`, `heroImageURLString`, `heroImageLargeURLString`, `categoryIDs`, `publishedAt`, `isSaved`, `downloadedAt`, `lastViewedAt` (the LRU-ordering hint — per CL-88 a synced field so cross-device "newest-saved-first" stays consistent), and the JSON-encoded detail payload blobs (`ingredientsJSON`, `instructionsJSON`, `nutritionJSON`, `videoJSON`) plus the detail scalars (`prepSeconds`, `cookSeconds`, `totalSeconds`, `servings`, `articleBodyHTML`). The CloudKit container is `iCloud.com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp` (per CL-93). **What does NOT sync (explicit per CL-88):** `CachedListPage` rows (feed-page hydration cache — re-derived from the network), `CachedImage` rows (re-fetched per-device from the CDN — image bytes would balloon the CloudKit storage), `CachedIngredient` rows (re-derived from each device's detail-merge passes — sync would re-hydrate from synced `ingredientsJSON` instead), `CachedComment` + `CachedRating` rows (server-of-truth is the WordPress install, not the device — per CL-88), the Keychain guest identity (per CL-88 — guest identity remains per-device Keychain per US-15 / CL-15, **not** a CloudKit record), the local Search recents in UserDefaults (`dod.recentSearchesV1` — per CL-88, ephemeral per-device surface), the Cook Mode in-flight check state (per AC-4.2 lifetime contract — not persisted across launches, so never synced), the Settings rows other than `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled` itself (per CL-88 — appearance / share-format / opt-out are per-device preferences), and the `PersistentShoppingListItem` SwiftData rows (deferred per CL-88's sub-decision since the SchemaV4 `@Model` has not yet landed in T-682 — the Shopping List remains per-device in v1.x; cross-device sync of the shopping list re-evaluates after T-682 ships and graduates as its own follow-up task per CL-88 with a documented activation trigger). **No CloudKit public or shared database usage in v1.0** (per CL-88 / REG-25 — saved-recipe sharing between users is out of scope and would need a separate spec). **No CloudKit Discoverability API usage in v1.0** (per CL-88 / REG-25 — we don't find friends, we don't ask for the user's name from their iCloud profile, and we don't trigger the system "find friends" permission prompt).
- **AC-41.5 ("Account deletion" semantics — T-702 + T-703).** Two events trigger removal of the user's CloudKit private-DB content for this app: **(1) the user toggles Settings → iCloud Sync off** (AC-41.3); **(2) the system reports the iCloud account is no longer available** (`CKAccountStatus` transitions to `.noAccount` / `.restricted` / `.couldNotDetermine` — i.e., the user signed out of iCloud or revoked iCloud Drive for our app at the system level). In either case, the app **deletes the local mirror of every CloudKit-backed `CachedRecipe`** (the saved-recipe rows themselves — the LRU-policy unsaved rows are unaffected since they were never synced) and `CachedImage` rows that backed those saved recipes' hero images. The Keychain guest identity (US-15), the local SwiftData blocklist signals, the UserDefaults Search recents, the Settings preferences, the Shopping List rows (when T-682 ships them), and the analytics opt-out state are **NOT** affected — those are per-device surfaces that pre-date CloudKit per CL-88. **Why this satisfies App Store Review Guideline 5.1.1(v) ("apps that offer account creation must offer account deletion"):** there is no "account" in our app — the user's iCloud account is theirs, not ours, and Apple has never required apps to "delete the user's iCloud account" because that would be a nonsensical request from a third party. What 5.1.1(v) demands of CloudKit-using apps is that the user can **fully remove the data the app has put into their iCloud space**, and the two trigger events above accomplish exactly that. The Settings row's toggle-off + the system-level iCloud sign-out are the two "delete account" affordances 5.1.1(v) is satisfied by. **No explicit "Delete account" button** is added because there is no account to delete; the toggle off and the system sign-out are the canonical paths. CL-92 captures the rationale and the considered-alternatives trail (a vestigial "Delete account" button, a confirmation sheet, a "delete everything including local saves" nuclear option).
- **AC-41.6 (Offline behavior — T-702 + T-705).** Local SwiftData writes (save, unsave, the JSON-LD detail merge, the explicit download mark, every other `RecipeStore` write path per the existing AC contracts) complete **immediately** regardless of network availability. The SwiftData → CloudKit sync adapter queues changes when the device is offline and flushes them when the network returns. Sync failures retry with exponential backoff (initial delay 30s, doubling up to 8 minutes, then steady-state retries at 8-minute intervals — the default `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer`-style retry shape SwiftData's CloudKit adapter inherits). Persistent failures (3 consecutive retries at the 8-minute steady-state) surface in Settings → iCloud Sync as a sublabel "Sync paused — tap to retry"; tapping the row re-fires the sync request. **The user never sees an error dialog or a loading spinner for sync.** The contract is "local writes are instant; CloudKit catches up when it can." Per CL-91, this is the same posture as iCloud Photos and iCloud Drive — sync is a background concern, never a foreground one. Performance: local writes hit the constitution §8 budgets unchanged (the existing AC-5.2's 5-second pre-download budget is unaffected since CloudKit sync is asynchronous to it).
- **AC-41.7 (Settings status surface — T-705).** Below the Settings → iCloud Sync toggle (AC-41.3), a secondary status label renders one of the following states per CL-91's status taxonomy: **"Last synced N ago"** (when sync is enabled and the last sync round-trip succeeded — N is rounded to "just now" < 60s, "M minutes ago" 1-59m, "H hours ago" 1-23h, "D days ago" ≥ 24h), **"Sync paused — no network"** (when sync is enabled and the device has no connectivity per the existing offline-banner detection in CC-2), **"Sync paused — sign into iCloud"** (when sync is enabled but `CKAccountStatus` is `.noAccount` / `.restricted`), **"Sync paused — tap to retry"** (when sync is enabled and 3 consecutive retries have failed per AC-41.6), **"Sync error — tap to retry"** (when sync is enabled and the most recent attempt failed but retry is still active), or **empty** (when sync is disabled per AC-41.3). The label updates in real time as the sync state changes (via the existing `@Observable` view-model pattern; no polling). The label's VoiceOver string is identical to its visible text. **No raw error codes** ever surface to the user (per CC-4) — the four error states above are the only user-facing surface, and the full `CKError` chain is logged via the existing `Logger` plumbing for support purposes only.
- **AC-41.8 (Conflict resolution: last-write-wins by `modifiedAt` — T-706).** When the same `CachedRecipe` row is modified on two devices between sync round-trips (e.g., the user saves the recipe on iPhone, also saves it on iPad while offline, then both devices come online and try to push the row), the conflict is resolved **last-write-wins by `modifiedAt`** — a new field added to `CachedRecipe` in the same PR cluster as the CloudKit wiring (T-702) that records the local wall-clock time of the most recent write to that row. The device whose write has the higher `modifiedAt` wins; the other device's write is overwritten. Per CL-90, this is the simplest resolution rule consistent with the user mental model for a one-person-multi-device pattern (the user knows whichever change they made *later* should win) and matches what iCloud Notes / iCloud Reminders do. **Defer 3-way merge to v2** (per CL-90's considered alternatives — a field-level diff merger would be the next escalation if user reports of "I lost a check state" emerge). Idempotent fields (e.g., the LRU `lastViewedAt`) use max-value resolution rather than wall-clock LWW so the "newest saw it first" semantic stays correct across devices. Tests pin the LWW contract in `RecipeStore` (`MergeResolutionTests`) and locked by REG-26.
- **AC-41.9 (Analytics events — T-707).** Four new analytics events fire per CL-96: **`syncEnabled`** (when the user turns sync on, either via the AC-41.2 opt-in prompt or the AC-41.3 toggle — payload empty), **`syncDisabled`** (when the user turns sync off via the AC-41.3 toggle — payload empty), **`syncCompletedSuccessfully`** (debounced to fire at most once per 60 seconds when at least one successful CloudKit round-trip has completed — payload empty), **`syncFailed(errorCategory:)`** (when a sync attempt fails after the retry budget per AC-41.6 — `errorCategory` is a closed-set enum string drawn from `network` / `accountStatus` / `quotaExceeded` / `serverInternal` / `other`, **never** the raw `CKError` code or message; the closed-set posture matches `widgetOpened`'s `kind` and `voiceCommandFired`'s `command` per the constitution §9 closed-enum allowlist convention). **No PII** in any payload. **No recipe IDs** in any payload (per CL-96 — sync is a session-level state, not a per-recipe action, so the existing `recipeSaved` event already covers per-recipe granularity and adding it here would double-count). Constitution §9 is amended in the same T-707 PR to add the four event names to the allowlist; the App Privacy questionnaire mapping table is unchanged per CL-94 (the four events fall under the existing "Usage Data — Product Interaction" row, and the synced data itself is **not** "data we collect" per CL-94's CloudKit-specific framing).
- **AC-41.10 (VoiceOver and accessibility — T-703 + T-704).** The Settings → iCloud Sync toggle (AC-41.3) carries `accessibilityLabel("iCloud Sync")` + an action hint that flips on toggle state (off: "syncs your saved recipes across your Apple devices", on: "stop syncing your saved recipes across devices"). The opt-in sheet (AC-41.2) carries `accessibilityLabel` strings on the headline, body copy, primary button ("Turn on iCloud Sync"), and secondary button ("Not now"); the secondary button is reachable via VoiceOver swipes before the user activates the primary. The status sublabel (AC-41.7) carries its visible text as the VoiceOver label. Per CC-1, all three surfaces respect Dynamic Type up to AX5 — the sheet's content scales without truncation, the Settings row's label and sublabel wrap on AX5 rather than truncating, and the toggle remains tappable at AX5 sizing. WCAG AA contrast on both the toggle's track and the status sublabel meets the constitution §7 floor in both light and dark mode.
- **AC-41.11 (Backward compatibility — T-702).** Existing v1.0 users (i.e., users who upgrade from a v1.0 build that pre-dates this PR cluster) keep working unchanged. The migration is **purely additive** — the AC-41.2 opt-in prompt is the only new surface they see on the first cold launch after upgrade, and tapping "Not now" leaves their existing saved recipes, comments, ratings, guest identity, Cook Mode preferences, search recents, Settings rows, and Shopping List rows (when T-682 ships them) **exactly as they were on the previous build**. No data is migrated to CloudKit unless the user opts in. **No destructive schema changes** — the new `modifiedAt` field on `CachedRecipe` is an additive Optional column (lightweight migration per the established R-5 rule in `MIGRATION.md`); the `dod.cloudKitSyncOptInPromptShownV1` + `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled` UserDefaults flags are net-new with safe defaults (`false` for both — never-prompted users and not-opted-in users default identically). Per CL-97, the rollback story is also clean: shipping a future build that removes CloudKit sync entirely would leave the local SwiftData store intact, the Settings row would simply disappear, and the user would not lose saved recipes (the CloudKit data is also still in their iCloud private DB and can be re-mirrored on a future re-enable).
- **AC-41.12 (Privacy policy update — T-700 spec PR captures the copy; operational web update is a TestFlight gate).** The privacy policy at `https://www.dutchovendaddy.com/app-privacy/` is updated to include a new "Optional iCloud Sync" paragraph (the exact text is captured in CL-95 and in the Marketing/AppPrivacy.md delta this PR ships) that documents: what data is synced (saved recipes only — title, image URL, ingredients/instructions blobs, and the saved-state flag), where it lives (the user's own iCloud private database under `iCloud.com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp`), who can see it (only the user, on their own Apple devices signed into the same Apple ID), and how to disable + clear it (Settings → iCloud Sync off, or sign out of iCloud — per AC-41.5). The web update is **operational** — not blocked by this spec PR — but is captured as a **TestFlight gate**: the privacy-policy text must be live on `dutchovendaddy.com/app-privacy/` before the build that turns on CloudKit sync is submitted to TestFlight (per CC-6's App Privacy requirement and the existing TestFlight pre-flight checklist in `Marketing/TestFlight.md`). The Marketing/AppPrivacy.md doc updated in this PR pre-captures the questionnaire delta so the next App Store Connect submission has the correct answers ready (per CL-94: no new data category to declare).
- **AC-41.13 (Regression locks — T-702 + T-708).** Two new REG bullets land alongside this US (locked by tests per AC-T4): **REG-25** locks the "no CloudKit public DB, no CloudKit shared DB, no Discoverability API" contract — the only `CKContainer` API surfaces touched by the app source are `privateCloudDatabase` (via the SwiftData adapter, not directly) and `accountStatus(completionHandler:)`; touching `publicCloudDatabase`, `sharedCloudDatabase`, or `discoverUserIdentity*` would be a privacy regression that the L1 test `CloudKitContainerSurfaceTests.onlyPrivateDatabaseIsAccessed` catches by inspecting the call graph rooted at the `CloudKitSyncAdapter` type. **REG-26** locks the "app works without iCloud sign-in" contract per AC-41.1 — the existing L3 smoke + L5 seed journeys must pass under a simulator with `CKAccountStatus = .noAccount`, locked by an additive case in `SmokeTests.test_appLaunchesUnderNoiCloudAccount` and an additive L5 case in `E2EJourneys.test_fullGoldenPathUnderNoiCloudAccount` (gated by the `e2e` label per CL-58). Both REG bullets join the AC-T4 list when T-708 lands.

**Constitution + spec notes:**

- **Replaces Spencer's round-6 OAuth framing.** The original round-6 backlog entry sized this as XL (~3-4 weeks iOS side + WP backend longer), required four constitution amendments (§1 / §3 / §4 / §9) **with** the GoogleSignIn SDK addition, and bottlenecked on a dad-led architecture conversation. CloudKit dramatically simplifies all four: no Google sign-in SDK (no third-party dep), no WordPress account plugin (no paired web effort), no password handling, no OAuth flow at all. The four §-amendments still happen but are *smaller* — see CL-87 for the full diff. **Spencer's original four CL slots reserved by the round-6 capture** (the round-6 backlog item flagged §1 / §3 / §4 / §9 + CL-5 as constitutional touch points) are still touched, but the diffs are bounded.
- **§1 amendment.** Adds a "v2 sync model" subsection acknowledging CloudKit. Guest identity (US-15) remains unchanged for commenting; CloudKit private DB stores saved recipes per US-41. The "mostly read-only with two write surfaces" framing is preserved — the CloudKit mirror is a sync mechanism, not a new write surface (the user-facing write surfaces remain Save / Comment / Rate per the existing US-13 / US-14 / US-15).
- **§3 amendment.** Adds `CloudKit.framework` to the allow-list. Note that it's **Apple-provided** (no third-party dep, no package-manager footprint, no entitlement-cost beyond the two new iCloud entitlement keys per CL-93). Reaffirms "default answer is no new third-party dep" but documents CloudKit as the deliberate iCloud-sync path. SwiftData's built-in `cloudKitDatabase:` adapter (iOS 17+) means the sync wiring is a single `ModelConfiguration` parameter, not a custom `CKModifyRecordsOperation` pipeline — see CL-88.
- **§4 amendment.** Adds "Synced records" category alongside existing "Cached data" and "Guest identity." Documents the boundary: synced records leave the device for Apple's CloudKit infra (user-owned, user-controlled, encrypted in transit + at rest per Apple's CloudKit security model). The §4 "No auth required for reads in v1" line stays intact — CloudKit reads/writes the *user's own* data, not the blog's read surface.
- **§9 amendment (paired with T-707).** Adds the four new event names (`syncEnabled`, `syncDisabled`, `syncCompletedSuccessfully`, `syncFailed(errorCategory:)`) to the allowlist. Confirms payloads carry no PII (no recipe IDs, no error messages, no device identifiers). The App Privacy questionnaire mapping table is **unchanged** per CL-94 — CloudKit data is the user's iCloud data, not "data we collect" under Apple's own definition; the four events themselves fall under the existing "Usage Data — Product Interaction" row.
- **CL-5 disposition (the original 2026-05-23 deferral).** CL-5 said "iCloud / CloudKit sync is explicitly deferred to v2 (already in spec out-of-scope)." CL-86 amends CL-5 with strike-through + a lineage note ("Reversed by CL-86 / US-41 — iCloud sync ships in v1.x via CloudKit private DB. AC-5.7's reinstall-wipes-saves limitation is also softened: with sync enabled, reinstalling restores saves from the user's iCloud private DB.") so the original deferral and the amendment both stay readable in `clarifications.md`. The v1 scope (out) section's "Cross-device sync (iCloud) of saved recipes" bullet is struck through with a "(Graduated as US-41 per CL-86, 2026-05-28)" marker.
- **AC-5.7 amendment.** The existing AC-5.7 limitation ("reinstall wipes saves") is amended: with CloudKit sync enabled, a fresh install on the same iCloud account restores the user's saved recipes after the AC-41.2 opt-in flow completes. The original wording is preserved with strike-through + the lineage marker pointing at CL-97 / US-41.
- **AppPrivacy.md "What changes for v2" section.** The existing "If you add CloudKit sync for saved recipes (v2)" speculation block is **replaced** (per CL-94) with the actual delta: no new data category to declare, because CloudKit data is user-to-Apple, not user-to-us. The original speculative block stays as historical context (commented out / preserved as a strike-through note) so the audit trail is clear.
- **CL-86..CL-99** capture every design decision and the considered alternatives. **CL-98** (Google Play / Android non-applicability) and **CL-88's sub-decision** (Shopping List sync deferred until T-682 ships) are the two explicit-deferral entries; the rest document v1.x paths. **CL-87** carries the Spencer-original-framing reversal trail — anyone reading the round-6 backlog entry first lands on CL-87 next and sees the entire OAuth-to-CloudKit rationale.

### US-42 — Local notifications for newly-published posts, type-aware (recipe vs. article)

> As a returning cook, when a new post drops on dutchovendaddy.com I want the app to tap me on the shoulder with a notification whose copy matches the kind of post — "New Recipe 🍳" for a recipe, "New Article 📖" for a roundup/article — and tapping it should open that post directly.

Added 2026-05-28 (round-8 backlog "Notifications" item, graduated). T-630 (US-36 / AC-36.1) shipped the **Settings notifications toggle** ("Notify me when new recipes drop") backed by the `dod.settings.notificationsEnabled` UserDefaults flag, deliberately **UI-only** — flipping it persisted a boolean and did nothing else. **T-631 wires that toggle to real behavior using on-device *local* notifications** (`UNUserNotificationCenter`), not Apple Push / APNs. v1 has no server signal for "a new post dropped," so the production notification trigger is deferred to a later push task; what T-631 delivers is the full local-notification plumbing — authorization request on toggle-on, a type-aware content builder keyed on `PostKind` (US-37), a tap handler that deep-links the post through the existing `DeepLinkDispatcher` / `WidgetDeepLinkParser` grammar (REG-10), a foreground-banner presentation, and a temporary DEBUG "Simulate New Post" affordance in Settings that fires two sample notifications (one article, one recipe) so the end-to-end path can be exercised in the simulator. Everything is device-local: no network round-trip, no remote payload, no data leaves the device (parallel to the on-device idle-timer note in constitution §9 and the Voice-Mode note in US-40). CL-100 captures the local-vs-push v1 decision, the type-copy mapping, the toggle-gating contract, and the temporary-test-affordance rationale.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- **AC-42.1 (Toggle requests authorization).** Flipping the Settings notifications toggle (AC-36.1) **ON** calls `NotificationService.requestAuthorization()`, which wraps `UNUserNotificationCenter.current().requestAuthorization(options: [.alert, .sound])`. If the user **grants**, the `dod.settings.notificationsEnabled` flag persists `true`. If the user **denies** at the system prompt (or has previously denied), the toggle reverts to OFF and the persisted flag is set back to `false` so the UI never claims notifications are on when the OS will suppress them. The intent is always persisted through the existing flag — no second source of truth.
- **AC-42.2 (Type-aware copy).** Notification content is built by a pure, unit-tested mapping keyed on `PostKind` (US-37): a **`.recipe`** post produces title **"New Recipe 🍳"** and body **"\<postTitle\> just dropped. Tap to start cooking."**; an **`.article`** post produces title **"New Article 📖"** and body **"\<postTitle\> is up. Tap to read."** The post title is interpolated verbatim; no other copy varies by kind. *(Copy amended in T-633 / CL-102: bodies are period-separated, no em dashes per the owner's request.)*
- **AC-42.3 (Tap deep-links to the post).** Each scheduled notification carries its deep-link target in `userInfo` under a stable key — `dod://recipe/<id>` for a `.recipe` and `dod://article/<id>` for an `.article`. `UNUserNotificationCenterDelegate.userNotificationCenter(_:didReceive:)` reads that URL and routes it through the existing deep-link path (`WidgetDeepLinkParser` for recipe URLs, `DeepLinkDispatcher`/`RootView.handle(...)` for the routing) so a tapped notification opens the post exactly the way a widget tap or Spotlight result does. No new URL grammar is introduced.
- **AC-42.4 (Toggle-off suppresses all notifications).** When `dod.settings.notificationsEnabled` is **OFF** (or system permission is not granted), **no notification is ever scheduled** — `NotificationService.scheduleNewPostNotification(...)` short-circuits and posts nothing. The toggle is the single gate: off ⇒ silence, with no edge case that leaks a notification. *(The AC-42.6 test affordance that previously also routed through this gate was removed in T-633.)*
- **AC-42.5 (Foreground banner).** `UNUserNotificationCenterDelegate.userNotificationCenter(_:willPresent:)` returns `[.banner, .sound]` (`.list` on iOS 14+) so a notification that fires while the app is in the foreground still surfaces a banner rather than being silently swallowed — important so the behavior is observable when the app is open (e.g. during the simulator test).
- **AC-42.6 (Temporary test affordance).** ~~A clearly-labeled, temporary **"▸ Test: Simulate New Post"** button sits directly below the notifications toggle in `SettingsView`, wrapped in `#if DEBUG` so it never ships in a release build. It is **gated by the toggle**: if notifications are OFF (or permission not granted) tapping it does nothing (AC-42.4). When ON, it fires **two** sample local notifications ~2s apart to demonstrate both kinds — an **article** ("Best Dutch Oven Recipes (30+ Tried and Tested Favorites)", `.article`) and a **recipe** ("Cast Iron Burgers (Easy Skillet Recipe)", `.recipe`) — each with a plausible WP post id in its deep-link payload. This affordance is a development scaffold for exercising the path in the simulator, not a v1 product feature.~~ **Removed in T-633 (CL-102):** the schedule → fire → banner → tap → fetch → detail path was verified in the simulator via the T-632 real-id swap, so the temporary DEBUG scaffold (button + `NotificationService.simulateNewPosts()`) is deleted. The real production trigger remains the deferred push follow-up; `scheduleNewPostNotification(title:postKind:recipeID:)` is unchanged and stays the seam that follow-up reuses.

**Constitution + spec notes:**
- **Local, not push (v1).** T-631 ships *local* notifications only (`UNUserNotificationCenter` scheduled on-device). There is no APNs registration, no device-token handoff, no backend opt-in, and no remote payload in this task — v1 has no server signal for "a new post published," so the real-world trigger is a deferred push follow-up. The local path is the complete, testable plumbing that a future push task slots its payload into (same content builder, same `userInfo` deep-link contract). CL-100 records the local-vs-push decision.
- **No new privacy / analytics surface.** Local notifications are a device-local, system-presented affordance — like the Cook Mode idle-timer (§9) and the Live Activity payload (§2), they are **not** a tracked event and **not** a new category of data collection. No notification content or schedule is sent to TelemetryDeck and the App Privacy questionnaire is unaffected. T-631 adds **no** new `AnalyticsEvent` case (no `notificationOpened` event in v1); constitution §9 gains only a clarifying note that device-local notifications carry no data off-device. (Should a future task want a `notificationOpened` adoption signal, it amends the §9 allowlist then.)
- **PostKind copy mapping.** The recipe-vs-article copy split reuses the `PostKind` discriminator (US-37 / CL-63) rather than inspecting the post itself — the content builder takes a `PostKind` and returns the title/body strings, so it is a pure function unit-tested per AC-42.2 with no `UNUserNotificationCenter` dependency.
- **Temporary test affordance.** ~~The "Simulate New Post" button is `#if DEBUG`-gated and explicitly labeled temporary — it exists so the orchestrator/QA can fire both notification kinds in the simulator (which can't be triggered by a server in v1). It is removed (or replaced by the real push trigger) when the push follow-up lands.~~ **Removed in T-633 (CL-102)** now that the path is verified — see AC-42.6. The push follow-up still slots into the unchanged `scheduleNewPostNotification(...)` seam.
- **CL-100** captures the local-vs-push v1 decision, the `PostKind`→copy mapping, the toggle-as-single-gate suppression contract, the `userInfo` deep-link payload shape (reusing the widget/Spotlight grammar), the foreground-banner choice, and the temporary DEBUG test-affordance rationale.

### US-43 — Site/app design coordination so dutchovendaddy.com and the iOS app feel like one brand

> As a user moving between dutchovendaddy.com and the iOS app, I want the two surfaces to feel like one continuous brand experience so the cognitive cost of switching is zero.

Added 2026-05-29 (round-9 backlog "Site ↔ app design coordination" item, graduated). Captured after @adamsned's first real-device install of TestFlight build `1.0 (2)` and a live design audit of the website's homepage and `/dutch-oven-recipes/` landing. Seven measurable visual gaps observed between the two surfaces: backdrop color (cream vs. white), card chrome (rounded elevated cards vs. none), hero aspect (16:9 landscape vs. 3:4 portrait), time chip presence, excerpt presence, numbered "Popular" badges, and brand-mark masthead. **Phase a (T-710)** lands the foundational token + typography layer; Phases b–d (T-711..T-713) deliver the visible compositional changes behind a `DODFeed.layoutVariant` flag so each screen reverts independently if a regression surfaces on real device. Constitution-amendment-free — all Apple-system primitives (SwiftUI surfaces, SF Pro / SF Rounded fonts, the existing `App/AppIcon.icon/Assets/DOD Master.png` reused at 32pt in the nav bar for Phase c). CL-110..CL-114 capture the per-axis rationale.

**Acceptance criteria (Phase a — T-710, this PR):**

- **AC-43.1 (Primary surface flips to white).** `Surface.colorset` light variant resolves to `#FFFFFF` (was `#F9F6EF`) — matches dutchovendaddy.com's white backdrop. Dark variant resolves to `#1B140E` (was `#42210B`) — deeper warm-brown per CL-111.
- **AC-43.2 (`SurfaceElevated` collapses).** `SurfaceElevated.colorset` light = `#FFFFFF` / dark = `#281F19`. On light mode the elevated surface visually collapses to `Surface` so cards read as borderless; Phase b's magazine `RecipeCard` variant relies on this collapse.
- **AC-43.3 (`SurfaceWarm` token added).** New `SurfaceWarm.colorset` token (`#FAF6EE` / `#281F19`) inherits cream's old role; Phase b (T-711) + Phase d (T-713) wire the Saved tab, empty states, and Cook Mode background to it. **Phase a adds the token; no view references it.**
- **AC-43.4 (`CreamSubtle` renamed to `SurfaceDivider`).** Public API breaking rename. New `SurfaceDivider.colorset` (`#E6DECF` / `#3D2B1F`) replaces `CreamSubtle.colorset`; `DODColor.creamSubtle` → `DODColor.surfaceDivider`. Dark variant changes from the prior single-mode `#E6DECF` to `#3D2B1F` so dividers stay readable in dark Cook Mode. Two call sites updated: `Colors.swift` (the token declaration) and `DODDesignSystemTests/ColorsTests.swift` (the resolution test).
- **AC-43.5 (`LabelOnAccent` token added).** New `LabelOnAccent.colorset` token (`#FFFFFF` / `#FFFFFF`) for white text on burnt-orange-filled surfaces (Phase c's numbered "Popular" badge). **Phase a adds the token; no view references it.**
- **AC-43.6 (Display weights shift to bold).** `DODType.displayLarge` and `DODType.displayMedium` change from `.semibold` to `.bold` to match the site's section-header weight.
- **AC-43.7 (`heading` + `caption` adopt SF Rounded).** ~~`DODType.heading` switches design from `.default` to `.rounded`; `DODType.caption` switches design from `.default` to `.rounded` and weight from default to `.medium`. Friendlier card-and-chip register. No new font asset — system primitives only.~~ **Amended by CL-128 / T-731 (2026-05-31):** the SF Rounded family swap is reverted per joint @adamsned + @spencer0706 decision. `DODType.heading` and `DODType.caption` use the default System font family (uniform with body); brand register comes through weight, color, and spacing instead. The `.medium` weight on `DODType.caption` stands; only the family-design portion of this AC reverses. Bumps to `DODType.brand` (AC-43.8) follow the same revert — see CL-128 for the full lineage.
- **AC-43.8 (`brand` token added).** New `DODType.brand` Font token (~~`.system(size: 22, design: .rounded, weight: .bold)`~~ → `.system(size: 22, weight: .bold)` per CL-128 / T-731, 2026-05-31 — SF Rounded family swap reverted; size 22 + `.bold` weight stand) reserved for "DUTCH OVEN DADDY" wordmark moments (splash, About, share-sheet preview cards). **Phase a adds the token; Phase c (T-712) is the first consumer when the nav-bar masthead lands.**
- **AC-43.9 (L4 baselines re-recorded).** Every snapshot baseline in the workspace that resolves any of `DODColor.surface`, `DODColor.surfaceElevated`, `DODColor.label`, `DODColor.labelSecondary`, `DODType.displayLarge`, `DODType.displayMedium`, `DODType.heading`, `DODType.caption`, or the renamed `DODColor.surfaceDivider` is re-recorded under the new tokens. Visual diffs reviewed and committed in this PR's snapshot directories.
- **AC-43.10 (No regression on L1, L2, L3).** All pre-existing tests pass against the new tokens. The L1 ColorsTests case that asserts `DODColor.surfaceDivider` resolves is updated; no other L1 test changes.
- **AC-43.11 (Public API breaking change documented).** The `creamSubtle` → `surfaceDivider` rename is the only breaking change. PR release notes call it out; the only consumers are `Colors.swift` (declaration) and `ColorsTests.swift` (test) — zero feature-package call sites — so no downstream rebuilds blocked.

**Phase b–d acceptance criteria are reserved**: AC-43.12..43.20 are reserved for the magazine `RecipeCard` variant (T-711), the nav-bar masthead + numbered Popular badge (T-712), and the Recipe Detail surface polish (T-713). Those phases ship under their own task IDs with their own AC numbering; the reservation here marks the public-API ceiling.

**Constitution + spec notes:**

- **Constitution-amendment-free.** All seven moves use Apple-system primitives — SwiftUI surfaces, SF Pro / SF Rounded fonts (no webfont asset added; constitution §3 default-no respected), the existing `App/AppIcon.icon/Assets/DOD Master.png` brand asset for Phase c's masthead, and `VNDetectRectanglesRequest` is intentionally **not** part of this US (that's a separate "Cast Iron Photo Scanner" backlog item — see backlog.md round-9 capture).
- **Dark-mode posture (CL-111).** The site is light-only; the app's dark mode keeps a warm-brown surface (`#1B140E` for `Surface`, `#281F19` for `SurfaceElevated` and `SurfaceWarm`, `#3D2B1F` for `SurfaceDivider`) to preserve the low-light cooking-mode read affordance. No goal to match the site's white in dark — the goal is light-mode coordination only.
- **Behind-the-flag from Phase b on.** Phase a's token shift is unconditional (every view that resolves `Surface` picks up white on next launch). Phase b–d gate their compositional changes behind a `DODFeed.layoutVariant` flag bound to a UserDefaults key + a Settings → Layout row so power users can A/B variants on real device and revert per-screen if needed.
- **No App Privacy questionnaire impact.** Visual-token + typography changes do not move data; the App Privacy answers stay byte-identical.
- **CL-110..CL-114** capture the surface-tier reshuffle rationale (CL-110), the dark-mode parity decision (CL-111), the `creamSubtle` rename trail (CL-112), the typography weight + rounded-design rationale (CL-113), and the Phase b/c/d punt-by-design with the open decisions each future phase still needs (CL-114).
