### US-44 — User profile (local-only v1) and gated write surfaces

> As a returning cook, I want a profile (display name + email + photo) tied to "me on this device" so my comments and ratings are attributed to me without re-entering the form every time and the app can gate write surfaces behind "you've set up a profile."

Added 2026-06-02 (Linear DUT-36). Ships in four PRs: **Phase a (T-739, this PR)** — Profile data model + Settings → Profile section + edit view + Sign Out + Delete Profile. **Phase b (TBD)** — photo picker + crop. **Phase c (TBD)** — Ratings gate + first-write popup. **Phase d (TBD)** — comment composer auto-fill from the profile. Storage is **local-only**: the profile is a single JSON-encoded ``UserProfile`` row in the Keychain (service `com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp.profile.v1`, account `"profile"`, device-local — survives a wipe-and-reinstall via OS-managed iCloud Keychain *backup* if the user has it on, but does NOT cross-device sync via iCloud Keychain). The photo, when Phase b lands, lives in the app's Documents directory as a JPG keyed by ``UserProfile/photoFilename``. **Bridge to US-15.** The on-device guest UUID (US-15 / CL-15) is the canonical "this device's commenter" identity; when a user creates a profile its `id` field carries that same UUID forward so old guest comments stay associated with the same identity. CL-136 captures the locked decisions + the deferred-phase scope.

**Acceptance criteria (Phase a — T-739, this PR):**

- **AC-44.1 (Profile section at top of Settings).** `SettingsView` renders a Profile section as the **first** section (above Use Metric Units). When no profile exists, the row reads "Set up your profile" + chevron, tap pushes ``ProfileEditView`` in an empty state. When a profile exists, the row shows a 60pt avatar leading + display name on top + email below + chevron trailing, tap pushes ``ProfileEditView`` in edit state. `.listRowBackground(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` matches the T-647 / CL-125 brand-brown treatment. *Amended by CL-179 (T-783, 2026-06-18): this Settings Profile section is the **iPhone** layout. On **iPad** the Profile moves to the top of the `NavigationSplitView` sidebar (`SidebarProfileRow` — avatar + name / “Set up your profile”, opening `ProfileEditView` as a sheet), and the Settings section is hidden there via a `ProfileSettingsSection` wrapper that checks `UIDevice.userInterfaceIdiom == .pad` (the Settings form sheet reports a compact size class on iPad, so size class can’t gate it). The iPad sidebar header is also retitled “DOD” → “Dutch Oven Daddy”. iPhone is unchanged.* *Amended again by CL-180 (T-784, 2026-06-19): the sidebar header title is now removed entirely — `.navigationTitle(“”)` + `.navigationBarTitleDisplayMode(.inline)` collapses the large-title band so the pinned `SidebarProfileRow` reads as the sidebar header. A separate “Dutch Oven Daddy” title crowded an already-busy sidebar. iPhone unchanged (no sidebar).* *Amended again by CL-182 (T-786, 2026-06-19): the empty-profile placeholder copy is Title Case — "Set Up Your Profile" (title) and, in the sidebar, "Add Your Name and Photo" (subtitle) — across the Settings row (`ProfileSection`) and the iPad sidebar row (`SidebarProfileRow`). Presentation-only.*
- **AC-44.2 (Display Name + Email required).** ``ProfileEditView`` renders two TextFields — Display Name (`.name` content type, `.words` autocapitalization) and Email (`.emailAddress` content type, `.emailAddress` keyboard, autocapitalization OFF, autocorrection OFF). The top-right Done toolbar item is **disabled** until display name is non-whitespace AND email matches the basic regex `^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$` (locked per CL-136 — server-side validation is authoritative; the client-side guard prevents `author_email=""` / malformed shapes from reaching the network per the CL-134 / DUT-7 pattern). The top-left Cancel toolbar item dismisses without saving.
- **AC-44.3 (Photo upload via PhotosPicker + custom square crop — amended Phase b T-740 / CL-137, again T-748 / CL-145).** ~~The edit view contains a "Profile Picture" row with the current avatar trailing. **Phase a's row is a stub** — tap is a no-op, `// TODO(2026-06-02, spencer)` marks the Phase b wire-in. No `PhotosPicker` is wired in this PR; the Documents-directory write path stays unimplemented.~~ The edit view's Profile Picture row presents a SwiftUI `PhotosPicker` (selection `matching: .images`, `photoLibrary: .shared()`) — Apple's privacy-preserving picker, so **no `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` Info.plist entry is required and no permission prompt is shown**. On selection the picker hands back a `PhotosPickerItem`, the row then presents a custom SwiftUI ``ProfilePhotoCropView`` modal (square crop via `GeometryReader` + `MagnificationGesture` 1.0..4.0 + `DragGesture` pan, dimmed overlay outside the square). Done renders the cropped 512×512 JPEG at 0.85 quality via `UIGraphicsImageRenderer`, hands the `UIImage` back, ``ProfilePhotoStore`` writes it to the app's Documents directory keyed by a UUID-derived filename (`profile-photo-<UUID().uuidString>.jpg`), `UserProfile.photoFilename` is updated with the filename, and ``ProfilePhotoView`` loads + renders the photo via `UIImage(contentsOfFile:)` (falling back to ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` when the file is missing — graceful degradation). The avatar fallback (per AC-44.5) is what renders before the user uploads a photo. Constitution §3 respected — custom SwiftUI crop view, no third-party image-cropping library (Mantis / BSImagePicker considered + rejected). **T-748 / CL-145 amendment (DUT-54).** The crop math helper signature is `cropRect(for:offset:imageSize:cropDiameterInScreenPoints:displayedImageScaleFactor:)` — it converts the screen-pt `offset` + the screen-pt `cropDiameterInScreenPoints` into source pixels via `displayedImageScaleFactor * scale` so the saved 512×512 region exactly matches the contents of the visible crop circle. The view captures `GeometryReader.proxy.size` into `@State` so `renderCroppedImage()` (called from the toolbar Done outside the GeometryReader closure) can compute the on-screen crop diameter + the `.scaledToFit()` ratio at crop time. Supersedes the pre-T-748 `cropSize: 1.0` contract which silently saved the full inscribed square of the source rather than the visible-circle region.
- **AC-44.4 (Sign Out + Delete Profile semantics).** ``ProfileEditView`` ships **both** a Sign Out button (accent color, no confirmation) AND a Delete Profile button (`Button(role: .destructive)` + confirmation alert reading "Delete your profile? This will remove your display name, email, and any future comments will be attributed to a guest."). Both clear the Keychain row + photo file via ``ProfileStoring/clear()`` and refresh the parent `SettingsViewModel.profile` to `nil` (back to guest mode). Identical behavior in Phase a per the locked decision — when DUT-16 lands and adds backend state, Sign Out keeps server data while Delete Profile nukes it. Shipping both today is the App Store 5.1.1(v) "Delete Account" requirement + the friendlier Sign Out UX rolled into one.
- **AC-44.5 (Initial-letter default avatar).** ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` renders a circle filled with `DODColor.accent` (brand burnt-orange) carrying the uppercase first **letter** of the trimmed display name in `DODColor.labelOnAccent`. Letter extraction skips emoji / digits / punctuation (`"🌟 Spencer"` → `"S"`); empty / all-emoji / whitespace-only names fall back to `"?"`. The view is 60pt in the Settings section header, scalable via the `diameter` parameter. No per-name color hash in Phase a (locked). *Amended by CL-183 (T-787, 2026-06-19): the no-letter fallback no longer renders a bare "?" — ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` renders ``ChefHatAvatarGlyph`` (Spencer's chef-hat person silhouette, white on the accent circle) for empty / all-emoji / whitespace-only names. "?" is retained internally as the `noLetterPlaceholder` sentinel the view branches on (letter-extraction contract unchanged); a name with a letter still renders the uppercase initial.*
- **AC-44.6 (US-15 guest UUID bridges to Profile.id).** ``UserProfile/id`` is the canonical "this device's commenter" UUID. When wrapping the existing guest identity into a new profile the composition root reuses the device's existing guest UUID rather than minting a new one, so the same UUID identifies both the old guest comments and the newly-attributed-to-profile new comments. Pinned by `ProfileStoreTests.saveWithSeededUUIDPreservesIdentity`. (Today's US-15 `GuestIdentityStore` does not surface a UUID — it stores name + email only — so Phase a uses a fresh UUID at first-profile creation and pins the round-trip contract; if a future task surfaces an existing on-device UUID alongside the name + email, this AC's round-trip guarantee carries that UUID forward.)
- **AC-44.7 (No retroactive attribution).** Old guest comments stay attributed to the guest identity that posted them; only new comments posted after profile creation use the profile. The app does not retroactively re-associate previously-submitted comments. Locked per CL-136.

**Acceptance criteria (Phase b — T-740, CL-137):**

- **AC-44.8 (Replace / Edit / Remove options when a photo exists — amended T-745 / CL-142, T-746 / CL-143).** When `UserProfile.photoFilename` is non-nil the Profile Picture row tap surfaces a confirmation dialog with four options: **Replace Photo** (re-presents the `PhotosPicker`, runs through the same crop flow, overwrites the previous photo file via ``ProfilePhotoStore/save(_:)`` AND persists the picked original via ``ProfilePhotoStore/saveOriginal(_:)`` — T-745), **Edit Photo** (new T-745 — loads the user's existing source via ``ProfilePhotoStore/loadOriginal(filename:)`` when `photoOriginalFilename != nil`, or falls back to ``ProfilePhotoStore/load(filename:)`` for legacy users with only the cropped derivative; presents the existing ``ProfilePhotoCropView`` for re-crop; on Done overwrites only the cropped derivative — the original stays as-is), **Remove Photo** (clears the in-flight `photoFilename` + `photoOriginalFilename` + calls ``ProfilePhotoStore/clear(filename:)`` AND ``ProfilePhotoStore/clearOriginal(filename:)`` on the previous files → row falls back to the ``InitialLetterAvatarView``), and **Cancel** (dismiss the dialog). When `photoFilename` is nil the row tap goes straight to the picker (no dialog) — pre-T-745 the conditional `inFlightPhotoFilename != nil` gate is already in place; T-745 verifies + documents it as load-bearing. **T-746 / CL-143 amendment (DUT-40) — conditional is file-existence-validated, not just nil-check.** On view-mount, ``ProfileEditView`` runs a `.task` after the existing `.onAppear` seeding that validates `inFlightPhotoFilename` (and `inFlightPhotoOriginalFilename`) against ``ProfilePhotoStore/exists(filename:)`` (and ``existsOriginal(filename:)``) — nil-ing any reference whose file is missing on disk. Self-heals the dev-rebuild scenario where the Keychain row persists across `simctl uninstall`/install but Documents is wiped (Keychain and Documents have different simulator persistence semantics) — and the theoretical production edge cases (Files.app delete, storage-pressure eviction, partial CloudKit restore). Post-validation, the `inFlightPhotoFilename != nil` conditional in `handleProfilePictureRowTap` is correct by construction; the next ``handleSave()`` persists the nilled state to the Keychain row so the inconsistency clears permanently (no migration / proactive scan needed).
- **AC-44.9 (Sign Out + Delete Profile also clear the photo files — amended T-745 / CL-142).** ``ProfileStoring/clear()`` is amended to also delete the on-disk cropped + original photo files when the current profile carries a `photoFilename` and/or `photoOriginalFilename`. So Sign Out and Delete Profile leave the Documents directory in the same clean state as the Keychain row — no orphan JPEGs of either kind. Pinned by `ProfileStoreTests` integration tests that mock the photo store and assert `clear(filename:)` + `clearOriginal(filename:)` are both invoked. **Photo (cropped + original) never leaves the device** — Constitution §9 (privacy / on-device defaults) — never POSTed to any backend, never sent to TelemetryDeck.

**Acceptance criteria (Phase c — T-741, CL-138):**

- **AC-44.10 (Ratings & Reviews write surface gated on profile + modal sheet presentation — amended T-744 / CL-141, again T-747 / CL-144).** ``RecipeDetailRatingsSection``'s WRITE composer (star picker + author fields + comment text editor + submit button — i.e. `rateAndReviewCard`) is gated on profile existence. When ``RecipeDetailViewModel/hasProfile`` is `false` the composer is rendered with `.blur(radius: 10)` + `.disabled(true)` + `.accessibilityHidden(true)`, and a `RatingsProfileGate` card centers over it carrying "Set up your profile" + "To leave a rating and review, set up your profile in Settings." + a "Set Up Profile" CTA button (`accessibilityIdentifier("dod.ratings.gate.cta")`). Tapping the CTA presents ``ProfileEditView`` via `.sheet(isPresented:)` wrapped in a `NavigationStack` (so Cancel + Done toolbar render) — modal sheet OVER the recipe (not a push), so the recipe stays mounted underneath and dismissing the sheet returns the user to the exact same scroll position. On sheet dismiss `.onDisappear` runs `viewModel.refreshProfile()` which re-reads the profile store; the `@Observable` `viewModel.profile` re-assignment triggers SwiftUI re-render, flips `hasProfile` to `true`, and the gate reactively disappears — no manual callback wiring. The composition root (`LiveRecipeDetailDependencies`) gains a `profileStore: any ProfileStoring` parameter so `loadUserProfile()` reads through the protocol seam. The Settings → Profile push entry path (Phase a) is unchanged. **T-744 / CL-141 amendment (DUT-38).** The `RatingsProfileGate` popup card uses a *solid* `DODColor.surface` background (no `surfaceElevated`). The card body is structured `VStack { … }.padding(…).frame(maxWidth: 320).background(RoundedRectangle.fill(DODColor.surface)).shadow(…)` so the SwiftUI compositor reads the card as a fully opaque region — `.shadow(...)` hoisted OUTSIDE the `.background(...)` fill chain so the fill commits to its own opaque layer first. Light mode renders white (`#FFFFFF`), dark mode renders deep warm-brown (`#1B140E`) — matching the recipe-detail page background `DODColor.surface` so the popup reads as a solid, on-brand affordance. **T-747 / CL-144 amendment (DUT-41).** Earlier revisions of AC-44.10 added a sibling `Rectangle().fill(.ultraThinMaterial)` between the blurred composer and the popup card to dim the composer further. T-747 removed that Material view from the ZStack entirely — in dark mode the Material reads as a translucent warm-grey wash framing the popup card (Spencer described it as the "grey square"). The composer's `.blur(radius: 10)` alone communicates "this is gated"; the Material was redundant with the blur and was the user-visible frame even after T-744 made the popup card opaque. The ZStack now composes exactly two layers when gated: the blurred + disabled composer (z-index 0) and the popup card (z-index 1). The composer's `.disabled` + `.accessibilityHidden` gating semantics are unchanged.
- **AC-44.11 (Existing reviews stay readable in all states).** The `commentsList` rendering existing reviews is NEVER gated — readers without a profile can still browse what others wrote. Only the WRITE surface (`rateAndReviewCard`) is blurred + overlaid. The `Divider` between the composer and the comments list stays visible regardless of profile state. Comments rendering, moderation badges, and load states all behave identically across `hasProfile == true` / `false`.

**Acceptance criteria (Phase d — T-742, CL-139):**

- **AC-44.12 (Composer surfaces source identity from Profile, not user input — amended T-744 / CL-141).** Composer surfaces (rating + review + comment) inside ``RecipeDetailRatingsSection``'s `rateAndReviewCard` no longer expose editable Name / Email inputs. The two `TextField`-backed `authorFields` rows (Display Name + Email) are removed entirely — the user only chooses a star rating (optional) and types the comment text. Above the comment text editor a new static `PostingAsHeader` sub-view renders the user's profile identity: ``ProfilePhotoView`` (~32pt avatar) leading + `profile.displayName` (heading style) + a `Spacer` trailing. ~~`profile.email` (caption / secondary label) below~~ **T-744 / CL-141 amendment (DUT-37) — email row removed from the composer surface.** The header is non-interactive — it is plain text reading "you're posting as this person", not another input. The Phase c gate is preserved verbatim — when `profile == nil` the composer stays blurred + popup-overlaid; AC-44.12 only changes the composer body that's revealed WHEN unlocked (post-profile-creation). On Submit, the WP REST payload's `author_name` + `author_email` fields are sourced directly from `profile.displayName` + `profile.email` (after trimming whitespace); `RecipeDetailViewModel.commentAuthorName` / `commentAuthorEmail` / `prefillAuthorIdentity()` / `persistAuthorIdentity()` are retired — the on-form fields they backed no longer exist. The WP REST endpoint + payload shape are unchanged (`POST /wp/v2/comments` with `author_name` + `author_email` + `content` + optional `meta.wprm_comment_rating`); only the SOURCE of the two author fields changes from user-input to profile-read. **T-744 / CL-141 amendment (DUT-37) — `PostingAsHeader` no longer renders the email row.** The composer surface displays only the avatar + display name (and a trailing `Spacer`) — the inner `VStack` collapses to a single `Text(profile.displayName)`. The email is preserved on Settings → Profile + ``ProfileEditView`` (where the user actively manages it) and continues to flow through unchanged as `author_email` in the WP REST submission — the composer header is the only surface that stops surfacing the email. Rationale: published comments never expose `author_email` (verified at 3 layers — WP REST `/wp/v2/comments` GET redacts the field server-side per the existing WordPress privacy posture; `WPCommentDTO` zeroes the field on parse; ``CommentRow`` has no email render path) so the composer's own-email display was inconsistent with that privacy posture. VoiceOver's `accessibilityLabel` collapses to "Posting as `<displayName>`" (drop the email phrase).
- **AC-44.13 (Own-comment rows render the Profile photo).** ``CommentRow`` rendering inside `commentsList` shows the current user's ``ProfilePhotoView`` (40pt diameter, matching the existing Gravatar avatar frame) for comments that are theirs, falling back to the existing Gravatar / initial-letter avatar path for everyone else. "Theirs" is determined by a case-insensitive equality check on ``RecipeComment/authorEmail`` against `profile.email` (`.lowercased()` on both sides per RFC 5321) — gated on a non-nil profile, since guest mode has nothing to match against. WordPress's public `/wp/v2/comments` GET does NOT include `author_email` in the response (privacy — email is moderation-only), so `RecipeComment.authorEmail` is empty for every fetched comment. Own-comment attribution therefore activates only for comments the user posts in-session: the comment-submit path stamps `RecipeComment.authorEmail = profile.email` locally before inserting the just-posted comment into `viewModel.comments` via ``insertPostedCommentIfNew(_:)``, so the row renders with the profile photo immediately. Per AC-44.7 (no retroactive attribution) old guest-attributed comments naturally fall through to the existing avatar path because their empty `authorEmail` doesn't case-insensitively match `profile.email`. After a relaunch the in-session stamp is lost (cache mirrors what WP returns: `authorEmail = ""`) and own previously-posted comments fall back to the existing avatar path — this is the locked trade-off: keeping the device-local stamp in the cache would require a CloudKit / Keychain schema bump for a strictly-presentational signal, which is over-investment per CL-139.

**Phase d completes the user-profile delivery.** AC-44.14..AC-44.16 are polish-pass additions on top of the shipped DUT-36 feature set (T-743 / CL-140) — not a new phase.

**Acceptance criteria (T-743 polish pass — CL-140):**

- **AC-44.14 (Ratings empty-state label gate requires BOTH lists empty).** ``RecipeDetailRatingsSection``'s `ratingsHeader` shows the "Be the first to rate this recipe." invitation only when the recipe has **zero ratings AND zero comments**. Gate: `(viewModel.ratingSummary?.count ?? 0) == 0 && viewModel.comments.isEmpty`. Truth table — 0 ratings + 0 comments → invitation shown; ≥1 rating + 0 comments → `StarRatingDisplay` (existing aggregate path); 0 ratings + ≥1 comment → label hidden (no aggregate to render — there is no rating count); ≥1 rating + ≥1 comment → `StarRatingDisplay` (existing aggregate path). The `commentsList` rendering below is independent of this gate; the invitation is the only thing it controls. Pinned by L1 tests covering the 4 combinations.
- **AC-44.15 (Uniform `Circle()` clipping across avatar render sites + circle crop overlay).** Every avatar render site in the app uniformly clips to `Circle()`: ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` (60pt Settings + 32pt PostingAsHeader + 40pt own-comment row); ``ProfilePhotoView`` (same diameters via `.clipShape(Circle())`); ``CommentRow``'s default AsyncImage path + the avatarOverride path Phase d added. ``ProfilePhotoCropView``'s dimmed-outside overlay during cropping uses a `Circle` "hole" (swapped from `Rectangle`) so the user sees how the avatar will actually look post-save. The math underneath (`cropRect(for:offset:imageSize:cropSize:)`) is UNCHANGED — the saved bitmap is still a 512×512 square JPG at 0.85 quality (locked per CL-137); only the VISUAL OVERLAY shape changes. Render-side clipping (not on-disk alpha masking) is the canonical iOS pattern (Apple Mail, Messages, Contacts) — preserves storage flexibility for any future non-circle avatar surface.
- **AC-44.16 (Custom back button + unsaved-changes confirmationDialog + `Save` rename; Sign Out / Delete Profile bypass).** ``ProfileEditView``'s `.topBarLeading` toolbar drops the "Cancel" `Button` and substitutes a system back chevron (`Image(systemName: "chevron.left")`, no text label, `.accessibilityLabel("Back")`). The system back button is suppressed via `.navigationBarBackButtonHidden(true)`. Three initial-value `@State` snapshots (`initialDisplayName`, `initialEmail`, `initialPhotoFilename`) seeded on `.onAppear`; the computed `isDirty` returns `true` iff display name / email / photo filename differ from their initial values. Tapping the back chevron with `isDirty == true` opens a `.confirmationDialog("You have unsaved changes", isPresented: $showLeaveConfirmation, titleVisibility: .visible)` with two buttons — "Continue Editing" (dismisses the dialog) and "Leave Without Saving" (role: `.destructive`, dismisses both the dialog and the view). Tapping the back chevron with `isDirty == false` dismisses the view directly (no dialog). The view applies `.interactiveDismissDisabled(isDirty)` so when presented as a modal sheet (Phase c gate-CTA path) the user cannot drag-down past unsaved changes — they must tap the back chevron, routing through the dialog. **Sign Out + Delete Profile bypass the unsaved-changes dialog by construction:** both buttons' action closures call `handleClear()` (which calls `store.clear()` then `dismiss()`) WITHOUT consulting `isDirty` or setting `showLeaveConfirmation`. After `clear()` the profile is nil, so the "unsaved changes" concept doesn't apply. The Delete Profile button's existing explicit destructive alert ("Delete your profile?") is preserved and is distinct from the new unsaved-changes dialog. The `.topBarTrailing` toolbar button is renamed `Done` → `Save`; action / disabled state / behavior unchanged; the accessibility identifier `"profile-edit-done"` is renamed to `"profile-edit-save"` to keep the contract honest.

**Acceptance criteria (T-745 — CL-142):**

- **AC-44.17 (Original-image storage + Edit Photo flow + downscale + legacy fallback).** ``UserProfile`` gains an optional `photoOriginalFilename: String?` field (default `nil`); Codable handles a missing key in legacy JSON via the auto-synthesized `decodeIfPresent` semantics for optionals so pre-T-745 profiles in the Keychain decode cleanly into the new struct shape. ``ProfilePhotoStoring`` protocol gains three new methods: `saveOriginal(_ image: UIImage) async throws -> String` (persists the picker's image as a JPEG inside the app's Documents directory, downscaled to a longest-dimension cap of 2048 pixels via `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` with `scale = 1.0` so the output is in pixels not points, preserving aspect ratio — both dimensions scaled by the same factor; if the input's longest side is already ≤ 2048, save at original resolution with no resize; JPEG 0.85 quality; returns a UUID-keyed filename `profile-photo-original-<UUID>.jpg`), `loadOriginal(filename: String) async -> UIImage?` (parallel to existing `load`; returns nil on missing file), `clearOriginal(filename: String) async throws` (parallel to existing `clear`; idempotent — missing file is success). Both production ``ProfilePhotoStore`` and ``InMemoryProfilePhotoStore`` implement all three. **Replace flow:** picker → on completion, `await profilePhotoStore.saveOriginal(image)` persists the original (→ `originalFilename`); then presents `ProfilePhotoCropView`; on crop Done, `await profilePhotoStore.save(cropped)` persists the cropped (→ `croppedFilename`); ``UserProfile`` is updated with both filenames; on Save, ``ProfileStoring/save(_:)`` persists the Keychain row. **Edit Photo flow:** action sheet → tap Edit Photo → loadPhotoForEditing async closure: (a) if `photoOriginalFilename != nil`, `await profilePhotoStore.loadOriginal(filename:)` returns the 2048-max source; (b) else `await profilePhotoStore.load(filename: photoFilename!)` returns the cropped 512×512 (legacy fallback — documented quality limitation, but functional); (c) present ``ProfilePhotoCropView`` via the existing `cropCandidate` `@State` + `.sheet(item:)` plumbing; (d) on crop Done, the existing `handleCroppedImage(_:)` writes a new UUID-keyed cropped file and the post-save cleanup deletes the orphan via `photoFilenameToClearOnSave`. The original stays as-is (only the cropped derivative is overwritten). **Remove flow extends** to mark both `photoFilenameToClearOnSave` (existing) and `photoOriginalFilenameToClearOnSave` (new) for post-save cleanup; `handleSave` calls `clearOriginal(filename:)` alongside `clear(filename:)`. **``ProfileStoring/clear()`` extends** to call `await photoStore.clearOriginal(filename:)` on `profile.photoOriginalFilename` if non-nil, mirroring the existing `clear(filename:)` call on `profile.photoFilename`. ``KeychainProfileStore`` + ``InMemoryProfileStore`` both extend. **Storage cost.** Per-profile Documents footprint grows from ~50-150KB (cropped only) to ~350-650KB total (cropped + 2048-max original); negligible on iOS devices. **Why 2048.** Covers every realistic re-crop scenario on iOS pixel surfaces (iPhone 14 Pro Max 2796×1290 is the largest viewport; 2048 covers it with headroom for crop's 4.0× zoom clamp), while keeping the per-profile footprint manageable. **Why not preserve full original.** Original camera photos can be 4032×3024 (3-5MB each); 2048 cap is the perceptually-equivalent threshold for re-crop with no user-visible quality loss. **JPEG 0.85 matches the cropped derivative quality (CL-137).** **Constitution §3 + §9 respected.** No new third-party dependency; both files stay device-local in the Documents directory; neither file is POSTed to any backend or sent to TelemetryDeck.

**Constitution + spec notes:**

- **No new third-party dependency.** ``KeychainProfileStore`` uses Apple's `Security` framework directly (constitution §3 default-no respected). The InMemory store is an `actor` matching `RecipeStore`'s concurrency posture.
- **No App Privacy questionnaire change yet.** Local-only Keychain entry — no data leaves the device in Phase a (no analytics event tied to profile creation; Phase d's auto-fill will dispatch the existing comment-submitted event whose name + email payload is unchanged). The App Privacy answers stay byte-identical until Phase d / DUT-16 introduce a backend identity.
- **Constitution §1 amendment not yet needed.** Write surfaces (Save, Comment, Rate) still work the same way in Phase a — they just gain a Profile prereq later in Phase c, not Phase a. The §1 amendment lands with Phase c.
- **CL-136** captures the locked decisions (Keychain storage, required email, initial-letter avatar, no retroactive attribution, Sign Out + Delete both ship), the deferred-phase scope, and the rejected alternatives (SwiftData persistence, per-name color hash, retroactive comment re-attribution).

### US-45 — Settings "Shop" row linking out to the BuzzyWaxx storefront

**As a** cast-iron cook who just fell in love with a DOD recipe,
**I want** an in-app path to buy BuzzyWaxx cast iron seasoning wax,
**so that** I can keep my skillet seasoned without hunting for where to buy it.

Added 2026-06-20 (DUT-94, authored by Ned). BuzzyWaxx is a sister brand under common ownership; the app's "Cast Iron Living" brand voice already primes the reader as a warm buyer, but today there is no in-app path to the store. This is a **pure outbound-commerce external link** — no native cart, no in-app catalog, no in-app checkout, **no StoreKit / IAP**. It reuses the established external-link-to-system-browser path (the DOD-ART-2 precedent) and mirrors the `SettingsView+CloudSync` / `SettingsView+Voice` extension-section pattern, so the engineering footprint is tiny. v1 is the Settings surface only; co-located CTAs inside the planned cast-iron care features (DUT-13 / DUT-49) are a fast-follow reusing the same URL + routing.

**Acceptance criteria (T-790 — CL-186):**

- **AC-45.1** `SettingsView` renders a new `Shop` section (a `ShopSection` sub-view in `SettingsView+Shop.swift`) styled like the existing extension sections — `DODType.heading` + `DODColor.label` header reading `"Shop"`, `.listRowBackground(DODColor.surfaceElevated)`, and a `DODType.caption` + `DODColor.labelSecondary` footer clarifying it is a physical product that ships and that the link opens the web store. It is positioned between the `About Dutch Oven Daddy` section and the version footer (a low-key promotional placement at the bottom of the page).
- **AC-45.2** The section contains a single `"Buy BuzzyWaxx Seasoning"` row: a `Button` whose primary label (Title Case per T-750) is `"Buy BuzzyWaxx Seasoning"`, a `DODType.detail` / `DODColor.labelSecondary` secondary line identifying the **physical** good (`"Cast iron seasoning wax · ships from buzzywaxx.com"`), a leading `shippingbox.fill` glyph (signals a physical good that ships) and a trailing `arrow.up.forward.app` external-link affordance, both in `DODColor.accent`. The row carries `accessibilityIdentifier("settings-button-shop-buzzywaxx")`, the `.isButton` trait (it is a `Button`), and `accessibilityLabel("Buy BuzzyWaxx cast iron seasoning, opens in browser")`; the two glyphs are decorative (`accessibilityHidden(true)`).
- **AC-45.3** Activating the row opens `SettingsViewModel.buyBuzzyWaxxURLString` (`https://buzzywaxx.com/collections/frontpage` — the owned Shopify "Shop All" collection with on-site checkout) in the **system browser** via the SwiftUI `@Environment(\.openURL)` action, building the `URL` at the call site with `if let` (the URL is stored as a `String` constant so the repo's `force_unwrapping`-as-error lint stays clean and the L1 suite can pin the literal). No in-app cart, catalog, or checkout is built; no payment is collected in-app; **no StoreKit / IAP code is added**. The system browser (not an in-app `WKWebView`) is the strongest "you are leaving the app to a real store" reviewer signal.
- **AC-45.4** The row is static content (a label + a URL constant) and always renders — it never shows a loading skeleton and does not depend on a network fetch. Tapping it offline simply opens the browser, which surfaces the system's own no-connection page; the row stays enabled (no `NetworkMonitor` gating in v1).
- **AC-45.5** v1 ships **untracked** — no analytics event is added for the tap, so this surface needs neither a constitution §9 allowlist amendment nor an App Privacy review. A count-only `shopLinkTapped(channel:)` event is a documented fast-follow (CL-186) that would carry both of those gates. No Amazon secondary link in v1 (direct-Shopify only, to keep full margin + first-party data); it is a documented fast-follow.

**Constitution + spec notes:**

- **App Store compliance (Guideline 3.1.3(e) — Goods and Services Outside of the App).** BuzzyWaxx seasoning wax is a tangible jar that ships and is consumed outside the app, so external web payment is the compliant path and **IAP is prohibited** for it. 3.1.1 (IAP) does not apply (it governs digital content/subscriptions); the 3.1.1(a) External Purchase Link entitlement is NOT needed (it exists for apps selling *digital* content). Stay clean by: making it unmistakable this is a physical product (label, "cast iron seasoning wax," external-link affordance), sending the user to `buzzywaxx.com` to complete checkout, building no native storefront, and never gating any in-app feature on the purchase.
- **No new third-party dependency.** Reuses the system browser via `@Environment(\.openURL)` exactly as `SettingsView+Voice` opens `openSettingsURLString` (constitution §3 default-no respected). No StoreKit.
- **CL-186** captures the locked decisions (direct Shopify storefront URL not Amazon; Settings placement for v1; single "Shop All" link; untracked v1 with the analytics + Amazon-secondary as documented fast-follows) and the rejected alternatives (in-app cart/catalog/checkout, a new bottom-bar tab, hard-coded prices).
