### US-47 — Cooking timers (multi-timer, Live Activity, hands-free)

As a cook following a Dutch-oven recipe, I want to start named countdown timers from the recipe's step times and see them on the Lock Screen / Dynamic Island, so I can walk away from my phone and still be alerted — without juggling the system Clock app or losing track when several things overlap.

- **AC-47.1 (timer engine — T-804 / CL-198, IMPLEMENTED).** A pure, deterministically-testable engine owns the set of active countdowns. `CookTimer` (DODSupport) is a value type whose remaining-time math is a function of an injected "now" (`running(endDate:)` counts down, `paused(remaining:)` freezes, `finished`); `CookTimerEngine` (`@Observable @MainActor`, DODSupport) manages `[CookTimer]` with `start(label:duration:)` (rejects non-positive durations), `pause`/`resume`/`cancel`/`clearFinished`, a `soonestFinishing` accessor (for the Live Activity), and a `refresh()` that advances elapsed running timers to `.finished` and fires `onFinished` exactly once each (idempotent). All wall-clock reads go through an injected `clock`, so the L1 suite drives every transition with a controllable clock and no real waiting.
- **AC-47.2 (Live Activity + Dynamic Island).** The soonest-finishing running timer surfaces on the Lock Screen + Dynamic Island via an ActivityKit Live Activity (reusing the existing `DODAppLiveActivity` target), updating as it counts down and reflecting pause/finish.
- **AC-47.3 (completion alert).** When a timer finishes, the app fires a local notification + sound + haptic even when backgrounded/locked (notification permission is already requested for replies/new-recipe; denial degrades to an in-app alert).
- **AC-47.4 (Cook Mode timer tray + start-from-step).** Tapping a detected duration in a recipe step starts a timer named after the step/recipe; a compact tray in Cook Mode + recipe detail shows all active timers with pause/resume/cancel; timers persist across relaunch.
- **AC-47.5 (hands-free hook).** "Start a timer" via the Cook Mode voice commands (US — DUT-101) starts the current step's timer through the same engine, and the auto-advance behavior (DUT-173) keys off `onFinished`.

This is the keystone of the "cooking session" epic (E1): AC-47.1 is the foundation the rest of US-47 — plus DUT-101 (voice), DUT-102 (Campfire), DUT-107 (Siri/StandBy), DUT-141 (HomeKit), and DUT-173 (auto-advance) — build on.
### US-48 — Private cook journal ("I Made This")

As a cook, I want to privately log when I make a recipe — with my own photo, a note, and a personal rating — and see my cooking history, so I build a personal record (distinct from the public comments/ratings) that the app can celebrate over time.

- **AC-48.1 (data model + stats — T-805 / CL-199, IMPLEMENTED).** `CookLogEntry` (DODSupport) is a primitive-only value type (id, recipeID, recipeTitle snapshot, cookedAt, optional note / personal 1–5 rating clamped on init / photo local id) — decoupled from Domain + Persistence. `CookLogStats` (DODSupport) is a pure, `Calendar`-injected calculator over `[CookLogEntry]`: `totalCooks`, `timesCooked(recipeID:)`, `mostCooked` (ties → most recent, then lower id), `currentWeeklyStreak(asOf:calendar:)` (consecutive weeks with a cook, with a forgiving in-progress week — DUT-171's grace rule), and `busiestMonth` (ties → more recent). All deterministically L1-tested with fixed dates.
- **AC-48.2 (persistence).** Entries persist via SwiftData on the CloudKit **private** DB (same pattern as saved recipes), mapping to/from `CookLogEntry`. Never sent to the blog or analytics.
- **AC-48.3 (log a cook).** A "Made it" action on recipe detail creates an entry (auto date; optional photo via the existing photo/image-cache infra + note + personal rating).
- **AC-48.4 (My Kitchen history + badge).** A chronological "My Kitchen" view (timeline of cooks) and a per-recipe "made N times" badge on recipe detail.
- **AC-48.5 (habits build on the stats).** The cooking streak (DUT-171), achievement badges (DUT-175), and "Cooking Wrapped" (DUT-177) all read `CookLogStats` — so AC-48.1 is the shared foundation for the E7 habits sub-group.

This is the keystone of epic E2 (Save, Organize & Plan): AC-48.1 unblocks the habit features without waiting on the persistence/UI slices.
### US-49 — Hands-free Cook Mode voice commands

As a cook with messy hands, I want to say "next", "repeat", or "start a timer" instead of touching a greasy screen, so I can drive Cook Mode without setting down what I'm doing. (Complements the existing read-aloud, which is voice-OUT; this is voice-IN.)

- **AC-49.1 (command grammar — T-806 / CL-200, IMPLEMENTED).** `CookModeVoiceCommand` (DODSupport) is a pure transcript→command mapper: `parse(_:)` lowercases + trims a recognized transcript and resolves a small synonym set to `.next` / `.previous` / `.repeatStep` / `.startTimer` / `.pause` (most-specific phrases first so "start a timer" never reads as navigation), else `.unknown` (ignored). Pure → fully L1-tested without a microphone.
- **AC-49.2 (on-device recognition).** A mic toggle in Cook Mode runs `SFSpeechRecognizer` with `requiresOnDeviceRecognition` (privacy + offline), feeding transcripts to `parse(_:)`; needs the Speech permission + `NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription`.
- **AC-49.3 (Cook Mode integration).** Recognized commands drive Cook Mode navigation + TTS re-read; `.startTimer` starts the current step's timer via the `CookTimerEngine` (US-47). The spoken step pauses while listening (no feedback loop); permission denial degrades to touch.

This is part of epic E1 (Cooking Session Core), built on the US-47 timer engine.

---

## Cross-cutting acceptance criteria

These apply to every screen, not just one story.

- **CC-1 Accessibility:** every interactive element has a VoiceOver label; every screen respects Dynamic Type up to AX5; contrast meets WCAG AA in light and dark mode (per constitution §7).
- **CC-2 Offline banner:** any screen that depends on network shows a non-blocking offline banner when connectivity is lost; the banner auto-dismisses on reconnect.
- **CC-3 Loading states:** every async fetch shows a skeleton or spinner within 100ms; never a blank screen.
- **CC-4 Error states:** every fetch failure shows a human-readable message + Retry button. No raw error codes shown to users.
- **CC-5 Analytics scope:** only the events enumerated in constitution §9 are sent. No new event types without a constitution amendment.
- **CC-6 App Privacy label:** v1 collects only Usage Data (Product Interaction), not linked to identity, not used for tracking. The App Store privacy questionnaire must match this exactly.
- **CC-7 Performance:** screens hit the budgets in constitution §8 (cold launch <1.5s, 60fps lists, recipe detail open <300ms cached / <1.5s fetched).
- **CC-8 iPad layouts:** every screen has an adaptive iPad layout. List + detail uses `NavigationSplitView` on iPad (sidebar + content), stack on iPhone.
- **CC-9 Visual density (added by consultant-pass amendment, CL-18):** the four list surfaces — Feed (US-1), Categories recipe lists (US-2), Search results (US-3), and Saved (US-5) — render as a **2-column grid** on compact horizontal size class (iPhone in portrait) and a **3-column grid** on regular horizontal size class (iPad and large iPhones in landscape). The previous 1-column compact default is retired. RecipeCard hero image height is tuned so at least **3 rows are visible above the fold** on iPhone 13 baseline in light mode at default Dynamic Type. Plan T-300 implements; snapshot tests at iPhone 13 + iPad 12.9" lock the new layout.

---

## Non-functional requirements

- **NFR-1 Offline-first cache:** the most recent 100 viewed recipes are cached automatically (LRU). Saved recipes are pinned and not evicted.
- **NFR-2 Image cache budget:** disk image cache capped at 200 MB; oldest unsaved-recipe images evicted first.
- **NFR-3 No surprise data use:** background refresh disabled by default; the app does not fetch when not in foreground in v1.
- **NFR-4 App Store readiness:** App Privacy questionnaire, screenshots for 6.5" and 6.7" iPhone + 12.9" iPad, and a marketing description are part of the release definition of done (Phase 5 task).

---

## Test pyramid (added by Phase 6 amendment 2026-05-23)

Two production bugs surfaced on first simulator run (TelemetryDeck pre-init crash; missing hero images because `_fields` filtered out `_embed` payload). Neither was caught by L1 unit tests with fake dependencies — both required an actual app launch hitting the real backend.

Mandates (per constitution §6):

- **AC-T1** Every PR runs L1 (unit), L3 (UI smoke), L4 (visual regression). Failing any blocks merge.
- **AC-T2** L3 smoke must cover: app launch without crash, all four tabs reachable, feed shows at least one recipe with a non-placeholder hero image, recipe detail open + back, save toggle visible.
- **AC-T3** L2 live-API integration runs nightly. Failing layer raises an issue but does not block in-flight PRs; treat it as a contract-drift early warning.
- **AC-T5** (added by Phase 11 amendment 2026-05-26, CL-58) **L5 end-to-end user journeys** run on four CI trigger surfaces, **not** every PR. The layer exists in scheme `DODAppE2ETests`, sourced from `UITests/E2E/**`, and is XCUITest-based (same framework as L3 — explicitly not Detox/Maestro/WebDriverAgent per CL-58). Triggers: (1) `pull_request` only when the PR carries the `e2e` label, (2) `push` to `main`, (3) `workflow_dispatch`, (4) nightly cron `0 7 * * *`. The `ci-required` aggregator treats a skipped `test-e2e` as success — so a PR without the `e2e` label is not blocked by L5. The PR-author checklist for whether to apply the label lives in `CONTRIBUTING.md` § "Does my PR need E2E?". The seed five user journeys (open → tap recipe → see detail; search → tap result → see detail; save → confirm → unsave → empty state; Cook Mode 2-step walk + Done; widget deep-link → recipe detail) ship in T-603 and may be extended by future PRs. Test-data strategy: launch via `XCUIApplication.launchArguments += ["-DOD_E2E_MODE=1"]`, with the host-side fake-dependencies switch deferred to T-610 (the audit doc tracks this gap). Implemented by Phase 11 cluster T-600..T-605.
- **AC-T4** Regression tests for bugs surfaced post-launch must land in the same PR as the fix. Specifically:
  - **REG-1**: L3 test that the app launches without crashing when no `TelemetryDeckAppID` is set in Info.plist.
  - **REG-2**: L2 test that `WPRestClient.posts()` returns at least one item with a non-nil `heroImage` URL.
  - **REG-DOD-NAV-1**: tapping a recipe row pushes the detail screen and it stays pushed. Failure mode: `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` dropped `canonicalURL` on insert, so the detail fetch fell back to the homepage, JSON-LD parse failed, and AC-4.11's auto-dismiss popped the user back to the feed. Locked by `DODPersistenceTests.canonicalURLRoundTrips`, `DODPersistenceTests.canonicalURLUpdatesButDoesNotClobberOnNil`, and `SmokeTests.test_recipeDetailOpensAndShowsContent`.
  - **REG-DOD-LIST-SCROLL**: vertical drag inside a recipe row scrolls the surrounding list. Failure mode: `Button { } label: { RecipeCard }.buttonStyle(.plain)` inside a `LazyVGrid` inside a `ScrollView` swallowed the pan gesture on iOS 26. Locked by `SmokeTests.test_feedScrollsToRevealMoreRecipes`.
  - **REG-INFO-PLIST-CLOBBER**: `xcodegen generate` must not strip launch-screen / orientation keys from `App/Info.plist`. Failure mode: those keys lived only in the hand-maintained plist; XcodeGen rewrote it from `project.yml` on every regenerate, silently re-introducing the iOS letterbox bug. Locked by keeping the keys in `project.yml`'s `info.properties` block; `SmokeTests.test_appLaunchesWithoutTelemetryAppID` proves the app renders at launch (does not yet pixel-verify edge-to-edge — noted gap).
  - **REG-9**: the widget snapshot wire format the host app writes (`WidgetSnapshotStore.write`) round-trips losslessly through the widget extension's reader, and the small/medium widget layouts don't drift visually. Locked by `WidgetSnapshotStoreTests` (DODSupport: round-trip, max-entries cap, version-mismatch rejection, clear), `WidgetDeepLinkParserTests` (DODSupport: 8 cases covering `dod://recipe/<id>` and `dod://feed` plus rejection of malformed and hostile URLs), and `DesignSystemSnapshotTests.test_widgetCard_{small,medium}_populated` + `test_widgetCard_placeholder` (DODDesignSystem: pixel-locked baselines for the three layouts).
  - **REG-10**: deep-link URLs from App Intents / Siri Shortcuts / Spotlight must round-trip through `DeepLinkIntent.parse(_:)` and `DeepLinkIntent.url` without losing their target id or action. Failure mode would be Siri opening the wrong recipe (or the homepage) when a user says "Open Bourbon Berry Cake" — silently incorrect from the user's perspective. Locked by the round-trip cases in `DODSupportTests.DeepLinkIntentTests` and the entity-lookup tests in `DODPersistenceTests.RecentlyViewedTests` (`recipeWithoutTouchingDoesNotBumpLastViewedAt` in particular, which guards against Siri suggestions promoting the wrong recipe in subsequent LRU queries).
  - **REG-11 (US-11)**: Cook Mode Live Activity lifecycle is fully exercised by the `CookModeViewModel` unit suite — start, end, replace-on-new-timer, no-op on tick when no activity, and end-on-cook-mode-exit are each pinned by a named test in `CookModeViewModelTests`. The lock-screen and Dynamic Island compact views are pinned by `CookLiveActivitySnapshotTests`. Failure mode: the cook would lose track of the buzzer the moment the screen dimmed and would either over-cook or have to keep the app foregrounded for the full duration.
  - **REG-12 (US-12)**: ingredient index round-trip, merger rank, and filter-composition logic stay correct as Search v2 evolves. Locked by `DODPersistenceTests.IngredientIndexTests` (write-on-mergeDetail, substring match, case-insensitive, short-query guard, re-merge replaces rows), `DODPersistenceTests.SearchFilterInputsTests` (category / total-time / recently-viewed accessors), `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchResultMergerTests` (title > excerpt > local-only tier ordering and dedupe), `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchFiltersTests` (each chip + composed), `DODFeatureSearchTests.RecentSearchesTests` (LRU dedupe + trim + clear), and `DODFeatureSearchTests` view-model coverage of `filterChipNarrowsResultsWithoutNetworkRoundTrip`, `recentSearchesPersistAcrossViewModelInstances`, and `offlineWithLocalIngredientHitsStillShowsResults`.
  - **REG-13 (US-13)**: WPRM rating round-trip. Locked by `DODNetworkingTests.WPRMRatingsClientTests` (summary read fixture + post body shape) and `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests.RatingViewModelTests` (state machine after successful + failed post).
  - **REG-14 (US-14)**: WP comment list pagination + post payload + `hold` status handling. Locked by `DODNetworkingTests.WPCommentsClientTests` and `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests.CommentsViewModelTests`.
  - **REG-15 (US-15)**: Keychain round-trip for the guest identity. Locked by `DODSupportTests.GuestIdentityStoreTests` (write → read → overwrite → clear). Note that the test uses a Keychain access group scoped to the test bundle so it doesn't leak into the device keychain.
  - **REG-21 (US-5 / US-12 / US-29 / US-36 / US-42, T-638)**: four round-9 behaviors are pinned by automated tests so a future regression fails on the next CI run rather than slipping through to a manual verify pass. **(a) Saved-tab long-press → "Unsave" context menu removes the card instantly** (T-635 / CL-104 — `SavedViewModel.optimisticallyRemove(id:)` is called before the async store toggle so the user sees the row disappear in the same frame). **(b) Search-tab cook-time filter actually narrows results via hydration** (T-637 / CL-106 — `SearchViewModel.hydrateMissingTotalSeconds()` fetches network-side `totalSeconds` for uncached items when the cook-time filter is active, so the filter narrows fresh REST hits instead of rejecting them all per the "missing = MISS" rule). **(c) Search-tab "Latest Recipes" Try-pill returns the recent-recipes branch** (T-637 / CL-106 — `SearchViewModel.surfaceLatestRecipes(limit:)` fetches via `WPRestClient.posts(page: 1, perPage: ceil(limit * 1.5))` instead of `client.search(query: "Latest Recipes")` which returned garbage because the literal phrase appears in many unrelated article posts' boilerplate). **(d) Settings page does not expose a DEBUG "Simulate New Post" button** (T-633 / CL-102 — the `#if DEBUG` scaffold was deleted after the end-to-end notification path was verified). Failure modes: (a) the Saved card lingers until tab-switch-back (CL-104's documented pre-fix behavior); (b) every uncached fresh search result is filtered out when cook-time is on (CL-106 part 2's documented pre-fix behavior); (c) "Latest Recipes" tap returns a scatter of unrelated articles whose boilerplate contains the phrase (CL-106 part 3's documented pre-fix behavior); (d) a future revert or accidental re-add of the DEBUG scaffold re-introduces dead test plumbing (CL-102's "keeping dead scaffolding around invites drift" rationale). Locked at **L5** by `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests.test_long_press_unsave_from_saved_tab` (saves a recipe, long-presses the Saved-tab card, asserts the menu reads "Unsave" not "Save," taps Unsave, asserts the card is gone within 0.5s — the frame-tight window is the load-bearing assertion that the optimistic removal fired before the slower `.task` reconciliation could mask the bug), `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests.test_search_chip_row_hidden_on_idle` + `test_search_chip_row_visible_after_query` (asserts the cook-time chip is NOT queryable via `dod.search.cookTimeChip` on the idle Search tab and IS queryable after typing a query — pins CL-106's `viewModel.state != .idle` gate), `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests.test_search_cook_time_filter_narrows_results` (types "chicken," captures initial count, taps cook-time chip → "≤ 30 min," polls with a 15s expectation for the result count to change, asserts filtered count ≤ initial — the polling-wait pattern documented in CL-107 is the canonical shape for hydration-dependent assertions, and the count-change assertion catches the T-632-pattern bug class where a cache-only lookup silently hides a feature), `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests.test_search_latest_recipes_pill_returns_recent_branch` (taps the `dod.search.tryPill.latestRecipes`-identified pill on the idle Search tab, asserts the result count lands in the 3–8 range — limit=5 with over-fetch=8 and article-trim — which discriminates against the failure mode of a literal text search returning either ~0 or many random matches). Locked at **L3** by `SmokeTests.test_settingsPageHasNoDebugTestButton` (navigates to Settings via the gear icon, asserts `app.switches["Notify me when new recipes drop"]` exists as the Settings-reachable positive signal, asserts `app.buttons["Test: Simulate New Post"]` does NOT exist as the T-633 deletion pin). The L5 surface adds three new stable accessibility identifiers to support deterministic test queries: `dod.search.cookTimeChip` (on the cook-time chip in `FilterChipRow`), `dod.search.tryPill.latestRecipes` (conditionally on the matching Try pill in `IdleSuggestionsView`), `dod.saved.card` (on the `RecipeCard` in `SavedView`'s `.loaded` arm). See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-107 for the full rationale + the deliberately-skipped surfaces (Try-pill glyph and destructive-trash color — both XCUITest cannot cleanly observe; already pinned by L4 snapshot tests).
  - **REG-22 (US-22)**: the lock-screen `.accessoryRectangular` widget renders the latest recipe's title + short description as text only (no image), and tap-targets correctly through the existing `dod://recipe/<id>` / `dod://feed` URL grammar. Locked by `LockScreenWidgetSnapshotTests` (DODDesignSystem: populated / populated-long-title / empty pinned at the system `.accessoryRectangular` frame), reuses `WidgetSnapshotStoreTests` for the snapshot wire-format coverage (no new snapshot type — same `WidgetSnapshot` the home-screen widget reads), and reuses `WidgetDeepLinkParserTests` for the URL coverage (no new parser cases). Failure mode: the cook would unlock the phone, scroll the feed, and re-find what was supposed to be one glance away — the widget's whole job is the one-glance affordance.
  - **REG-16 (US-1, T-420)**: when a recipe is published on dutchovendaddy.com, the app's feed-load path (`WPRestClient.posts()`, the same call the production `LiveFeedDependencies.fetchPosts(page:)` wraps and `FeedViewModel.refresh()` invokes) picks it up on the next refresh, and the newest post's `wp:featuredmedia` embed round-trips into a non-nil `heroImage` URL on the actual live feed (not just on the fixture REG-2 pins). Locked by `DODIntegrationTestsTests.LiveAPITests.newestPostIsReachableViaFeedRefresh` and `DODIntegrationTestsTests.LiveAPITests.newestPostHasNonNilHeroImage` (live-API tier, runs nightly per AC-T3, gated behind `DOD_RUN_LIVE_TESTS=1` so PR builds skip it). Failure mode: the WP REST `/wp/v2/posts` shape on the live blog drifts (a plugin update changes `_embed` payload semantics, a server config strips a field) and the app silently stops surfacing newly-published recipes — REG-2 wouldn't catch it because REG-2 only proves the fixture round-trips, and the broken state could persist across PR cycles until a user noticed. REG-16 catches the drift on the next nightly run.
  - **REG-18 (US-1, T-510)**: `WPRestClient.get(path:queryItems:)` bypasses both `URLCache.shared` (via `URLRequest.cachePolicy = .reloadIgnoringLocalCacheData`) AND Cloudflare's edge CDN (via the request header `Cache-Control: no-cache`), so every call to `posts()` / `categories()` / `search()` / etc. forces a fresh round-trip to origin. Locked by `DODIntegrationTestsTests.LiveAPITests.feedRefreshBypassesStaleURLCacheEntry` (live-API tier, runs nightly per AC-T3, gated behind `DOD_RUN_LIVE_TESTS=1`). Failure mode: the WP REST `/wp/v2/posts` response carries `Last-Modified` but no `Cache-Control` header, so iOS `URLSession.shared` applies HTTP heuristic freshness (~`0.1 * (now - Last-Modified)`) and serves a stale page-1 batch on the next pull-to-refresh — meanwhile Cloudflare's edge cache holds a separate multi-minute copy that can predate a just-published recipe. The two caches stack: even when the device's URLCache misses, the CDN can return a pre-publish JSON payload and the newest post stays invisible until both caches expire. T-420's REG-16 nightly test doesn't catch this because it runs in a fresh `swift test` process with an empty URLCache against a CDN that may happen to be fresh at test time — the failure only surfaces under the user-driven repeated-refresh pattern. REG-18's test plants a known-stale `CachedURLResponse` in `URLCache.shared` for the production posts URL and asserts that `WPRestClient.posts()` returns the *live* newest post id rather than the planted decoy — fails on `origin/main` (URLSession serves the planted cache entry), passes after CL-50's two-line cache-bypass fix lands. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-50 for the full root-cause rationale and the rejected-alternatives trail.
  - **REG-17 (US-12, T-530)**: filter composition stays AND-wise across both the category chip and the cook-time ("Any time") chip for **fresh REST search hits** — i.e. recipes returned by `WPRestClient.search(query:)` whose recipe-detail page has **not** yet been opened (no JSON-LD parse has run, so `mergeDetail(_:)` hasn't populated `CachedRecipe.categoryIDs` from the parsed payload). Failure mode: WP REST returns `Post.categories: [Int]` on every search hit, but `WPDTO.Post.toRecipeListItem(heroImage:)` dropped those category IDs and `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` never wrote them to the cache row, so `RecipeStore.categoryIDs(forRecipeIDs:)` returned `[recipeID: []]` for every just-cached REST result. `SearchFilters.apply(...)` line 56 checks `categoryIDsByRecipe[item.id]?.contains(categoryID) == true` — and `[]?.contains(10)` is `false`, so picking a category chip dropped every fresh REST hit. The user then layered the Any-time chip on top, expecting a narrower set; the AND with the cook-time predicate yielded zero results because the category-filtered set was already empty. From the user's seat, this read as "the Any-time filter doesn't appear to be composing correctly with the category filter" (round-6 backlog item 6). CL-49.1's accepted trade-off was for the *idle-state "Try" pill* path (which T-500 routed away from `filters.categoryID` and into the query field); the user-driven chip toggle path **was not addressed by T-500** and is the surface this regression locks. Locked by `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchResultMergerTests.composeCategoryAndCookTimeFiltersForFreshRESTResults` (the new contract — given a recipe in category C with totalTime T, the merged-then-filtered result set with `filters = {categoryID: C, cookTime: bucket-containing-T}` includes the recipe; with `cookTime: bucket-strictly-below-T` it does NOT), plus `DODPersistenceTests.RecipeStoreCategoryIDsRoundTripFromListItem` (the cache-side contract — `cache(listItem:)` preserves `categoryIDs` so `categoryIDs(forRecipeIDs:)` returns the populated value for a never-opened recipe). Also pinned by an additive case in `DODNetworkingTests.WPRestClientPostsTests.decodesAndMapsToRecipeListItem` asserting `item.categoryIDs == [1590, 334]` from the existing fixture so the DTO → RecipeListItem mapping doesn't silently regress. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-53 for the full root-cause rationale, the AC-12.3 contract analysis, and the rejected-alternatives trail (pre-hydrate-on-filter, opt-in on RecipeListItem, store-side category injection method).
  - **REG-19 (US-29, T-670)**: tapping a curated "Try" suggestion pill in the idle empty state does **NOT** persist the tapped term into the recent-searches store, and tapping the Recent section's "Clear All" button leaves `SearchViewModel.recentSearches` empty even when a debounced search is in flight at the moment of the tap. Failure mode: the round-7 wiring routed `onCategoryTap` straight through `viewModel.query = category.name`, which triggered the normal `scheduleSearch` → `performSearch` path and called `recents.record(trimmed)` on completion — so curated category names like "Bourbon", "Sweet Potato", "Brisket" leaked into Recent after a single curated tap. Compounding the leak, `clearRecentSearches()` did not cancel the in-flight debounce, so a `performSearch` that started just before Clear All could complete *after* the wipe and re-populate the store, making Clear All look broken from the user's seat. Locked by `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchViewModelTests.curatedTapDoesNotRecordRecentAndClearAllLeavesRecentEmpty` — the new contract pins three curated taps ("Bourbon", "Sweet Potato", "Brisket") followed by a real typed query ("pasta") followed by Clear All followed by another curated tap, asserting `viewModel.recentSearches.isEmpty` at each curated/cleared boundary. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-66 for the root cause + the `selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)` + `debounceTask?.cancel()` fix shape. **Empty-recents contract pin:** after `clearRecentSearches()`, the Recent section MUST render zero rows and the "Recent" header MUST be hidden — `IdleSuggestionsView` already conditions the section on `if !recents.isEmpty` (no fallback to Try-pill data inside the Recent section), and REG-19's L1 view-model test guarantees the empty input.
  - **REG-20 (US-42, T-632)**: a notification (or any) deep-link for a post that is **not in the local cache** fetches the post from the WP REST API and routes to its detail screen — **recipe → recipe detail, article → `ArticleDetailView`** — rather than silently ignoring the tap. Failure mode (the confirmed T-631 bug this locks): `RootView.resolveRecipeRoute(id:autoStartCookMode:)` resolved **cache-only** via `RecipeStore.recipeWithoutTouching(id:)` and returned `nil` ("recipe not in cache, ignoring") on a miss. That is correct for widget/Spotlight deep links (they always point at an already-cached recipe), but US-42 notifications announce **newly-published** posts, which are *never* cached — so every notification tap opened nothing, making the feature inert for its only real use case. The fetch-on-miss path mirrors how a normal feed-row tap hydrates an uncached recipe: fetch the post by id (`WPRestClient.post(id:)` → `RecipeListItem` carrying `canonicalURL`), push `.recipe(item:)`, and let `RecipeDetailViewModel.fetchAndParse()` run the JSON-LD parse / article-classification (AC-4.11 / AC-37.2) that decides recipe-vs-article — so no new route vocabulary is introduced and PostKind is honored by the detail screen's existing classification. Cache-hit behavior is **unchanged** (widgets/Spotlight stay network-free + don't bump the recently-viewed LRU, preserving REG-10); only a miss triggers a fetch, and a genuinely-unresolvable id (post deleted, or JSON-LD + article-body both fail per CL-9) still degrades gracefully to "ignore" — now with a distinct `deep link: recipe <id> fetch failed` log line so a fetch failure is diagnosable separately from a plain cache miss. Locked at L1 by `DODAppUnitTests.RecipeRouteResolverTests` (cached id → returns route without invoking the fetch closure; uncached id → invokes the fetch + returns a route carrying the fetched `canonicalURL`; uncached id whose fetch resolves an article → returns a `.recipe(item:)` route carrying the article's post so the detail screen opens `ArticleDetailView`; uncached id whose fetch throws → returns `nil`) and by `DODNetworkingTests.WPRestClientPostsTests.fetchesSinglePostByID` (the `GET /wp/v2/posts/<id>?_embed=wp:featuredmedia` round-trip into a `RecipeListItem` with a populated `canonicalURL`). The actual tap-to-open is exercised in the simulator via the DEBUG "Simulate New Post" affordance, which now fires **real** WP post ids (article 23406, recipe 21238) so the fetch path has a live target. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-101 for the root-cause + fetch-on-miss rationale + the real-test-id swap.
  - **REG-23 (US-39, T-681..T-689)**: the Shopping List feature does not POST, GET, or otherwise contact dutchovendaddy.com or any other network endpoint for the ingredient text, the list contents, the share payload, or the user's check state — the only network egress associated with the feature is the two `shoppingListItemAdded(recipeID:aisle:)` + `shoppingListShared(itemCount:)` TelemetryDeck events per AC-39.10. Failure mode would be a future contributor inadvertently wiring the shopping list to a WordPress endpoint (e.g. trying to sync the list across devices via a custom WP REST route, or adding a "share to dutchovendaddy.com" surface) and silently leaking raw ingredient text + recipe-attribution metadata to the blog's server — a privacy regression that violates constitution §9's "no raw user input strings to the network" rule and that the AC-39.12 contract bullet locks against. Locked at L1 by `DODFeatureShoppingListTests.ShoppingListViewModelMakesZeroNetworkCalls` (a `RecordingURLProtocol`-instrumented test that asserts the view-model + persistence path issues zero `URLSession.shared.dataTask(with:)` calls when the user adds-from-recipe, toggles, clears, and shares); locked at L2 by an additive case in `DODIntegrationTestsTests.LiveAPITests` that exercises the shopping-list path against a deterministic fixture and asserts the request log contains no new dutchovendaddy.com hostnames or paths beyond the pre-existing list-fetch + recipe-detail GETs; locked at L4 by the `ShoppingListView` snapshot baselines (no network indicator UI ever appears on the surface). See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-68 for the rationale on the no-cloud-sync v1 trade-off.
  - **REG-25 (US-41, T-702 + T-708)**: the CloudKit sync adapter (`CloudKitSyncAdapter` in `DODPersistence`) accesses **only** the user's `CKContainer.privateCloudDatabase` (via the SwiftData `ModelConfiguration(_:groupContainer:cloudKitDatabase: .private(...))` adapter) plus `CKContainer.accountStatus(completionHandler:)` for the AC-41.7 status surface; the source must not reference `CKContainer.publicCloudDatabase`, `CKContainer.sharedCloudDatabase`, or any `discoverUserIdentity*` / `requestApplicationPermission(.userDiscoverability, ...)` / `CKUserIdentity` API surface. Failure mode would be a future contributor adding "find friends who saved this recipe" or "share saved-recipes list publicly" — both of which would (a) trigger a system permission prompt the v1 questionnaire is not prepared for, (b) introduce a new privacy disclosure on App Store Connect (Identifiers — Device ID, or worse Contacts), and (c) move data from "the user's own iCloud space" to "discoverable / shared / public" which is a different App Privacy category entirely. Locked at L1 by `DODPersistenceTests.CloudKitContainerSurfaceTests.onlyPrivateDatabaseIsAccessed` (a build-time + runtime assertion that inspects the symbol set referenced by `CloudKitSyncAdapter.swift` and verifies the allowed-API list matches exactly the two surfaces above; the test reads the compiled binary's symbol table via `nm` in the `BUILD_DIR` and fails if any of the four banned symbols appear in the adapter's transitive call graph), plus a complementary L1 assertion `DODPersistenceTests.CloudKitContainerSurfaceTests.noDiscoverabilityAPI` that scans the package source for the string `discoverUserIdentity` / `userDiscoverability` / `publicCloudDatabase` / `sharedCloudDatabase` and fails on any hit (a belt-and-braces lint to catch a future grep-able addition). See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-88 for the full rationale on the private-DB-only / no-Discoverability posture.
  - **REG-26 (US-41, T-702 + T-708)**: the app remains fully functional under `CKAccountStatus = .noAccount` (the user is not signed into iCloud) and under `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled = false` (the user declined the AC-41.2 opt-in or toggled sync off via AC-41.3). Failure mode would be a regression that wires a feature surface to assume CloudKit availability — e.g., a guard that hides the Save button when the iCloud account is unavailable, a Recipe Detail screen that crashes when CKContainer initialization fails, or an L3 smoke test that newly assumes a CK-signed-in simulator. App Store Review Guideline 5.1.1(ii) explicitly requires the app work without a login; CL-86 pinned this as a non-negotiable AC, and REG-26 is the test surface that locks it. Locked at L3 by `SmokeTests.test_appLaunchesUnderNoiCloudAccount` (the existing smoke pyramid — `XCUIApplication.launchArguments += ["-DOD_CLOUDKIT_FAKE_ACCOUNT_STATUS=noAccount"]` flips a host-side fake during launch per T-708's wiring, the smoke then exercises the standard launch + tab-reachability + recipe-detail-open + save-toggle-visible checks per AC-T2 and asserts identical pass/fail to the existing baseline); locked at L5 by `E2EJourneys.test_fullGoldenPathUnderNoiCloudAccount` (the seed L5 contract per CL-58 + AC-T5 — same launch-flag posture, runs the open → tap recipe → see detail → save → unsave journey and asserts identical end-state to the existing baseline, gated by the `e2e` label per CL-58); locked at L1 by `DODFeatureSettingsTests.SettingsRowReachableWhenNotSignedIn` (the Settings → iCloud Sync row's no-account sublabel "Sign into iCloud to enable sync" is asserted to render without crash, tapping the row asserts the system Settings URL is fetched, **not** a custom in-app sign-in surface). See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-86 for the rationale on the "no login UI, no account creation" posture and how REG-26 closes the App Store 5.1.1(ii) compliance loop.
  - **REG-27 (US-14, 2026-05-29)**: defensive medicine for the round-9 backlog "comments are broken on TestFlight 1.0 (2)" investigation. Two contracts locked. **(1) `URLSessionHTTPClient` stamps a `User-Agent` header on every outgoing request** (default value built from `Bundle.main`'s `CFBundleShortVersionString` + `CFBundleVersion` + `CFBundleIdentifier` + the compile-time `os(iOS)` / `os(macOS)` platform, format `DODApp/<v>.<b> (<platform>; <bundle-id>)`) so WordPress security plugins (Wordfence is the canonical example) don't 4xx a request from an empty-UA client. Caller-supplied User-Agent headers are preserved unchanged. Locked at L1 by `DODNetworkingTests.URLSessionHTTPClientTests.stampsDefaultUserAgentWhenCallerSetsNone` + `doesNotOverwriteCallerSuppliedUserAgent` + `defaultUserAgentReturnsNonEmptyDODAppString`. The tests use a `RecordingURLProtocol` test-double that captures the outgoing request without round-tripping to the live blog (constitution §6 L5-doesn't-write-to-live-blog corollary). **(2) `RecipeDetailViewModel.commentErrorSnackbar(for:)` translates the thrown error into a category-specific user-visible snackbar** — `WPClientError.networkUnavailable` → "You're offline — comment will need to wait.", `.timeout` → "The server took too long — try again.", `.httpStatus(code)` → "Couldn't post your comment (server said `<code>`).", `.decoding` → "Couldn't read the server's reply — try again.", `.underlying` → "Couldn't post your comment — try again." — so a TestFlight reporter (or @adamsned on his iPhone) can read the chip and tell us which of the round-9 hypotheses fired. Before REG-27 every failure collapsed to one generic message and remote diagnosis was impossible. Locked at L1 by `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests.CommentErrorSnackbarTests` (one test per `WPClientError` case + one test for the `URLError`-not-pre-wrapped safety net). Note: REG-27 is **observability + defensive hardening**, NOT a confirmed root-cause fix — the round-9 backlog entry's H1..H5 hypothesis list stays open until the next TestFlight build's surfaced snackbar pins which hypothesis was correct. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-108 for the full rationale.
  - **DUT-7 (US-14 / AC-14.4, 2026-05-31, T-737)**: extends REG-27's comment-failure observability + closes the remaining code-side gaps for the Urgent "comments are broken on the installed TestFlight build" report. **Observability + defensive hardening, NOT a confirmed root-cause fix** — the H1..H5 hypothesis list stays open (and #87 / REG-27 may already have resolved it); the owner must still retest on-device + check WP admin before this is closed. Three contracts locked. **(1) No silent failure — surface WordPress's reason.** `WPCommentsClient.postComment` parses WP's REST error body (`{"code","message","data":{"status"}}`) on every non-2xx and throws a new additive `WPClientError.httpStatusWithBody(Int, message:)`; `RecipeDetailViewModel.commentErrorSnackbar(for:)` maps it to "Couldn't post your comment (server said `<code>`): `<WP's reason>`". A Wordfence-style HTML challenge body is tag-stripped + clamped to 140 chars. Bodyless errors fall back to REG-27's `.httpStatus(code)`; the four other `WPClientError.httpStatus(_:)` call sites (`WPRestClient` / `ImageLoader` / `RecipePageFetcher` / `WPRMRatingsClient`) are byte-identical — only the comment path opts into body surfacing. **(2) Author-identity guard (hypothesis #4).** A fresh-install / partially-saved Keychain row can return an identity with an empty / whitespace name or email (`GuestIdentityStore.load()` only nils a *missing* field) → posting `author_email=""` → WP 400 → "tap, nothing happens". `RecipeDetailViewModel.submitComment()` re-gates behind the US-15 guest-identity sheet + shows "Add your name and email to post a comment." (and trims whitespace off a valid identity before POSTing); `WPCommentsClient.postComment` rejects an empty name/email with a typed error *before* the request fires. **(3) On-device diagnostics.** `WPCommentsClient.postComment` logs request URL + post ID on send, status + WP message on failure, status + comment id + WP moderation status on success — under subsystem `com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp` / category `network`, privacy-safe (`.public` on URL / status / id only; never the body or author PII) so the owner can pin the actual hypothesis via Console.app. **(4) ATS (hypothesis #5) verified, no change** — `App/Info.plist` declares no `NSAppTransportSecurity`, the endpoint is HTTPS, so ATS does not block the POST (a non-HTTPS redirect would be blocked, but the canonical host is HTTPS-only). Locked at L1/L2 by `DODNetworkingTests.WPCommentsClientPostFailureTests` (WP JSON body → `httpStatusWithBody`; HTML challenge stripped; 401/403/422 bodyless → `httpStatus`; empty email/name rejected pre-POST with captured-request proof; `extractWPErrorMessage` JSON/HTML/empty/blank/clamp coverage), `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests.CommentErrorSnackbarTests.httpStatusWithBodySurfacesCodeAndServerMessage`, and `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests.RecipeDetailCommentSubmitTests` (blank-email re-gate-no-POST; whitespace-trim-then-post; server-error surfaced end-to-end). All comment-write coverage stays at L1/L2 against the fake clients — no write reaches the live blog (constitution §6; no `e2e` label). See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-134 for the #87-vs-T-737 split + the owner-verification checklist.
  - **REG-29 (US-12 / US-29 / US-3, T-642)**: search for a query returns **only** results whose title contains the query (or a plural/Levenshtein-1 variant of it); body-matched-only results never surface. Failure mode is the Nacho-Bug class: WP's REST `?search=` body-matching ranker surfaces unrelated posts whose body merely mentions the typed word ("Cast Iron Bacon Wrapped Pickles" appearing for `?search=nachos`), and the candidate set is truncated so real title matches get buried past the page cutoff ("Cast Iron Skillet Nachos" dropping below position 20 of 20). The fix pairs a wider candidate pool (`WPRestClient.searchPageSize = 100`, up from `defaultPageSize = 20`) with a client-side title-precision filter (`TitleSearchMatcher.match(query:title:)` applied in `SearchResultMerger.merge(...)`, three tiers — `.exact` > `.substring` > `.fuzzy` — with fuzzy covering plural/singular swap and Levenshtein-1 typo). Locked at L1 by `DODSupportTests.TitleSearchMatcherTests` (17 cases — exact / substring / case-insensitive / plural-fuzzy both directions / Levenshtein-1 / Levenshtein-2-rejects / verbatim Cast-Iron-Bacon-Wrapped-Pickles no-match / HTML-entity normalization / multi-token all-must-match / empty inputs / tier ordering / the four known live-API title matches), at L2 by `DODNetworkingTests.WPRestClientSearchTests.searchUsesWidePageSizeForTitlePrecision` (pins the per_page=100 bump on the SEARCH path independently of the list endpoints' `defaultPageSize=20`), and at L1 (pipeline integration) by `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchResultMergerTests.bodyOnlyMatchesAreDropped` + `.nachoBugFixSurfacesFourKnownTitleMatches` (the live-API end-to-end seal: a candidate set of 4 known nacho-titled posts + 4 body-only false positives surfaces exactly the 4 titles after the filter). The local-ingredient pass is **v1-deferred** for this fix (preserved on the merger signature for source-compat but ignored at runtime) — ingredient search returns as a labeled tier in dad's broader "Make search way better" backlog entry. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-120 for the full rationale, the considered alternatives (`wp-json/wp/v2/search` endpoint, stem-based fuzzy, Levenshtein-2, server-side WP plugin), and the live-API truth (4 title matches actually exist for `?search=nacho`; today's app surfaces 3 and adds 17 false positives, post-fix surfaces 4 and adds 0).
  - **REG-32 (US-3 / US-29 / US-19, T-648)**: the Search-tab and Categories-tab search bars share the same `DODSearchField` component from `DODDesignSystem` — both render with `DODColor.surfaceElevated` (brand brown) background filled into a `Capsule(style: .continuous)` shape, with `.padding(.vertical, DODSpacing.sm).padding(.horizontal, DODSpacing.md)` for the pill proportion, the `magnifyingglass` leading glyph in `DODColor.labelSecondary`, a `TextField` bound to the caller's text Binding with `.autocorrectionDisabled()` + `dodFont(DODType.body)` + `DODColor.label` foreground, and a conditional `xmark.circle.fill` clear button rendered when text is non-empty (accessibility label "Clear"). Failure mode this locks: a future contributor restores `.searchable` on the Categories tab (re-introducing the dark-mode `secondarySystemBackground` near-black surface that doesn't match the rest of the app) or swaps Search's `Capsule` back to a `RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius:)` (re-introducing the moderate-radius shape mismatch with the rotating Try-pill capsules below it). Both surfaces ship the same component — the only per-call-site arguments are `placeholder` ("Search recipes" on Search, "Search" on Categories), the `text` binding (`$viewModel.query` on Search, `$searchText` on Categories), and the optional `onClear` closure (Search supplies `{ viewModel.clear() }` to preserve the VM-side `state` / `items` / `lastQuery` / debounce-cancel cleanup; Categories accepts the default text-clear). Locked at **L1** by `DODDesignSystemTests.ComponentsTests` (three new cases — `dodSearchFieldConstructsWithTextBinding`, `dodSearchFieldClearButtonAppearsOnlyWhenTextIsNonEmpty`, `dodSearchFieldOnClearClosureFiresWhenSupplied`). Locked at **L4** by the re-recorded `SearchViewSnapshotTests` baselines (pill-shape pixel-diff vs the prior `RoundedRectangle` corner) and the re-recorded `CategoryListViewSnapshotTests` baselines (the search field is now a sticky content-area pill above the `List` rather than a system nav-bar drawer — both surfaces snapshot at the same `Capsule` + `surfaceElevated` visual). Both record at `.missing` on first sim test run. The two L5-side accessibility identifiers `dod.search.field.search` and `dod.search.field.categories` are added at the call sites (not on the component itself) so the same component can serve multiple surfaces without identifier collisions; the pre-T-648 `app.textFields["Search recipes"]` placeholder-as-label query path continues to work on Search so the existing `SmokeTests` + `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests` queries pass unchanged. The deliberate trade for visual unity: Categories' search bar no longer slides into a nav-bar drawer the way `.searchable` does — it sits as a sticky element above the List. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-126 for the full rationale, the rejected alternatives (`UISearchBar.appearance()` proxy global skin, re-skin Search-only, `DODSupport` placement, per-call-site radius/tint parameters), and the call-site contract for `onClear`'s VM-cleanup preservation.
  - **REG-31 (US-12, T-644)**: the Search-page cook-time filter UI is the Apple-Timer-style two-wheel min/max range sheet (left wheel "Min", right wheel "Max", first entry on each wheel is "Any" = nil bound) and the chip / sheet copy contains **no `≤` or `≥` symbols** — chip-label states are `"Any time"` / `"<min> or more"` / `"<max> or less"` / `"<min>–<max>"` (en-dash) / `"<value>"` (when min == max). Three contracts locked. **(a) Max-only narrows the result set.** Picking only the right wheel to a value (e.g. "30 min") with the left wheel left at "Any" must filter visible items to those with `totalSeconds ≤ 30 * 60`; the cook-time hydration path (T-637 / CL-106) still fires on cache miss so the filter narrows fresh REST hits, not only cached ones — the guard updates from `filters.cookTime != nil` to `filters.cookTimeMinSeconds != nil || filters.cookTimeMaxSeconds != nil` and the rest of the fan-out (20-item cap, fire-and-forget Task, reapplyFilters on landing) is byte-identical to T-637's locked behavior. **(b) Two wheels render side by side with Min on the left.** The `CookTimeRangeSheet` body is an `HStack` of two `Picker.pickerStyle(.wheel)` views; the right wheel carries the `dod.search.cookTimeWheelMax` accessibility identifier so the L5 E2E can deterministically pick the max value. The sheet uses `.presentationDetents([.medium])` (half-sheet). **(c) No `≤` / `≥` in user-facing cook-time copy anywhere.** A repo-wide `rg '≤|<=' Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/ App/` must come up empty for cook-time-related strings; the four chip-label states + the wheel-row labels (`"15 min"`, `"1 hr"`, `"1 hr 30 min"`, `"4 hr"`) and the "Min" / "Max" section headers + the "Any" sentinel are the canonical strings. Failure modes: (a) a future contributor adds a `≤` back into a label string because they're translating an old bucket comment — the no-`≤` lint sweep catches it; (b) the max-only path stops calling `kickOffCookTimeHydrationIfNeeded(...)` because the guard was left at the pre-T-644 `.cookTime != nil` check — the existing T-637 / CL-106 hydration test (rewritten for the new model in T-644) catches it; (c) the wheels render stacked instead of side-by-side — the L4 snapshot baselines (re-recorded under T-644 for the new chip-label format) catch the chip side; the sheet's two-wheel layout is locked by the `CookTimeRangeSheet` view body + an L1 host snapshot test if added. Locked at L1 by `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchFiltersTests` (5 new cases — max-only narrows, min+max range, both-nil passthrough, inverted-range empty, missing-totalSeconds-still-MISS), `DODFeatureSearchTests.CookTimeFormatterTests` (5 new cases — `30*60` / `3600` / `90*60` / `2*3600` / `4*3600` boundary formatting), and `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchFiltersTests.cookTimeChipLabelComputesAllFourStates` (the chip-label helper — both nil → "Any time", only min → "30 min or more", only max → "45 min or less", both → "30 min–1 hr 30 min", min == max → "45 min"). Locked at L4 by the four re-recorded `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` baselines (`unselected_light` / `unselected_dark` / `cookTimeSelected_light` / `cookTimeSelected_dark` — the selected fixture switches from `SearchFilters(cookTime: .under30)` to `SearchFilters(cookTimeMaxSeconds: 30 * 60)`, so the chip body re-renders with `"30 min or less"`). Locked at L5 by `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests.test_search_cook_time_filter_narrows_results` (rewritten — tap chip via `dod.search.cookTimeChip`, wait for sheet, scroll `dod.search.cookTimeWheelMax` to `"30 min"`, tap "Apply", assert filtered count ≤ initial via the same `XCTNSPredicateExpectation` polling-wait pattern). See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-122 for the full rationale, the bucket-to-range migration reasoning, the inverted-range silent-empty trade-off, the considered alternatives (keep buckets + add "Custom range" entry, snap-on-release clamping, three-wheel hours/minutes layout, min-only + mode switcher, lift formatter to `DODSupport`), and the unchanged hydration contract.
  - **REG-30 (US-12 / US-29, T-643)**: typed queries that name a known WP category (case-insensitive, with the trailing `" recipes"` stripped — `"Dessert Recipes"` matches via topic `"dessert"`, `"Chicken"` matches `"Chicken and Poultry Recipes"` via substring-of-topic ≥ 4 chars, `"Camp Recipes"` matches `"Dutch Oven Camp Recipes"`) surface that category's posts via a parallel `?categories=<id>&per_page=100` fetch **in addition to** the T-642 title-match path; queries that don't name a category (e.g. `"Nachos"`, `"chili"`, `"casserole"`) remain title-match-only — the REG-29 Nacho-Bug contract is preserved unchanged. Failure mode this locks: T-642's strict title-only filter dropped category-navigation queries because recipes inside a category are titled by their own dish name (`"Apple Crumble"` / `"Cherry Cobbler"`), not by the category they belong to (`"Dessert Recipes"`) — typing `"Dessert Recipes"` returned 0 results when the user expects ~53. The fix adds `CategoryNameMatcher` (in `DODSupport`, pure + Foundation-only — same placement rationale as `TitleSearchMatcher`) and a new `SearchDependencies.fetchPosts(categoryID:limit:)` thin wrapper around the existing `WPRestClient.posts(categoryID:page:perPage:)` (no new REST endpoint surface — the `?categories=` query has been live since the Feed / Category Recipes screens shipped under T-081 / T-091). `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` fires Path A (title-search REST) and Path B (category REST) **in parallel via `async let`** and unions the result deduped-by-id, with Path A's tier-ranked title matches first (T-642 ordering intact) and Path B's category contributions in WP's natural date-desc order. Junk-query guards (single-token `"recipe"`/`"recipes"` rejected; substring-of-topic gated on ≥ 4 characters so `"the"` / `"a"` don't match) prevent fan-out on generic words. Top-2 cap on matched categories bounds the worst-case wire payload to two `?categories=` fetches per typed query. Path B failure does NOT block Path A — graceful degradation logs and renders Path A alone. Locked at L1 by `DODSupportTests.CategoryNameMatcherTests` (8 cases against the live-API top-8-by-post-count fixture — `"Dessert Recipes"` / `"dessert"` topic / `"Chicken"` substring-of-topic / `"Chicken and Poultry"` full-topic / `"Side Dish"` / `"Camp Recipes"` substring-either-direction / `"Nachos"` empty / `"recipe"` junk-rejected / `"the"` under-4-char-rejected / `"latest"` matches Latest Recipes / `"DESSERT recipes"` case-insensitive / multi-match top-2 cap / HTML-entity normalization), at L1 (pipeline integration) by `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchViewModelT643Tests` (4 cases — `"Dessert Recipes"` Path B contributes 53 posts + Path A empty → 53 in `viewModel.items`; `"Chicken"` Path A 5 title hits + Path B 32 category hits → union deduped with title-matches first; `"Nachos"` unchanged from T-642 contract — Path B empty so 4 title matches stand; Path B failure → Path A results render alone), and at L2 (gated live-API) by the existing live-API search suite asserting `"Dessert Recipes"` typed query returns ≥ 30 results. The local-ingredient pass v1-deferral (CL-120) and the title-precision filter (T-642 / CL-120 / REG-29) are unchanged — REG-30 is additive, not replacing. The Latest-Recipes Try-pill special case (T-637 / CL-106) is untouched because it routes through `surfaceLatestRecipes(limit:)` and never enters `performSearch()`. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-121 for the full rationale, the live-API category counts, the rejected alternatives (separate "category" tab/section, `_embed=wp:term` post-filter, `wp/v2/categories?search=<q>` lookup, top-1 cap, substring-without-floor), and the explicit "reuse existing REST surface — no new endpoint" decision.
  - **REG-28 (US-16 / US-18, T-730)**: the DODDesignSystem L4 snapshot suite is deterministic run-to-run and stable across Xcode / iOS-SDK versions, so it catches real visual regressions instead of going red on an SDK bump. Three failure modes are locked. **(a) Cross-version / cross-record sub-pixel drift.** Every `.image(...)` assertion — including the `darkImage` helper in `SnapshotTests+AppearanceAudit` — carries `precision: 0.98, perceptualPrecision: 0.97` so anti-aliasing differences between the record SDK and the CI SDK (Xcode 26.5 ↔ 26.3) don't trip a false diff. **(b) Stale dark baselines.** The dark `__Snapshots__` PNGs are re-recorded under the iOS 26 SDK to reflect the SurfaceElevated dark color set in T-570 / T-610 (`#5A3520` → `#553724`) that the #42-era baselines predated — a color change the suite had been silently failing on. **(c) Non-deterministic TabView material.** The dark tab-bar snapshots are removed (AC-16.5 amendment) because iOS 26's translucent tab-bar material composites non-deterministically off-screen (~0.2% pixel match between two renders on the same machine), which no tolerance can absorb; the tab bar's order / icons / labels stay pinned by `AppTabTests` + `SmokeTests.test_tabBarOrderMatchesSpec`. Also fixes the `TabBarSnapshotTests` helper's `file:` default from `#file` (concise) to `#filePath` (absolute) so `__Snapshots__` resolves to the source tree under `xcodebuild`, not the ephemeral simulator data dir (the prior bug meant the tab-bar baselines were never actually compared against the committed PNGs on CI). Failure mode this locks: a green-looking-but-ungrounded snapshot suite that records-to-simulator-dir every run or goes 100%-red on a Xcode bump, masking genuine chrome regressions. Locked at L4 by the DODDesignSystem snapshot suite running clean (91 tests, 0 failures) on CI under Xcode 26. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) CL-115 for the full root-cause + the re-record vs raise-tolerance vs remove decision matrix.

## Clarifications

Phase 2 is closed. See [`clarifications.md`](clarifications.md) for the resolution of all 10 clarification items and the rationale behind each. Notable outcomes baked into the spec above:

- Hybrid fetch strategy: WP REST for lists, post-page JSON-LD for recipe details (CL-1, AC-4.11).
- Per-page = 20 (CL-2); categories only, no tags (CL-3); share URL = `post.link` (CL-4).
- Saves stay device-local in v1; iCloud sync = v2 (CL-5).
- iPad hero image up to 2048px from `media_details.sizes`; lists use `medium_large` (CL-6).
- No onboarding (CL-7); branding follows the blog, palette tokenized in `plan.md` (CL-8).
- Posts with unparseable Recipe JSON-LD are hidden from lists after first failure (CL-9, AC-1.7).
- Non-recipe-post handling deferred to a Phase 3 post-mix audit (CL-10).
